# VGC Stock Manager — Project Reference

> **Purpose of this file.** A complete, self-contained technical reference for the VGC Stock Manager system. Written so that a new chat (or a context-collapsed one) can pick up the work with no other background. Kept in GitHub (`vgc-ltd-wp/vgc-plugin-updates` → `docs/`), deliberately **not** part of any release zip.
>
> **Pinned to:** Stock Manager **1.206.0** · Stock Bridge **0.7.0**
>
> ⚠️ **This file is updated and pushed with every release** — it must never lag the shipped version. See §7 (Working conventions).

---

## 1. What this is

A **workshop inventory and production system** for a small manufacturer (candles, mugs, gift sets). It is **not** a shop — it runs on its own dedicated WordPress site (a backoffice subdomain) and pushes finished-goods stock to a separate WooCommerce shop.

### Two plugins

| Plugin | Slug | Installed on | Job |
|---|---|---|---|
| **VGC Stock Manager** | `vgc-stock-manager` | Backoffice WP site | The whole app: items, recipes, production, costing, reports, PWA |
| **VGC Stock Bridge** | `vgc-stock-bridge` | The WooCommerce shop | Tiny token-auth REST surface: list products, read/adjust/set stock. **No orders, no customers.** |

### Non-negotiable design decisions (do not "fix" these)

1. **Unified item model.** No hard line between material and product. A Base Candle is a sellable product *and* a component of a Gift Box. This is what makes nested recipes possible.
2. **The ledger is truth.** Stock is the **sum of append-only movements**, never a typed number. `items.stock_qty` is only a cache, rebuildable via `recompute_stock()`.
3. **Production is batch-based.** Recipes have a **yield** ("1 run makes 24"). The UI inputs **runs**, not units.
4. **Consume-from-stock.** One run = **one assembly level**. To make a Gift Box you must already have Base Candles in stock. You produce each level as its own run.
5. **Push deltas, never absolutes.** Producing a sellable item pushes `+N` to the shop, so **online sales made in the meantime are never clobbered**. `set` exists only as an explicit, confirmed reconcile.
6. **Shop price is never imported as cost.** A selling price is not a cost price; it would poison every roll-up and margin.
7. **All UI on the WP front-end**, at the **site root**. Never wp-admin. Other front-end URLs redirect to `/`.
8. **No framework, no build step.** Vanilla JS, `innerHTML` rendering, hash routing, one plain CSS file. Must work offline as an installed PWA — **no CDNs, no external fonts/icons**.

## 2. Repository / file layout

Local working dir: `C:\Projecs\Claude Code\`

```
vgc-stock-manager/                  ← the app plugin (zipped for release)
  vgc-stock-manager.php             main file: constants, table helpers, bootstrap
  uninstall.php                     drops tables + options
  readme.txt                        changelog
  includes/
    class-vgc-plugin-updater.php    shared VGC self-update (reused verbatim)
    class-install.php               dbDelta schema
    class-units.php                 VGC_SM_Units — unit registry + conversion
    class-repository.php            VGC_SM_Repository — all DB access + ledger
    class-bom.php                   VGC_SM_BOM — recipe maths
    class-production.php            VGC_SM_Production — production runs
    class-sync-client.php           VGC_SM_Sync — talks to the Bridge; settings
    class-i18n.php                  VGC_SM_I18n — catalogue + editable overrides

    class-access.php                VGC_SM_Access — capability + role
    class-rest-api.php              VGC_SM_REST_API — every endpoint

    app.css                         the entire design system
    app.js                          the entire SPA
    icon-192.png, icon-512.png      PWA icons (generated, brand terracotta)

vgc-stock-bridge/                   ← the shop companion (zipped for release)
  vgc-stock-bridge.php
  includes/class-rest-controller.php   ← the whole surface
  includes/class-settings.php          ← token + API URL display
  includes/class-vgc-plugin-updater.php

_releases/                          ← built zips live here
vgc-stock-manager-design-brief.md   ← design brief (not shipped)
vgc-stock-manager-reference.md      ← THIS FILE (not shipped)
```

## 3. Data model

### Companies (1.153.0): one table set and one option set per company

The app can keep the books of more than one company. **Company 1 is the install as it always was** — every table below keeps its `wp_vgc_sm_` name and every option its name; nothing migrates. **Company N>1** gets `wp_vgc_sm_c{N}_<suffix>` tables and `vgc_sm_c{N}_<key>` options. The fact of which table a row is in IS which company it belongs to; no row carries a company id. **Shared, never prefixed:** `partners`, `partner_locations`, `partner_contacts` (and, from 1.154.0, `partner_companies`, the link table saying which company sees which partner; and, from 1.160.0, `item_shares`, the link table saying which companies' product rows are one product). `partner_prices` is per company. The registry of companies is one shared option, `vgc_sm_companies` (absent on a single-company install — company 1 is implicit).

`VGC_SM_Company` (`includes/class-company.php`, loaded before the helpers) resolves the current company once per request and every one of the 42 `vgc_sm_*_table()` helpers and every per-company option key (`vgc_sm_option_key()`) reads it. `run_as( $id, fn )` switches for the duration of a callback and always comes back; `each( fn )` runs once per active company (the two crons). Entering another company drops every request memo (`Costing::flush()`, `Repository::forget_maps()`, `Expenses::forget()`, `REST_API::forget_caches()`). The `plugins_loaded` migration loop runs every flag-guarded upgrade per company (`vgc_sm_flag()` / `vgc_sm_flag_set()`), so a company created later is upgraded by the same code that upgraded company 1. Access is a level per company: `vgc_sm_level` is company 1's (cap and no meta = operator, the founding rule), `vgc_sm_level_c{N}` another's (nothing by default). The uninstaller drops the other sets by name pattern. **1.154.0 — Companies.** The registry is `vgc_sm_companies`. `VGC_SM_Company::create()` writes the row, makes the tables under `run_as()` (`create_tables()`), stamps the per-company DB version and seeds settings = company 1's minus everything about the shop and the letterhead (`VGC_SM_Sync::company_seed()`); the company's one-time migrations run on the NEXT request through the loop, because none of its flags are set. `update()` renames or archives (company 1 never; tables stay). **Context:** a page's company is `resolve_page()` — URL `vgc_sm_co` (honoured when the user may use it, and remembered in user meta `vgc_sm_company`) → the remembered one → the first accessible; **a till page with no company is company 1's** (installed tills predate companies and their location id may exist in both); no company at all → a message, not the app. A REST call's is `rest_context()` on `rest_pre_dispatch` from the `X-VGC-Company` header, before any permission callback: unknown/archived/not allowed → 403 `vgc_sm_company_forbidden`; absent → the remembered one (older clients keep working); never remembered from a call, so two tabs never fight. Boot carries `company` and `companies` (the accessible ones); the client sends the header on every `rawFetch`/`upload`, stores `company` on every queued write and replays each into ITS company, and switches by purging every cache then reloading `?vgc_sm_co=N` (every screen file captured boot at load). **Partners:** one list; `partner_companies (partner_id, company_id)` says who sees whom; `Partners::all()` JOINs on the current company, `create()` links the maker, `set_companies()` (admin, the current company always kept), `visible()`, `companies_of()`/`companies_map()`; the REST layer guards get/update/archive/prices/statement/services and the `partner_id` of order/purchase/note saves (the model's `get()` stays unfiltered so old documents render). `partners.company_id` marks the row that IS a company: `sync_company_rows()` (on create/rename/archive and on a letterhead save) keeps name/VAT/address/logo in step, links it to every other company and never to its own, and only once there are two. One shop per company: `save_settings()` refuses a `bridge_url` another company uses (`vgc_sm_shop_taken`). **Tills:** company N>1's manifest is `vgc-till-c{N}-{loc}.webmanifest` (id `vgc-till-c{N}-{loc}`, icons `vgc-till-c{N}-{loc}-192.png`, start URL with `vgc_sm_co`), served with that company entered; the SW URL carries `&c=N` and its cache is `vgc-sm-<ver>-c{N}`; company 1's names are all unchanged. Team is per company (`/team?company=`, body `company`).

**1.155.0 — Trade between the companies** (`includes/class-intercompany.php`, `VGC_SM_Intercompany`). The owner's rule: stock moves between two companies ALWAYS as a sale. An order in the seller whose partner is a company row (`partners.company_id`) is mirrored into that company as an OWNED purchase: `Orders::save()`/`set_complete()`/`add_payment()`/`delete_payment()` fire `vgc_sm_order_saved`, `Orders::delete()` fires `vgc_sm_order_deleting`; `on_order_saved()` reads everything from the seller FIRST (`payload()`: lines with the item's sku/name/unit/vat/for_sale/is_service/image/category name, `seller_vat = VGC_SM_Sync::vat_registered()`, the seller's company row id, the number `IC<seller>-<order number>`) and only then `run_as( buyer )` → `write_mirror()`: the buyer's item by SKU or `create_item()` (kind raw, `supplier_partner_id` = the seller's row, category by name in the product/material scope), then `Purchases::save( …, $id, array( 'mirror' => … ) )` with `qty_received ← qty_sent`, `qty_returned` mirrored, `unit_cost ← unit_price`, `vendor_vat_registered ← seller_vat`, `prices_include_vat`/terms/dates/status mirrored, `date_received ← date_sent`, transport `customer`→`us` / `us`→`vendor`, `already_received ← already_sent`, display lines skipped; existing mirror lines are matched by item so `applied_in` survives; payments replaced wholesale. Then `backlink()` writes `orders.mirror_company_id/mirror_purchase_id`. Columns: `orders.mirror_company_id`, `orders.mirror_purchase_id`, `purchases.mirror_company_id`, `purchases.mirror_order_id` + `UNIQUE KEY mirror (mirror_company_id, mirror_order_id)` (DB 0.55.0; `ensure_intercompany_columns()` under per-company flag `intercompany_v1`). **Guards:** `Purchases::owned_error()` refuses `save/delete/set_complete/add_payment/delete_payment` on a row with `mirror_order_id` unless `$opts['mirror']` (409 `vgc_sm_purchase_owned`); `Orders::save()` refuses `on_sale` to a company row (`vgc_sm_company_on_sale`) and a company's own row (`vgc_sm_own_company`); `Notes::save_draft()` refuses a company row (`vgc_sm_company_partner`). A partner change drops the old mirror (and its stock) and makes a new one; deleting the order deletes the mirror. A mirror that fails is audited (`intercompany.mirror_failed`), the order stays saved with no back-link, `shape_order()` says `mirror_missing`, and the next save retries. Shapers: orders carry `partner_is_company`, `mirror_company_id/name`, `mirror_purchase_id`, `mirror_url` (`Intercompany::link_to()` — only for a user who may work there; carries `vgc_sm_co` whenever the target is not the current company), `mirror_missing`; purchases carry `owned`, `mirror_company_id/name`, `mirror_order_id`, `mirror_url`. Screens: the order form fixes the mode to Pay in full and shows the mirror banner (`order-mirror`); the purchase page shows `purchase-mirror` and drops Save/Complete/Delete; the purchases list tags a mirror.

**1.162.0 — the same trade, started by the buyer** (same class). A purchase whose vendor is a company row is an ORDER REQUEST to that company. `Purchases::save()` fires `vgc_sm_purchase_saved` and `delete()` fires `vgc_sm_purchase_deleting`, both only when `$opts['mirror']` is absent. `on_purchase_saved()` skips a mirror, a non-company vendor and a legacy row (`Purchases::request_is_legacy()`: no `request_state`, no `mirror_order_id`, something already received), reads the buyer's side first (`request_payload()`: stock lines' SKU / qty_ordered / unit_cost / line_note, header dates, terms, `prices_include_vat`, transport with the payer flipped `us`→`customer`, the purchase number) and then `run_as( seller )` → `write_request()`: items by SKU (any missing → `vgc_sm_request_skus` naming them), `Orders::save( …, $existing, array( 'request' => true ) )` with `status = 'requested'`, `unit_price ← unit_cost`, nothing sent, then `backlink()` so the request points at the purchase from the start. Back in the buyer, `stamp_request()` writes `purchases.request_company_id / request_order_id / request_state / request_error` (DB 0.60.0, `ensure_request_columns()` under per-company flag `intercompany_request_v1`). A failure is audited (`intercompany.request_failed`), `request_state = 'failed'` with the reason on the document, and the next save retries. **While requested** the order counts for nothing: `Orders::requested_error()` refuses `set_complete`, both payment writers and all three sale-report writers (6 sites), `request_qty_error()` refuses any posted `qty_sent/qty_sold/qty_returned`, `count_for_partner()`/`turnover_for_partner()`/the statement skip it, `can_transfer` is false, and `on_order_saved()` returns early so the buyer leads — the seller may re-price, but the buyer's next save rewrites it. `'requested'` is only ever written with `$opts['request']`. **Accepting** (`accept_request()`, `POST /orders/{id}/accept`, operator): status → `open`, then the ordinary `on_order_saved()` — and because the order already points at that purchase, `write_mirror()` updates it in place, stamping `mirror_order_id`, so the buyer's own document becomes the read-only mirror under its own number; `request_state = 'accepted'`; audited in both. **Declining** (`decline_request()`, `POST /orders/{id}/decline`) deletes the order; `on_order_deleting()` sees `requested` and marks the buyer's purchase `declined` instead of dropping it (the buyer's own delete sets `self::$dropping` so the request goes quietly with it). Refusals on the buyer's side: `vgc_sm_own_company`, `vgc_sm_company_consignment`, `vgc_sm_request_received`. Shapers: orders carry `requested`, `request_company_name`, `request_purchase_number` (`Intercompany::peek_purchase_number()`), `request_url`; purchases carry `request_state/error/company_id/company_name/order_number/url` and `partner_is_company`. Screens: `order-request` (Accept, Decline, no Complete, no payments, the three quantity boxes disabled) and `purchase-request` in its three states (Recv. disabled, consignment not offered), plus Requested/Declined tags on both lists and `statusBadge('requested')`.

**1.163.0 — the VAT boundary between the companies** (same class). A company that is not registered reclaims nothing, so what it pays is final. `Orders::gross_unit( $line, $incl )` is what the customer actually pays for one unit (the net plus `VGC_SM_Sync::vat_rate()`, which is 0 for an unregistered seller); `payload()` carries it per line as `unit_gross`, read in the SELLER. `write_mirror()`, inside the buyer, asks `$reclaims = VGC_SM_Sync::vat_registered()`: when it cannot reclaim, the line's `unit_cost` is the seller's gross and the document is written `prices_include_vat = 1`, so nothing takes the VAT out again — the same rule `Order_Transfer::lines_for()` keeps when an order crosses. `request_payload()` carries `buyer_reclaims` the other way, and `write_request()` prices the request gross-typed when the buyer cannot reclaim, so the seller invoices exactly the final figure that was asked for. A one-time per-company repair, `remirror_all()` under the flag `intercompany_gross_v1`, re-writes every existing mirror (update in place, lines matched by item, nothing received twice) so trades booked before this release stop being short by the VAT. **On the client:** `core.js vatRate( raw )` mirrors `VGC_SM_Sync::vat_rate()` (`V.vatRate`, with `V.vatReg`), and `purchases.js` (`netCost`, `stripVat`, `totals`) and `orders.js` (`stripVat`, `totals`, the printed sheet) now read every line rate through it — before this they stripped VAT whatever our status, so a gross-typed document in an unregistered company showed a cost ~16.7% under what the server had stored, and a margin that much too good.

**1.158.0 — Moving an order to another company** (`includes/class-order-transfer.php`, `VGC_SM_Order_Transfer`). An untouched order (nothing sent, nothing paid, open) leaves one company's books for another's in one action. Destination first, inside `run_as()`: the customer linked if hidden, lines matched by SKU, the real `Orders::save()` (own number, own cost snapshot, the intercompany hook mirroring afresh when the partner is a third company); then the source row becomes a frozen stub (`status='transferred'`, `transferred_*` → the moved order, which carries `origin_*` back). One transaction around both. The customer's total is unchanged across the VAT boundary: the price travels gross-typed and the destination reads VAT out of it by its own rules. Moving home revives the stub under its old number; deleting the moved order deletes the stub. Everything about the source is read before `run_as()`, as in Intercompany.

**1.160.0 — A product in more than one company** (`includes/class-item-share.php`, `VGC_SM_Item_Share`). Products cannot be one shared row (every ledger keys on a company's own item ids), so a product ticked *Also in* another company is a twin there under the same SKU, remembered in the shared `item_shares` table and kept in step both ways: name, unit, VAT rate, category (by name and scope), image, barcode, pack, shelf life, notes, label colour, product/material, service and the SKU travel; stock, costs, prices, recipes, kind, supplier, reorder level, the shop, favourite and archive stay each company's own. A product the source makes is bought in from the source over there. The repository's own `update_item()`/`delete_item()` drive the propagation, diffed first and guarded against re-entrancy; the source is read before `run_as()`, as in Intercompany.

### Tables (prefix `wp_vgc_sm_`; `wp_vgc_sm_c{N}_` for company N>1 — see Companies)

| Table | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `items` | The unified item model. |
| `categories` | User-defined categories. |
| `boms` | One recipe per manufactured item: `output_item_id`, `yield_qty`. |
| `bom_items` | Recipe components: `component_item_id`, `quantity`, `unit`, **`basis`** (`item`\|`run`). |
| `bom_extras` | Non-material costs: `label`, `amount`, `basis`, `kind` (`labour`\|`other`), `hours`, and — when the line names a standing cost — `cost_type_id` + `quantity` (1.134.0). |
| `cost_types` | The standing non-material costs (1.134.0): `name`, `unit` (what one of it IS), `rate`, `note`, `active`, `sort_order`. |
| `expenses` | What the business spends outside a product and outside an event (1.135.0): `category_id`, `label`, `partner_id`, `amount_net`/`vat_rate`/`amount_gross`, `spent_on`, `note`, plus `due_day`/`every_months`/`template_id` for recurring bills. **`due_day IS NOT NULL` means the row is a TEMPLATE, not spending.** `purchase_id` + `purchase_line_id` (UNIQUE, 1.143.0) mark a row a delivery owns: written by `save_owned()`, read-only on the Business costs screen. |
| `movements` | **Append-only ledger.** `type` (receive/consume/produce/ship/scrap/correct/consign_out/consign_return), signed `qty`, `ref_type`/`ref_id`, `partner_id`. |
| `production_runs` | One row per run; movements reference it. |
| `sync_log` | Every Bridge call. |
| `partners` | Shops we supply / makers we take from. `type` (`customer`\|`supplier`\|`both`), `active` (archived, never deleted), `vat_registered`/`vat_number` (1.9.0), `payment_terms_days` (1.10.0, default 30; drives note due dates). Flat `contact_name`/`email`/`phone`/`address` are a **cached mirror of the primary** child rows (kept in step by `sync_primary_fields()`). |
| `partner_locations` | Branches/warehouses, **each with its own contact**: `label`, `address`, `contact_name`/`contact_role`/`contact_email`/`contact_phone` (1.6.0), `is_primary`, `sort_order`. |
| `partner_contacts` | **Additional** people not tied to a location: `name`, `role`, `email`, `phone`, `is_primary`, `sort_order`. |
| `partner_prices` | Agreed unit price per (partner, item). Drives note pricing. |
| `stock_notes` | The documents. `number` (`SN-YYYY-NNNN`), `partner_id`, `location_id` (destination), `direction`, `type`, `status`, `note_date` (user-set; drives `issued_at`), `due_date` (note_date + partner terms; money notes only), `skip_stock` ("already sent" — no stock effect), `is_refund` (a return that credits money and skips the pile), `total_net`/`total_vat`/`total_gross`. |
| `stock_note_lines` | `item_id`, `qty`, `unit`, `unit_price` (**net**), `vat_rate`, `line_net`/`line_vat`/`line_gross`, `line_note`, `is_display` (a display piece on loan). |
| `consignment_ledger` | **Second append-only ledger**: how much of an item is at a partner. Releases add, sale reports and returns subtract; cancels write reversing rows. `is_display` splits the pile into **sellable vs display pools** — a sale report can only draw on the sellable pool. |
| `orders` | **The order model (1.11.0) — deliberately MUTABLE.** One living doc per customer order: `number` (`ORD-YYYY-NNNN`), `partner_id`, `location_id`, `date_ordered`/`date_sent`/`date_received`, `payment_mode` (`full`\|`on_sale`), `payment_terms_days`, `due_date`, `prices_include_vat` (1.12.0 — whether a typed `unit_price` already has VAT in it), `transport_cost`/`transport_payer` (`us`\|`customer`), `already_sent`, `paid_amount`, `status` (`open`\|`completed` — never final). |
| `order_lines` | `item_id`, `qty_ordered`/`qty_sent`/`qty_sold`/`qty_returned`, `unit_price`, `unit_cost` (**snapshot**, so margin never drifts), `vat_rate`, `is_display`, `line_note`, plus **`applied_out`/`applied_pile`** — what the ledgers already reflect for this line. |
| `order_payments` | Partial payments against an order (1.13.0): `paid_on`, `amount`, `note`. **Paid is SUM(payments)** — never a typed-over field. One-time `migrate_paid_amounts()` turns a legacy `orders.paid_amount` into the first payment (`vgc_sm_order_payments_migrated`). |
| `audit` | Activity log (1.7.0): `user_id`, `user_name`, `action` (dotted slug), `object_type`/`object_id`, `summary`, `ip`, `created_at`. Append-only, best-effort (never blocks the action it records). |

### `items` columns worth knowing

`sku` (unique) · `name` · `kind` (`raw`\|`manufactured`) · `unit` (base/stock unit) · `stock_qty` (**cache**) · `reorder_level` · `is_sellable` · `woo_sku` · `woo_product_id` · `supplier` · `barcode` · `category_id` · `image_id` · `pack_label` · `pack_size` · `cost_net` · `vat_rate` (default 20) · `active` (0 = **archived**) · `shop_held` (held units currently listed on the shop) · `shop_baseline` (shop qty as last accounted for, drives reconcile)

**DB_VERSION is currently `0.16.0`.** Bump it in `vgc-stock-manager.php` whenever the schema changes — `create_tables()` runs `dbDelta` on `plugins_loaded` when it differs, which auto-migrates.

### Options

| Option | Contents |
|---|---|
| `vgc_sm_db_version` | Schema version guard (per company: `vgc_sm_c{N}_db_version`). |
| `vgc_sm_companies` | The registry of companies (1.153.0), shared: `[{id, name, active, created_at}]`. Absent on a single-company install. |
| `vgc_sm_partners_split` | Set once the 1.3.0 flat→child partner migration has run. |
| `vgc_sm_notes_vat_backfill` | Set once the 1.9.0 backfill (pre-VAT notes → `total_gross = total_net`, 0% VAT) has run. |
| `vgc_sm_settings` | `bridge_url`, `bridge_token`, `auto_push`, `currency` (default `€`), `language` (default `en`). Per company (`vgc_sm_c{N}_settings`), as are `vgc_sm_units`, `vgc_sm_count_draft_*` and every `vgc_sm_*_v1` migration flag. Shared: `vgc_sm_companies`, `vgc_sm_i18n_overrides`, `vgc_sm_partners_split`, `vgc_sm_partner_logo_v1`. |
| `vgc_sm_units` | Custom (non-builtin) unit codes. |
| `vgc_sm_i18n_overrides` | `{ lang: { source_string: translation } }` — user-edited wording. |

---

## 4. The engine (the part that must stay correct)

### Units — `VGC_SM_Units`
Built-in registry with a **dimension** and a **factor**: weight (mg/g/dag/kg → base g), volume (ml/cl/dl/l → base ml), count (pcs). Custom units are count/1. `convert($qty,$from,$to)` scales within a dimension and returns unchanged across dimensions (degrade safely).

### Recipe maths — `VGC_SM_BOM`
The pivotal helper:

```php
per_item_qty($c, $yield)      // basis 'item' → quantity; basis 'run' → quantity / yield
demand_per_item($c, $yield)   // per_item_qty converted into the COMPONENT'S base/stock unit
```

**Everything routes through `demand_per_item()`** — `direct_requirements()`, `max_buildable()`, `plan_produce()`, `explode_raw()`, `unit_cost_net()`. This is why a recipe in grams reconciles with stock in kg, and why per-item and per-batch both work.

- `direct_requirements($item,$qty)` — one assembly level. Returns need (in the component's stock unit), available, short, unit_cost, line_cost, plus `recipe_unit`/`need_recipe` for the "180 g → 0.18 kg" display.
- `plan_build($item,$qty)` — recursive **net requirements**: uses on-hand stock of intermediates first, only builds/buys the shortfall. A mutable `available` map is decremented as it allocates, so a component shared across branches isn't double-counted. Returns `feasible`, ordered `builds` (children first), `raw_short`.
- `unit_cost_net($item)` — recursive roll-up: Σ(component unit cost × demand_per_item) **+ extras** (per-item, or per-batch ÷ yield). Cycle- and depth-guarded.
- `would_create_cycle()` — used by the recipe editor to reject A→B→A.

### Production — `VGC_SM_Production`
`run()` is **transactional**: `production_runs` row + `consume` movements + `produce` movement + optional `scrap` movement, all inside `START TRANSACTION`/`COMMIT`. Blocks on shortages unless `allow_negative`. Fires:

```php
do_action( 'vgc_sm_production_completed', $item_id, $net_qty, $run_id );
```

`$net_qty` is **produced minus scrap** — the sync client listens here and pushes that, so defective units never reach the shop.

### Sync — `VGC_SM_Sync`
`request()` → Bridge with `X-VGC-Token`. `push_adjust()` (delta, the normal path), `push_set()` (reconcile only), `read_stock()`, `pull_products()`, `shop_levels()`, `push_item()`. Everything logged to `sync_log`.

### Partners and stock notes — `VGC_SM_Partners`, `VGC_SM_Notes`

`VGC_SM_Notes::types()` is the single table that drives everything. Each type declares its effect:

| type | direction | `stock` | `consignment` | movement | owes us | we owe |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `release` | out | −1 | +1 | `consign_out` | no | no |
| `sale_report` | out | 0 | −1 | — | **yes** | no |
| `return_in` | out | +1 | −1 | `consign_return` | no | no |
| `direct_sale` | out | −1 | 0 | `ship` | **yes** | no |
| `take_in` | in | 0 | +1 | — | no | no |
| `purchase_in` | in | +1 | 0 | `receive` | no | **yes** |
| `buy_held` | in | +1 | −1 | `receive` | no | **yes** |
| `return_out` | in | 0 | −1 | — | no | no |
| `sold_held` | in | 0 | −1 | — | no | **yes** |

Read it as: *what happens to my stock* and *what happens to the pile*. For `direction=out` the pile is **our goods at the partner** (`consignment_ledger` direction `out`); for `direction=in` it is the **held bucket** — the maker's goods at our place (direction `in`). A release moves goods out of stock into the out-pile without selling; a sale report never touches stock but clears the out-pile and the partner owes us. Inbound mirrors it: `take_in` fills the held bucket without touching our stock; `sold_held`/`buy_held` empty it and we owe the maker; `buy_held`/`purchase_in` are the only inbound types that add to our own stock.

**Held stock is never our own stock and is never pushed to the shop** (that is the on-demand Phase 4). Only `stock`-affecting movements push, so `purchase_in`/`buy_held` push a positive delta for sellable items; everything else inbound is stock-neutral.

- `save_draft()` — drafts only; an issued note is immutable. A line with no price falls back to the partner's price list.
- `issue()` — **pre-flight first**: never release stock you do not have, never report/return more than the partner is holding. Problems → `WP_REST_Response` 409 with `problems[]` and **nothing is written**. Otherwise one transaction: movements + consignment rows + number + status, then shop pushes.
- `cancel()` — writes **reversing entries** in both ledgers and pushes the reverse to the shop. Never deletes.
- `outstanding()` / `outstanding_all($partner,$direction)` / `at_partners()` / `held()` / `balance_owed()` / `balance_we_owe()` — all derived by SUM over the ledgers, so they cannot drift. `outstanding_all(..,'in')` is the held report; `held($item)` is the per-item held total.
- `statement($partner,$from,$to)` (1.5.0) — every money-bearing note both ways over a period (issued + settled), with outstanding `they_owe` / `we_owe` / `net`. Outbound money = `sale_report`,`direct_sale`; inbound money = `purchase_in`,`buy_held`,`sold_held`. Only `issued` notes count towards outstanding.
- `push_to_shop()` — sellable + `auto_push` only, and always a **delta**.

**Shop publishing of held stock (1.4.0).** Held goods stay off the shop until the operator publishes them.
- `publish_held($item,$qty)` — push `+qty` delta, `shop_held += qty`, set `shop_baseline` to the shop qty just read back. `unpublish_held()` is the reverse.
- `reconcile_scan()` — for every item with `shop_held > 0`, read the shop; `sold = clamp(baseline − current, 0, shop_held)` (only the held share; own-stock sales are out of scope by the push-only design); split `sold` across makers **FIFO by earliest `created_at`** via `held_by_maker()`.
- `reconcile_preview()` (advisory) / `reconcile_apply()` (books one issued `sold_held` note per maker with `skip_clamp`, then lowers `shop_held` and re-baselines). Both recompute live.
- **Invariant:** the shop never lists more of a maker's goods than we still hold. `clamp_shop_held($item)` runs after any held-reducing note (`issue()` for inbound `consignment<0`; `cancel()` of a `take_in`) and pushes the surplus off. Reconcile passes `skip_clamp` because the online sale is what reduced the shop.

### Orders — `VGC_SM_Orders` (1.11.0)

The user asked for a spreadsheet, not a stack of immutable documents. So an order is **fully editable, forever** — and the ledgers stay append-only anyway, via reconciliation rather than rewriting.

- **The invariant:** every line stores `applied_out` / `applied_pile` = what the movement ledger and the consignment ledger already reflect for it. On each `save()` → `reconcile()`, the engine computes the *desired* state and writes **only the delta**:
  - `want_out  = already_sent ? 0 : (qty_sent - qty_returned)` → movement of `-(want_out - applied_out)`
  - `want_pile = qty_sent - qty_sold - qty_returned` → consignment row of `want_pile - applied_pile`
  This is why editing a sent qty from 3 → 5 a week later Just Works, and why history is never rewritten.
- **Removing a line** zeroes its quantities first so reconcile releases the stock, *then* deletes the row (same for `delete()` of a whole order).
- `net_unit($line, $incl)` (1.12.0) is the one place VAT-inclusive entry lives. `unit_price` is stored **exactly as typed**; `prices_include_vat` says how to read it (`incl ? price/(1+vat/100) : price`). Storing the typed figure rather than a converted one is deliberate — no rounding drift.
- **Display lines carry no money at all**: `billable_qty()` returns 0 for them, and the editor renders them in a separate section on a shorter grid (`.vgc-sm-oline--demo`, 8 columns) with no price/cost/VAT/margin fields.
- `billable_qty()` is the one place the payment mode lives: `full` → `sent - returned`; `on_sale` → `sold`. Display lines are **never** billable.
- `totals()` derives everything (net/VAT/gross/cost/**margin**/paid/balance/out_there). Transport lands on whoever `transport_payer` says: on the customer it adds to gross; on us it comes out of margin.
- **A stub left by a move (1.158.0)** has `status = 'transferred'` — a value `save()` never accepts (the whitelist stays `open|completed`); only `VGC_SM_Order_Transfer` writes it, by direct update. Every writer consults `moved_error()`; every aggregate skips it.
- `set_complete()` only flips `status`. **No status is final** — this is a product requirement, not an oversight.
- Orders write to the **same** ledgers as stock notes, so "Out on consignment", item stock and the shop push all keep working across both models.

---

> **P5 (short sales at the till) — ANSWERED in 1.118.0.** The v2 audit asked whether a real sale should ever be blocked for want of stock. The owner's answer reframed it: at the workshop counter, selling something the records have not got means it was made and not yet logged, so the sale goes through and the app writes down what it owes the books (see the primary counter, above). **WIDENED in 1.120.0, at the owner’s request:** every location can now sell ahead of its books, but only when the operator ticks *Sell as backlog* on the line. The default everywhere except the primary counter is still to refuse — at a stall a shortfall is usually a miscount, and an app that shrugs at that stops being read. The tick is the difference between "it happened" and "we did not notice".

## 5. REST API (`vgc-stock/v1`)

Auth: same-origin cookie + `X-WP-Nonce`. **Access levels (1.7.0)** — `VGC_SM_Access::level()` resolves a user to `viewer`|`operator`|`manager`|`admin`: admin = `manage_options`; otherwise the `vgc_sm_level` user-meta (default `operator`) for anyone with the `vgc_sm_access` cap; `null` = no access. Permission callbacks: `$auth` = viewer+ (reads), `$write` = `require_operator` (day-to-day writes), `$mgr` = `require_manager` (partners/prices/deletes/shop ops), `$admin` = `authorize_admin` (settings/translations/team/audit). Enforcement is server-side; the SPA hides what a level can't do via boot `perms` (`write`/`manage`/`admin`). Existing Stock Operators resolve to `operator`; nothing to migrate.

| Method | Route | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| GET/POST | `/items` | list (filters: `search`, `kind`, `category_id`, `low_only`, `archived`) / create |
| GET/PUT/**DELETE** | `/items/{id}` | DELETE takes `?mode=archive\|delete`; refuses if used in a recipe (409 + `used_in`) |
| GET | `/items/{id}/movements` | ledger history |
| GET | `/items/{id}/shop-stock` | live shop qty for a sellable item |
| GET | `/items/lookup?code=` | barcode/SKU |
| GET | `/items/suggest-sku?name=` | unique SKU from a name |
| POST | `/items/import` | CSV rows upsert |
| POST | `/movements` | `receive` \| `ship` \| `scrap` \| `adjust` \| `set` |
| GET/PUT | `/recipe/{id}` | components + extras; PUT rejects cycles |
| GET/POST | `/recipe-templates` | list / save (POST `from_item_id` captures a product’s recipe instead) |
| GET/**DELETE** | `/recipe-templates/{id}` | one template with its lines / delete (never has dependants) |
| POST | `/recipe/{id}/apply-template` | stamp a template on; `dry_run` returns the same plan the preview draws |
| POST/**DELETE** | `/locations/{id}/backlog/plan` | put something on a location’s make-and-send list / take a PLANNED entry back |
| GET | `/locations/{id}/event` | an event, what the workshop owes for it, and what blocks closing |
| POST | `/locations/{id}/event/debt` | make good what went before it was made *(operator)* |
| POST | `/locations/{id}/event/close` | freeze an event’s figures; `on:false` reopens *(manager)* |
| GET | `/locations/{id}/event/settlement` | the closing-count sheet: took / rung up / should be left *(manager)* |
| POST | `/locations/{id}/event/settle` | act on the count — sell the leftover, bring the rest home, write off the gone *(manager)* |
| GET/POST | `/locations/{id}/event/costs` | what the weekend cost beyond the goods *(manager)* |
| **DELETE** | `/locations/{id}/event/costs/{cost_id}` | remove one *(manager)* |
| GET | `/locations/{id}/event/pnl` | did it pay *(manager)* |
| GET | `/production/preview` | requirements, shortages, materials/extras/total cost |
| GET | `/production/plan` | recursive build plan |
| POST | `/production/run` | `qty`, `allow_negative`, `scrap_qty`, `scrap_note` |
| GET | `/dashboard` | low materials / low products |
| GET | `/reports/stock`, `/reports/production` | + scrap; CSV built client-side |
| GET/POST | `/categories`, PUT/DELETE `/categories/{id}` | |
| GET/POST | `/units` | codes + `meta{dimension,factor}` |
| POST | `/media` | image upload (gated by `vgc_sm_access`, **not** `upload_files`) |
| GET/POST | `/settings`, POST `/settings/test` | *(admin)* |
| GET/POST | `/translations`, POST `/translations/reset` | *(admin)* |
| GET | `/shop/levels`, POST `/shop/push` | |
| GET | `/shop/products`, POST `/shop/pull` | *(admin)* pull products, incl. image sideload |
| GET/POST | `/partners`, GET/PUT/**DELETE** `/partners/{id}` | DELETE archives. GET {id} returns outstanding + notes + price list + `owed`. |
| POST | `/partners/{id}/prices` | upsert one agreed price (empty `unit_price` clears it) |
| GET | `/partners/{id}/statement?from=&to=` | money owed both ways over a period (lines + totals) |
| GET/POST | `/notes`, GET/PUT/DELETE `/notes/{id}` | PUT/DELETE are **drafts only** |
| POST | `/notes/{id}/issue` | 409 + `problems[]` if it would break stock |
| POST | `/notes/{id}/cancel`, `/notes/{id}/paid` | cancel = reversing entries; paid = `settled` |
| GET | `/consignment/outstanding` | `direction=out` (default, "what have we shipped") or `direction=in` (held stock); + totals at price and at cost |
| POST | `/consignment/publish`, `/consignment/unpublish` | list/de-list an item's held units on the shop (`item_id`, `qty`; qty≤0 = all) |
| GET/POST | `/consignment/reconcile` | GET = preview; POST = book detected shop sales as `sold_held` notes |
| GET/POST | `/orders`, GET/PUT/DELETE `/orders/{id}` | the order model; PUT is always allowed (never locks) |
| POST | `/orders/{id}/complete` | `{complete:0\|1}` — reversible |
| GET/POST | `/locations`, PUT `/locations/{id}` | sale locations (POS). Create/edit = *(manager)*. |
| GET | `/locations/{id}/inventory` | a location's on-hand rows (qty, retail, value) |
| POST | `/locations/{id}/push`, `/locations/{id}/pull` | *(manager)* hard-transfer stock workshop↔location (`item_id`, `qty`) |
| GET | `/items/{id}/locations` | where an item is stocked across locations |
| GET/POST | `/sales` | till sales — list (filters: `location_id`,`from`,`to`; returns `rows`+`totals`) / checkout (`location_id`,`lines[]`,`discount_type`,`discount_value`) |
| GET | `/sales/{id}` | one receipt (lines + net/VAT/total + refund state) |
| POST | `/sales/{id}/refund` | *(manager)* refund lines (`lines:[{line_id,qty}]`) or `void:true` — returns stock to the location |
| GET | `/help` | the in-app wiki |
| GET/POST | `/team`, `/team/{id}` | *(admin)* list users + levels / set a user's level (`viewer`\|`operator`\|`manager`\|`none`); per company since 1.154.0 (`?company=` / body `company`, default the current one; the answer carries `company` + `companies`) |
| GET/POST/PUT | `/companies`, `/companies/{id}` | (1.154.0) GET *(viewer+)* the companies the user may work in (an admin: all, archived included) + `current`; POST *(admin)* `{name, vat_registered}`; PUT *(admin)* `{name, active}`. Every route in the namespace reads `X-VGC-Company` first — see §3 Companies. |
| GET | `/audit` | *(admin)* activity log, filtered (`search`,`user_id`,`action`,`from`,`to`,`page`) + paginated |

### Bridge (`vgc-stock-bridge/v1`) — on the shop
Auth: `X-VGC-Token` header (shared secret) over HTTPS.

`GET /ping` · `GET /products?page&per_page&search&category&type` (variations expanded; returns `image`+`image_full`, `virtual`/`downloadable`; `category`=term slug, `type`=simple/variable/grouped/external — 0.4.0) · `GET /product-categories` (0.4.0) · `GET /stock?skus=` · `POST /stock/adjust` (**signed deltas — the normal path**) · `POST /stock/set` (absolute).

## 6. Front end

`class-frontend.php` serves the app at the **site root** on `template_redirect` with a standalone full-screen template (no theme). Not logged in / lacking `vgc_sm_access` → a branded login (not `wp-login.php`).

- **PWA**: manifest + service worker served *virtually* at `/vgc-app.webmanifest` and `/vgc-sw.js` (matched by path on `init`, so root scope works). SW is registered with `updateViaCache:'none'` + a version-stamped URL, calls `reg.update()` each load and **reloads once on `controllerchange`** — this is why plugin updates land without a hard refresh. The REST API is **never** cached.
- **Offline**: writes go through an **IndexedDB queue** (`write()`), replayed on reconnect. Reads fail offline by design.
- `app.js` boot payload (`window.VGC_SM`): `restUrl`, `nonce`, `appUrl`, `swUrl`, `logoutUrl`, `canManage`, `currency`, `lang`, `strings`, `user`, `version`.

**POS till — separate page.** `maybe_render()` branches on `?vgc_sm_pos=1` (needs `operator`) → `render_pos()` serves its own full-screen shell that loads `core.js` (helpers only, no `VGCSM.start()`) + `js/pos/pos.tpl.js` + `js/pos/pos.js`, then `window.VGCSM_POS.start()`. Opened from the **Open till** button on Sale locations (`posUrl` in the boot). A **Scan** button opens a self-contained camera overlay (BarcodeDetector, mirrors `production.js`'s scanner but continuous with a 1.5s same-code de-dup); each scan calls `GET /items/lookup?code=` then adds from the location's stock via `addToCart` (which caps at on-hand). Camera-absent/insecure-context falls back to manual barcode/SKU entry. Data model: `sale_locations` + `location_ledger` (truth) + `location_stock` (cache, PK location+item); `sales` + `sale_lines`. Push/pull = **hard transfer** (`record_movement(item,'to_location',∓qty)` + location ledger); a sale is a **location-only decrement** (no stock movement) that records a receipt. Company inventory = `stock_qty` + Σ `location_stock`. Discount math is gross-facing: per-line (`pct`|`val`) → line_gross; cart discount allocated across lines by gross share (last line takes the rounding remainder); net = gross/(1+vat/100). `VGC_SM_Locations` + `VGC_SM_Sales`; REST in `trait-pos.php`.

**Refund / void (1.66.0, DB 0.23.0).** `VGC_SM_Sales::refund($sale_id, $lines, $note, $void)` returns units to the selling location via `VGC_SM_Locations::return_from_sale` (a `sale_return` location-ledger entry, mirror of `deduct_for_sale`; no stock movement). Per-line refund value is proportional to the stored post-discount line totals; **clearing a line takes the exact stored remainder**, so a full void returns precisely what was charged. New columns: `sale_lines.refunded_{qty,net,vat,gross}`, `sales.refunded_{net,vat,gross}` + `refunded_at`/`refunded_by`; `sales.status` moves `completed`→`partial_refund`→`voided`. `recent()` surfaces `status`, `refunded_gross`, `net_gross`. Manager-only, on the main-app receipt (`receipt-refund` card) — the till stays sell-only. Defensive `ensure_refund_columns()` (guarded by option `vgc_sm_sales_refund_columns`) adds the columns if dbDelta skipped them.

**Printable receipt (1.67.0).** The receipt page has **Print 80mm slip** / **Print A4** buttons → `openReceiptPrint(mode, sale)` mounts the shared `.vgc-sm-printov` overlay (same mechanism as the order print sheet) with `V.tpl.locations.receiptSheet` (single-column `.vgc-sm-rsheet`, `.is-80` = compact thermal slip). Paper size is set per mode by injecting a `<style id="vgc-sm-pagesize">@page{size:80mm auto|A4}</style>` at print time (removed on close). Letterhead comes from `boot.print` (Settings). No server side — pure print/PDF.

**"Nothing here yet" on every location — the real root cause (1.71.0).** `VGC_SM_Locations::inventory()` and `shape()` selected **`i.price_gross`**, but there is **no such column** on the items table — `price_gross` is derived (`price_net * (1 + vat_rate/100)`) everywhere in the app. MySQL threw *Unknown column* → `get_results` returned empty → location inventory, the till product grid, and location value totals were all blank, even though the item page's `item_breakdown()` (which selects no item price columns) showed the right per-location quantities. Fixed by selecting `price_net` + `vat_rate` and computing gross in PHP (`inventory`/`shape_stock_row`/`shape`, and `VGC_SM_Sales::checkout` which read `$item['price_gross']` off the raw `SELECT *` row — also undefined). **Lesson:** the items table stores `price_net`/`price_b2b`/`cost_net`/`vat_rate` only; never `SELECT i.price_gross`/`i.cost_gross`.

**Location-stock cache is ledger-derived (1.70.0).** `location_stock` is **recomputed from `location_ledger`** after every write via `VGC_SM_Locations::sync_stock_cache($loc,$item)` (SELECT SUM → upsert as a `%s` canonical-decimal string), so it can't drift. One-time repair on upgrade (option `vgc_sm_pos_tables_repaired_v1`): `VGC_SM_Install::ensure_pos_tables()` (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`) + `VGC_SM_Locations::rebuild_stock_cache()`. Good hygiene, but note the cache was NOT the display bug — the phantom-column SELECT above was. Push/pull UI is Orders-style inline (staged rows / inline pull editor) — **no `prompt()`** (see [[vgc-stock-manager-no-browser-prompts]]).

**Till redesign (1.75.0, DB 0.25.0) — first slice of the Claude Design pass.** Design deliverable lives in the `App design brief` project (`0aea936f-…`, read via the DesignSync MCP) and locally as `vgc-stock-manager-design-v3.html`; **tokens are unchanged**, so the whole redesign is CSS + markup inside the existing system. Till changes: dark counter bar (`--vgc-panel`), location picked from a **sheet** (`.vgc-sm-possheet`) not a `<select>`, **filter chips** (All / ★ Favourites / one per category, derived from what's actually on the shelf), tiles with image-or-tinted-placeholder + in-cart badge + disabled-when-out, `%`/amount **segmented** discounts, green **Charge** button (`--vgc-ok`), and a done screen that can **print the 80mm slip from the counter**. Phone: the sale becomes a **docked summary** (`.vgc-sm-posdock`, mini lines + total always visible) that expands into a bottom **sheet** — the answer to "you can't see the total while adding". New data it needs: `items.favourite`, `sale_locations.kind` (counter/popup/market/other), and `category`/`favourite` now returned per location-inventory row (LEFT JOIN categories). Guarded by `vgc_sm_item_columns_v4` + `VGC_SM_Locations::ensure_location_columns()` (`ensure_pos_tables()` only creates missing tables — it cannot ALTER an existing one). The receipt sheet moved to **`js/tpl/receipt.tpl.js`, loaded by both the app and the till page** (`V.tpl.receipt`; `V.tpl.locations.receiptSheet` now delegates), and the till boot gained `print` (letterhead). The till page is **viewport-fit=cover + black-translucent**, so every till surface must inset itself with `env(safe-area-inset-*)` (1.75.1 — the bar grows by the top inset, the dock/foot/sheets clear the home indicator; `height:100dvh` because iOS 100vh includes browser chrome). The main app shell already did this; the till CSS did not. **Design pass complete** (from the imported Claude Design redesign): till (1.75.0), nav grouping (1.83.0), Sales-over-time chart (1.84.0), receipt refund-in-place (1.85.0), sale-location tiles (1.86.0). Only the QR addendum (brief 4) may still be awaiting Claude Design's own response.

**QR bin labels (1.73.0, DB 0.24.0).** `app/js/qr.js` is a **self-written** QR encoder (byte mode, EC level M, versions 1–6 ⇒ 106-char ceiling; auto version + mask). Written rather than vendored because the app is a self-hosted offline PWA. **Verified by round-trip decoding with `zbar`** — 7 payloads × 8 forced masks + auto — not by matrix-diffing a reference encoder (that gives false failures: `segno` picks alphanumeric mode and pads differently). That round-trip caught a real bug: the alignment-pattern centre is `size-7`, **not** `4*version+6`; v1 decoded fine while every v≥2 was unreadable, i.e. every real label URL. `V.qrSvg(text, {size, unit, quiet, fg, bg})` renders inline SVG. Label = `item-label` card, 65×55mm (`.vgc-sm-lbl`), QR at 32mm with a **4-module quiet zone**, always on a **white tile** so a dark `label_color` stays scannable; ink auto-flips black/white by luminance. `label_color` is a nullable `#rrggbb` column on items (strict regex in `sanitize()`, which is a **whitelist** — a new field silently vanishes unless added there), added via dbDelta + `ensure_item_columns()` guarded by the new `vgc_sm_item_columns_v3` option (v2 is already set on live installs). Print uses the shared overlay with `@page{size:65mm 55mm;margin:0}`; Download SVG emits the QR at exact size. QR payload = `<appUrl>?vgc_sm_scan=<SKU>`.

**Scan route + landing (1.74.0).** `maybe_render()` branches on `?vgc_sm_scan=<code>` **after** the auth gate → `VGC_SM_Repository::get_item_by_barcode()` (barcode, falling back to SKU — which is why a SKU payload resolves) → `wp_safe_redirect( url() . '#/item/{id}?scanned=1' )`; no match → `#/products?scan_unknown=<code>`, which the router turns into a warn toast. The login form's action carries `vgc_sm_scan` through, so scanning while signed out still lands on the item after login. The router passes `r.query` into `S.viewItem(id, query)`; `scanned` renders `scannedPanel()` (`item-scanned`) **above the hero** with **Receive stock** → the existing inline `movementForm(i,'receive')` and **Record production** → `#/produce?item={id}` (made items only). This is the whole point of the QR work: it needs **no in-page scanning API**, so it works on iOS where `BarcodeDetector` does not.

**Mobile navigation (1.72.0).** The bottom tab bar only renders `NAV_MAIN` entries that carry a `tab:` label (6 of them), so every other destination used to be desktop-sidebar-only — unreachable on a phone. The topbar now has `#vgc-sm-menu` (`.vgc-sm-topbar__menu.vgc-sm-phoneonly`, hidden ≥900px) → `openNavDrawer()` in core.js, which reuses `openDrawer()` to list **all** groups (`NAV_MAIN` + `NAV_MANAGER` if manager + `NAV_ADMIN` if admin + `NAV_HELP`) with the active route highlighted, plus the signed-in user and sign-out in the drawer foot. Each entry carries `data-drawer-close="1"` so a tap navigates *and* dismisses. The click is bound at **module load** (not in `V.start()`) so the menu works as soon as the shell exists.

**Material costing (1.76.0–1.77.0, DB 0.26.0).** `class-costing.php` derives what a material really costs from `purchase_lines` — the workshop buys the same thing at seasonal prices, so a typed `cost_net` goes stale. **Derived, never accumulated:** purchases are living documents (a price can be corrected months later), so a running average would drift silently; everything is recomputed from history on every `reconcile()` (`recalculate_for_purchase`), exactly as `location_stock` is recomputed from its ledger. `items.avg_cost` / `last_cost` / `last_cost_at` are a CACHE, not truth.

`landed_unit_cost()` = the line's **discounted** net unit price (`VGC_SM_Purchases::line_shares()[id]['unit_net']`, falling back to `net_cost()` when the line is not in the document) **+ this line's share of `transport_cost` allocated by discounted line value** when `transport_payer='us'` (1.142.0; before that it was `net_cost()`, the list price). `average_for_on_hand()` walks receipts **newest-first until they cover `stock_qty`** (FIFO-consistent: what's left is the newest stock), falling back to the whole history when stock is 0 so the figure carries forward instead of collapsing. Consignment (`mode='consignment'`) is excluded — not ours until bought; `qty_returned` comes off. `stats()` adds the trailing-window average (`WINDOW_DAYS` 90 — "what it costs to buy now", which diverges sharply from the holding average after a seasonal buy; price against the former, value stock at the latter). Both maths functions are **pure and unit-tested** (17 cases: VAT strip, freight-by-value, returns, seasonal, zero stock).

**Which figure a recipe costs from is a setting, defaulting to today's behaviour** (`cost_basis`: `manual` | `average` | `recent`, added 1.77.0). `VGC_SM_Costing::effective_cost($item, $basis=null)` is the single door — `class-bom.php` calls it for every leaf instead of reading `cost_net` directly, so the whole cost tree follows the setting. Precedence: **`cost_manual` always wins** (a hand-typed cost is an explicit human decision), then the basis, then a fallback to `cost_net` when there's no purchase history. A zero average is a real zero, not "missing". Switching basis rewires every margin in the system, so `preview_basis()` returns the before/after for every affected item and the Settings card makes you look at it before applying. `report($days)` powers the Material prices screen (GET `/costs`, manager) and `preview_basis()` powers GET `/costs/preview` (admin). 24 unit cases cover the two maths functions plus `effective_cost` precedence.

**`receipts()` has two sources** (1.78.0, DB 0.27.0): purchase lines, and **hand receipts priced on the item page** (`movements.unit_cost`, added by `ensure_movement_columns()`). Not every delivery gets a purchase document, and an unpriced receipt is invisible to costing. Movements raised BY a purchase are excluded on `ref_type <> 'purchase'` so nothing is double-counted, and the merged list is re-sorted newest-first because `average_for_on_hand()` depends on that order. `unit_cost` is only ever written by POST `/movements` for `type=receive`; an empty box records NULL, never 0. **Gotcha:** in pack mode the UI reads the price *per pack* (matching the invoice) and divides by `pack_size` before posting — the label must follow the mode from first render, not just on change.

**Lot pricing (1.114.0, DB 0.34.0).** `movements.lot_cost` and `purchase_lines.lot_cost` (both `DECIMAL(14,4) NULL`, added by `Install::ensure_lot_price_columns()`, guard `vgc_sm_lot_price_v1`). **Not-null IS the mode** — there is no separate flag to fall out of step with the number. The **lot total is stored and the unit price is derived**, never the reverse: 100.00 over 3 units is 33.3333 each and 33.3333 x 3 is 99.9999, so a round trip through the unit price would show a total nobody was charged. `Purchases::unit_from_lot()` divides by `qty_received` when anything has arrived, else `qty_ordered` (the invoice is for the goods that came); `qty_returned` never re-rates the goods. `POST /movements` derives from `abs(qty)`, which is already in BASE units — a lot price on a delivery received by the box must NOT be divided by `pack_size` as well. `Corrections::edit_movement()` re-splits the same total when the QUANTITY is corrected, and clearing `lot_cost` (empty string) returns the entry to a price each. Everything downstream still reads one `unit_cost`.

### Hardening (1.81.0)
Security audit outcome — foundations were sound (prepared SQL, esc() discipline, per-route permissions, whitelisted uploads, token never echoed); what shipped closes the gaps found:
- **Headers**: `security_headers()` in class-frontend runs for every `head()` document (app, till, login) — CSP (`'unsafe-inline'` required: boot payload + template style attrs are inline by design), SAMEORIGIN framing, nosniff, same-origin referrer.
- **Login throttle** is two-axis (per-IP AND per-username, 8 fails / 10 min) and covers wp-login.php/XML-RPC via `authenticate` (prio 30) + `wp_login_failed`/`wp_login` hooks. Counting happens ONLY in those hooks — handle_login must not also count, or every branded-login failure counts twice.
- **Updater** verifies `sha256` from the manifest in `upgrader_pre_download` when present (hash_equals). **The release ritual must therefore put the zip's sha256 into plugins.json** — an entry without one installs unverified (back-compat). The updater copy is per-plugin; other VGC plugins still bundle the old one.
- **CSV**: `csvCell()` neutralises leading `=+-@` on non-numeric values (Excel formula injection); real negatives stay numeric.
- **Logout** purges Cache Storage before navigating (cached shell embeds the boot payload).
- **Session expiry** (C1): `rawFetch`/`upload` detect `rest_cookie_invalid_nonce` → one-time full-screen overlay → reload. WP nonces live 12–24 h; this WILL happen daily on a till.
- **Perf**: `report()` uses `receipts_for_all()` (2 queries total) + pure `stats_from_receipts()` — `stats($id)` wraps the same maths, so figures cannot diverge. Audit log prunes to 365 days via the `vgc_sm_daily` cron (cleared on deactivation).
- Location `push()`/`pull()` now return `movement_id`/`ledger_id`; corrections link reversals to exact ids, never "newest row".
- **Sale-location tiles (1.86.0)**: `locKind(kind)` -> {label,tone} and `locStatus({active,units})` -> {label,cls} are the two derived-identity helpers in locations.tpl.js; `locTile()` renders the grid card. Kind accent = coloured left border via `--vgc-loc-accent` per `.vgc-sm-loctile--{counter|popup|market|other}`. Status is DERIVED, not stored: active+stock=live, active+empty=empty, inactive=off (no open/closed field exists - do not invent one). formCard gained a `kind` <select> (server already accepted `kind` on create/update since 1.75.0 - this was a UI-only gap); save body sends `kind`. Open till promoted to primary on the list and detail. devids unchanged (`locations-list`, `location-header`, `location-actions`).
- **Check against the shop, and past orders for the books only (1.152.0, DB 0.53.0)** — owner: *"after the fixes how do I update the orders — add a resync"*, and the books-only import I had offered for pulling history.

  **First, a correction I owed:** 1.151.0's notes said re-pulling would fix orders imported before the negative-line fix. It would not. `apply()` on a sale already in the books that still counts only restamped its status and fee — it never compared the lines — and the hourly pull never revisits an order the shop has not touched. So a pre-fix sale stayed wrong for ever. Said so in the readme where it is read.

  **Resync** (`VGC_SM_Shop_Orders::resync()`, `POST /shop/orders/resync`) walks every order from the start date, ignoring the hourly cursor and leaving it where it is, and for each: missing → import; stopped counting → void; **differs** → `delete_sale()` and import again from the shop's order; agrees → left exactly alone. `differs()` compares the lines as a sorted bag of (what, how many, how much to the stotinka) as well as the total — two wrong lines can add up to a right total, and "what" includes whether a line is a product or not, so a product linked since import is a difference. **`apply` defaults to false: a PREVIEW** that decides everything and writes nothing, down to not deleting a voided sale it would revive. The screen shows it order by order with the reason in money, and the Apply button exists only when there is something to apply.

  **Books-only history.** `orders_history_before`: orders dated before it (in the workshop's clock, same as the receipt) are imported through `checkout()` with `no_stock` — sale, lines, prices and cost recorded; nothing touches a shelf, a maker's bucket or the backlog. `sales.stock_moved` records it, **stored rather than derived** because "no ledger rows" could equally mean a service or a maker's goods, and `refund()` needs to tell them apart: a books-only sale's refund gives back nothing it never took. The line is set on the day the pull is switched on, and an install already pulling gets it at its `orders_from` on boot, so moving the start date back can never double-count. An empty or unreadable value is ignored rather than clearing it. **A resync never changes a sale's stock mode**; a correction and a revived order both keep the mode the sale was first imported with.

  **Two more found on staging before release, neither by a test:**
  1. **A refund stopped the pull (Bridge 0.6.1).** Reading `debug.log` after the shipping probe showed `OrderRefund::get_order_number()` — `wc_get_orders()` had handed back a REFUND alongside the orders, shaping it was fatal, and a failed page ends the pull, so one refund anywhere in the range kept every later order out of the books. The shipping probe stayed green because nothing asserted that its pull had no errors after the refund step. Now `'type' => 'shop_order'` on the query and an `is_order()` check (class AND the methods shaping relies on) on both the list and the by-id paths; the harness reproduces the exact fatal with the fix removed; the probe asserts no errors and that an order placed after a refund still arrives.
  2. **An estimated fee was never replaced.** `chase_fees()` only asked about sales with NO fee, so a fee estimated from a rule at import — because the gateway had not yet written its figure — stayed an estimate for ever. `expenses.basis` ('gateway' / 'rule') now records how each fee was arrived at (not the note, which is in the site's language), the chase asks about `e.id IS NULL OR e.basis = 'rule'`, and it only writes when something is learned. The probe for this had been passing by LUCK: its orders were created within one second, so the order it changed sat on the cursor's inclusive boundary and the ordinary pull re-read it. It now pushes the cursor past them so only the chase can find the change, and runs three times.

  Verified: 135 checks in `shoporders.php`, 69 in `bridgeorders.php`, 40 in `shoptrack.js`, a **23-mutant sweep all caught**, and on staging against real WooCommerce — fees 22/22 three runs running, resync 32/32, orders 33/33, shipping 49/49 including the refund path, `debug.log` clean — — a month-old order in for the books with the shelf untouched and its cancellation inventing nothing; a pre-fix sale found by the preview (which changed nothing), then corrected with the stock exactly where it was; a missed order found and brought in; an unknown product linked later and then taken off the shelf. The orders, fees and shipping probes re-run green on this build (33 + 17 + 47).

- **Shipping validated; a negative line fixed; card fees recorded (1.151.0, DB 0.52.0 — with Bridge 0.6.0)** — owner: *"some shipping costs are not appearing correct — validate it"* and *"for orders paid with cards there is a cost of the transaction, can you log that as well."*

  **Shipping was validated and is correct.** The 1.150.0 probe had run against a shop with NO TAX RATES, so every line's tax was zero and the VAT path was never exercised — a hole in the verification, not in the code. A real 20% rate (shipping taxable) and every shape a shop produces: flat, free, two carriers on one order, amounts that do not divide by the quantity, large quantities, a taxed fee, an untaxed fee, no shipping at all, **prices entered inclusive of tax**, and **tax rounded at the subtotal**. Gross, net and the sale's VAT match WooCommerce to the stotinka in all of them.

  **What was wrong next door: a negative line was clamped to zero.** `checkout()` did `max( 0, $unit_price )` — right for a till, where an operator typing −25 has made a mistake, and wrong for an imported line, because **a shop records a discount as a fee of minus four lev** and most discount and loyalty plugins do exactly that. The discount vanished and the sale read HIGHER than the customer paid: 66.00 against 61.20. Negatives are now allowed on external lines only, and an external line's unit is kept to 6 decimals so a line total divided by a quantity comes back exact. External lines also stopped counting towards `item_count` — postage is not a thing anybody carries home.

  **Card fees.** Bridge 0.6.0 sends `payment_method`, `payment_method_title` and `payment_fee` — the last read from the gateway's own meta (`_stripe_fee`, `_paypal_transaction_fee`, `_ppcp_paypal_fees` and friends), **null when nothing is recorded, never zero**: "nobody said" and "it was free" are different facts. The manager uses that exact figure when it exists and falls back to a **rule per payment method** (percent + fixed, on the GROSS — the processor took its cut of the whole payment and has no idea which part was VAT). No rule and no report means nothing is booked; an estimate that looks like a fact is worse than a gap.

  Each fee is an **expense owned by the sale**: `expenses.sale_id`, UNIQUE, upserted, read-only in the Business-costs screen, removed when the sale is voided or deleted. Filed in a **Card fees** category with `in_product_cost = 0` — taking a card payment is a cost of selling, and folding it into a product's cost would restate every recipe.

  **The finding that shaped the design:** a gateway commonly writes its fee when the payout settles, days later — and **a meta-only save does not bump an order's `post_modified`** (proved against WooCommerce 11.1). So the order looks untouched and a `modified_after` pull will never see it again; the fee would simply never be recorded. `chase_fees()` therefore asks the shop **by id** about recent shop sales that still have no fee — `GET /orders?include=…`, last 7 days, 25 at a time, stopping by itself. Bounded twice, because it is a poll.

  Verified: 95 checks in `shoporders.php`, 64 in `bridgeorders.php`, an **18-mutant sweep all caught**, 85/85 harnesses, and **97 checks on staging** across three probes against real WooCommerce — shipping in every configuration, the whole order flow, and the fees end to end including a fee written after the fact.

- **The shop's orders become sales (1.150.0, DB 0.51.0 — with Bridge 0.5.1)** — owner: *"I want to be able to pull the orders as well."*

  **The decision the whole thing rests on: an online order is a SALE like any other**, at a location of kind `online` called Online shop. Not a parallel set of numbers with its own screens and its own totals. That is what makes turnover, profit, margin, the chart, the CSV, refunds and the cost of goods all work without one report being touched.

  **The status is the fact.** The owner picks which of the shop's statuses mean the goods have gone (default `wc-completed`; the list is read live from the shop, so a custom status is offered like any other). Counts and no sale yet → write it. Counts and already in → nothing. Stops counting → **void** it, goods back, reason recorded. So a cancellation reverses itself, and an order that bounces between statuses ends up wherever it last landed. **The pull deliberately does NOT filter by status** — a filtered pull is exactly the pull that cannot see an order leaving the counting set, and could therefore never reverse one.

  **`VGC_SM_Locations::sells_from_workshop()`** is the new single answer to "does this place sell off the workshop shelf": true of the primary counter and true of the online shop. `checkout()` and `held_place()` both read it, so an online sale walks its goods over as it records them (company inventory = workshop + locations stays true) and a maker's goods sold online come out of the workshop's bucket rather than a shelf a website does not have.

  **Money is the shop's own, worked backwards.** The shop sends net + tax per line; the till runs on a gross unit price and a rate, so the gross goes over exactly as charged and the rate is derived per line. The sale's total therefore equals the order total to the stotinka — asserted on staging against a real order. **Postage and fees are carried as lines with no item** (`item_id = 0` + a label), as is a product the workshop has never heard of; the difference between them is that an unknown product keeps its SKU, and `item_id = 0 AND sku <> ''` is what the screens read to count what needs linking. The fact is the mode; there is no flag.

  `sales` gains `source` / `ext_id` / `ext_number` / `ext_status` with **`UNIQUE KEY ext (source, ext_id)`** — the backstop against two overlapping crons importing one order twice. Receipts are numbered `W-<order number>`, which cannot collide with the till's `S-00001`. Dated by the shop's own moment, localised, so a day's takings do not move depending on when the cron ran.

  Hourly cron (`vgc_sm_pull_orders`), a **Pull now** button, and a cursor on `date_modified` — inclusive, and never advanced past an order that failed.

  **Two bugs found only by putting WooCommerce on staging and pulling from it, neither reachable from a fake:**
  1. **The shop's REST answers were being HTTP-cached by the host.** The backoffice asked what had changed and was handed the same answer as minutes before — so it imported a sale for an order that had since been DELETED, and could not see a cancellation at all, because the response never changed. Nothing errored; both sides worked perfectly on stale data. Bridge 0.5.1 declares the whole namespace uncacheable (`no-store`, plus the headers SiteGround, LiteSpeed and Cloudflare each read) and the manager adds a cache-buster to every GET, because a host that ignores that header is a host that ignores that header.
  2. **`refund()` returned the goods to the SELLING LOCATION** — right for a stall, wrong for a website, which has no shelf. They now walk home to the workshop when the location is online. The same probe also showed `refund()` had always accepted a `$note` and never stored it; it is appended to the sale's note now, which for a shop-reversed sale is the only record that the shop, not a person, did it.

  Verified: 69 checks in `shoporders.php`, 52 in `bridgeorders.php`, `payloadkeys.js` extended to a third form posting to the same route, a **31-mutant sweep, all caught**, 85/85 harnesses — and **33/33 end to end on staging** against real WooCommerce 11.1 over a real HTTPS round trip: order in, sale out, totals matching, stock down, cancelled, stock back, completed again, once.

- **The Bridge can be asked what the shop sold (Bridge 0.5.0)** — groundwork for pulling online orders. `GET /orders` (number, status, dates, money, lines, each line typed `line_item` / `shipping` / `fee`) and `GET /order-statuses` (read from `wc_get_order_statuses()`, so a shop's custom status can be chosen). The controller's header used to promise "no access to orders"; the new promise is written down in its place: **read only and anonymous** — no name, address, email, phone or customer id, so no second copy of the shop's customer list is created on the backoffice server, and nothing here can cancel, refund, complete or edit.
  Totals are the SHOP'S own figures, never recomputed: the backoffice's copy of an order has to add up to what the customer was charged, and a rounding rule that disagreed with WooCommerce would put stotinki between the two books for ever.
  **`modified_after`, as an integer timestamp**, is what makes an incremental pull correct. Two findings from staging, both invisible to the fakes: an order placed last week and completed this morning has an OLD creation date and a NEW modification date (so asking by creation date never sees it change), and `wc_get_orders()` **silently ignores a formatted date string** — it returns every order, every time, without erroring. `'>=' . $ts`, inclusive, so nothing falls through the boundary second. Oldest first, so a pull that dies halfway leaves a cursor still true of everything before it.
  Verified: 52 checks in `bridgeorders.php` against fakes, and 21 against **real WooCommerce 11.1 on staging** over a real HTTPS round trip (the backoffice staging site now hosts WooCommerce and the bridge and pulls from itself).
  Still to come, the backoffice half: settings (which statuses count as sold, from-date), an "Online shop" location, orders landing as sales, postage as its own line, unknown SKUs imported and flagged.

- **The shop's stock can be an allowance rather than a mirror (1.149.0, no DB change)** — owner: *"the online store holds stock more like a stop gap that controls the amount of orders we receive, rather than actually having its own stock."* A quantity on a shop product means one of two things and only the owner knows which: a MIRROR of the workshop shelf, or an ORDER ALLOWANCE that caps how many orders come in. The plugin had only ever assumed the first — and every push topped the allowance back up, so tracking quietly defeated the cap.
  `track_shop_stock` (default **1**, so an existing install is unchanged) in `VGC_SM_Sync::settings()`, read through `tracks_shop_stock()`. The gate stands in `push_adjust()` and `push_set()` — the only two transports that WRITE — so notes, orders, purchases, production and the Shop screen are all covered by one refusal (`vgc_sm_shop_untracked`) rather than seven. `on_production()` returns **before** logging: a refusal per batch would fill the sync log with failures nobody can act on.
  Screens obey it: the settings card explains which mode it is in and **disables** auto-push (a live tick box that does nothing is worse than a dead one); the Shop screen becomes **Shop allowance**, drops *Set shop = local*, and keeps the levels because reading them is how you see what is left. `tracked` rides on `/shop/levels`; **absent means tracked**, in the template and in the screen alike, so an older cached client behaves as it always did.
  The staging probe found a real one before release: `/shop/levels` had **two returns** and the unconfigured one had never heard of `tracked`. It now builds one array once, and two mutants pin that.
  Verified: 12 new checks in `settingsmerge.php` (with the HTTP stubs now RECORDING, since the claim is that nothing goes over the wire), a new `shoptrack.js` (17) rendering both screens in both modes, `payloadkeys.js` extended to the shop-connection card, and a 13-mutant sweep, all caught.

- **A location's list settles from stock we already have (1.148.0, no DB change)** — owner-reported: *"when I have a backlog of a product in a sale location and I manufacture it, I should be able to use the current stock to resolve the backlog."* `resolve_item()` always ran a production run (or invented a delivery) for the whole of what was owed and only then pushed, so a batch already on the shelf was made **twice**: materials consumed for goods that existed, and the workshop's count left permanently high. A correctness gap, not a convenience one.
  **The model:** the negative on a shelf has two possible stories, and the app cannot know which. *Made and not written down* is a production run; *taken off the workshop shelf and not written down* is a plain transfer. So there are now two ways out of one list, and the screen offers the one that fits what the shelf actually holds.
  `VGC_SM_Backlog::settle_from_stock()` **makes nothing, ever** — it is `push()` plus closing the entries. It sends `min( owed, max( 0, stock_qty ) )`; **what the shelf cannot cover stays on the list** (owner's choice over auto-producing the difference), and `remaining` says so out loud.
  **Partial settlement splits an entry** rather than decrementing it: the settled part is `resolved` against the transfer's `movement_id`, and a NEW open row carries the remainder with the same `sale_id`, `sale_line_id`, `user_id` and `created_at` — so a later refund still finds it, the list stays dated by when the debt arose, and `resolved_ref_id` remains the one thread from a closed entry back to what closed it. Entries close **oldest first**.
  `outstanding_for()` (raw rows, `ORDER BY created_at, id`) and `close_oldest_first()` are now the single authors of "what is open here" and "what closing means"; `resolve_item()` was refactored onto both.
  **Only for what we make** — a bought-in shortfall keeps logging its delivery (owner's call: the paperwork there is an invoice), refused server-side as well as hidden on the screen. `grouped()` carries `at_workshop` (clamped at 0, so an overdrawn workshop is not a negative offer) and the row draws **Send from stock** as the primary button only when there is something to send.
  Route: `POST /locations/{id}/backlog` with `source: 'stock'`; operator-level like the production settlement, because the quantity and destination are both fixed by an obligation already on the books.
  Verified: 47 new checks in `primary.php` (the real `push()`, backlog and ledger) and 15 in `primaryui.js`, plus a **19-mutant sweep, all 19 caught** — including two faults in the *harness* it exposed: the fake was writing only `status` on a bulk close, and was handing over `stock_qty` the SELECT had not asked for.

- **Fix: a partner's logo was dropped by the route (1.147.1)** — owner-reported within the day: *"adding logo to a partner does not save."* `trait-partners.php`'s `partner_input()` copies a NAMED LIST of fields out of the request, and `logo_id` was not on it. Everything underneath was right — the column, `sanitize()`, `create()`/`update()`, the shaper — so the screen sent the id, the route dropped it, and nothing errored or logged.
  **Why the release's own gates missed it:** `logos.js` drives the templates and the picker, which were both correct; the staging probe called `VGC_SM_Partners::create()` **directly**, which is the layer below the one that broke. A probe that skips the route cannot see the route.
  **The guard, not just the fix:** `payloadkeys.js` reads the keys of the body each form posts out of the client source and the field list out of the PHP handler, and fails when they disagree — partners and settings today. Verified by reinstating the fault: with `logo_id` off the list it goes red on exactly the two checks it should. The staging probe is now `rest_do_request()` with the body the browser posts, and it reproduced the bug on 1.147.0 (6 failures) before passing on 1.147.1.

- **Logos, ours and our partners' (1.147.0, DB 0.50.0)** — owner: *"I need to have the ability to add logos for me and my partners."* **Owner's decisions:** ours on printed documents AND the app's own header AND the till; theirs on their page and the list AND on documents involving them AND on items we buy from them; **any shape, fitted, never cropped**.
  **Storage.** Ours is a setting, `biz_logo_id`, beside the other printed-document fields; theirs is `partners.logo_id` (repair `vgc_sm_partner_logo_v1`). Both are attachment ids in the media library, uploaded through the `POST /media` route an item photo already uses — so an operator who may photograph stock may set a logo, with no `wp-admin` rights.
  **One reader each.** `VGC_SM_Frontend::logo_url( $size )` is the only place the setting is resolved, because it is wanted in four (sign-in, app chrome, till, documents) and an attachment deleted from the library afterwards has to read as "no logo" in all four rather than as a broken image in three. `VGC_SM_Partners::logo_urls( $row )` does the same for a partner, returning medium and thumbnail.
  **One gesture.** `V.logoPicker( prefix, url, id )` + `V.bindLogoPicker( prefix, root )` in core.js: the current picture or "No logo", Choose, Remove, a hidden input the form reads exactly as the item form reads `f-image_id`. `prefix` keys every attribute, so Settings and a partner form can hold one each without writing into the other.
  **Fitted, never cropped** is `object-fit: contain` in every one of the eight places, with the box setting only the room available. A wide wordmark and a square roundel both work, which is the whole of the rule the owner asked for.
  **The fallback is the feature.** Every site had no logo yesterday, so everywhere a logo can appear keeps what was there before — the ◐ mark on paper, on the till and in the sidebar, the coloured initial for a partner. `logos.js` (28 checks) exists mostly to hold that line.
  **The one thing the eye caught:** on the printed letterhead a 150px logo beside the business name left the name 24px and broke it over three lines. `.vgc-sm-psheet__brand` now takes the room the meta column does not want.
