  **Gates:** `logos.js` 28, plus the two screens that hand a partner's mark to a document (`purchasetabs.js`, `orderlinks.js`) — a pure-template test cannot see that half. Sweep: 20 faults, all caught.
- **The goods-received note (1.146.0, no schema change)** — owner: *"When I got a delivery of product on consignment and I accept them, I need to be able to print out a list of the product I recieved."* **Owner's decisions:** the goods **and what they are worth**; **signature lines** so the maker keeps a copy; the received quantity, with the ordered one **only where they differ**.
  `T.receivedSheet()` in `purchases.tpl.js` and `openReceivedPrint()` in `purchases.js`, reusing the order print's overlay wholesale — `.vgc-sm-printov`, the `vgc-sm-has-printov` body class the `@media print` block keys off, Escape/backdrop/Close. `printBar()` moved from `orders.tpl.js` into **core.js** (the `discountControl()` precedent) so the two documents cannot end up with different ways out; orders keeps its old export name pointing at it.
  **What it values, and why it is not the document's own total.** A line is worth `qty_received × net_cost` — NOT `line_shares()['line_net']`. On consignment `billable_qty()` is `qty_sold`, so the document's line total is **zero** until the goods sell, and a delivery note of twelve candles valued at nothing is no use to the maker who just handed them over. The label follows the mode: *Value if all sold* on consignment, *Value of goods, excl. VAT* on a buy — neither claims to be a bill, which is also why a discount for the whole delivery does not appear on it.
  A consignment note says **On consignment** and states the goods remain the supplier's; a buy says **Bought outright**. Non-stock lines (1.143.0) are left off — a machine is not goods off a van — and so is any line where nothing arrived. The button appears only on a saved purchase, which is the only kind with a number to head the page.
  **Gates:** 20 checks inside `purchasetabs.js`, driving the real screen and opening the real overlay (the short delivery's extra column and its matching header count, the cost line carrying quantities that must still be left off, the two labels, the property notice, the signature block, the printing-mode class going on and coming off, Escape, and that opening it prints nothing by itself). The sweep is 18 faults, all caught.
- **A maker's goods have a place (1.145.0, DB 0.49.0)** — the owner's third decision from the till work: a stall should hold a maker's goods in its own right rather than the held bucket being one company-wide pool.
  **One ledger, one more dimension.** `consignment_ledger.location_id`, 0 = the workshop, plus a `(location_id, item_id)` key, behind `vgc_sm_held_location_v1`. Every existing row is at the workshop, which is true, so there is nothing to backfill. **Not a second table** — held must have one answer, and a `location_held` mirror would be a second one. `held( $item, $loc = null )` and `held_by_maker( $item, $loc = null )` keep their old meaning when no place is given, so every older caller is untouched; `plan_held_sale()` takes the place through to the FIFO walk; `held_places( $item )` says where an item's held stock is.
  **A move is a PAIR of rows** with the same maker — `−q` at the source, `+q` at the destination, `type = 'move'` — so the maker's total never changes and every balance, statement and `clamp_shop_held()` keeps working without knowing places exist. `move_held()` refuses more than that maker holds at the source, and refuses a move to where they already are.
  **Which bucket a till sells from:** `VGC_SM_Locations::held_place( $loc )` — the workshop's (0) when it IS the workshop's counter, its own otherwise, exactly the rule our own stock follows. `checkout()` reads it once, caps against it, plans against it and passes it to `issue()` as `held_location`, so the ledger row says where the goods left from. `cancel()` reads that row back rather than assuming, and `sale_held_notes.location_id` records it on the sale — **the bug the two-places test found**: without it, a part refund at a stall cancelled four at the stall and re-issued two out of the WORKSHOP's bucket.
  **Screen:** `location-held` on a sale location — what is here, what is still at the workshop, Send here / Send back per maker, quantity defaulting to all of them. Absent at the workshop counter, which sells that bucket directly. New routes `GET /locations/{id}/held` and `POST /locations/{id}/held/move` (manager).
  **Behaviour change to know about:** a stall can no longer sell a maker's goods that are sitting in the workshop — they have to be sent over first. That is the point, and it is why `at_held` on the till feed is now per place.
  **Gates:** `heldtill.php` is 101 checks (two places holding the same maker's goods, the counter that cannot spend the stall's, the move and its refusals, the refund that goes back where it came from) and `primaryui.js` 402. The sweep is 20 faults, all caught; one case is left out with a note, because `sellable_catalogue()`'s primary branch only runs for a primary location, where `held_place()` IS 0.
- **A maker's goods at the till (1.144.0, DB 0.48.0)** — owner, after a consignment purchase: *"I dont see the qty on the till and this allow me to sell more than I currently hold on consignment."* Three defects, one of them money. (1) `sellable_catalogue()` built the till's quantity as `location_stock + items.stock_qty`, and held is in neither, so a consigned item read as 0 with twenty on the table. (2) Nothing capped the sale — `Infinity` at the primary counter, a tick-and-backlog anywhere else. (3) **`class-sales.php` did not mention consignment at all**: selling five of a maker's candles left Held saying twenty, the maker owed nothing, and the deduction taken against our own (zero) stock, so it landed in backlog as goods we owed ourselves an explanation for.
  **Owner's decisions:** the till **sells held and books it** (a `sold_held` note per maker at the moment of sale); **our own stock goes first**; held later gains a per-location slice (1.145.0).
  **How it works.** `VGC_SM_Notes::plan_held_sale( $item_id, $qty, $already )` is the single answer to "whose goods did we just sell" — oldest consignment first, capped at what is really held, `$already` keeping two cart lines of one product from spending the same maker's last three candles. `reconcile_apply()` (the shop reconcile) now reads it too, so a unit cannot be booked to one maker on one screen and another on the other. `checkout()` plans the WHOLE cart before writing a row: own stock (shelf, plus the workshop at the primary counter) first, then the plan; `book_held()` raises one issued `sold_held` note per maker through `save_draft()` + `issue()`, priced from `partner_prices`; only `qty − held_taken` comes off our shelf, and the backlog gets what neither could cover. The line carries `held_qty` and a **blended `unit_cost`** — a maker's unit cost us what we now owe them, so the receipt's margin is the real one.
  **Nested transactions.** `issue()` and `cancel()` take `$opts['nested']`: MySQL has none, so a COMMIT inside them would commit the caller's half-written sale. In nested mode they write no transaction statements, return their error rather than rolling back, and leave the shop side effects to the caller — `checkout()` runs `clamp_shop_held()` for each affected item **after** its own COMMIT, because until then none of it had happened.
  **Unwinding.** `sale_lines.held_qty` + `sale_held_notes (sale_id, sale_line_id, item_id, note_id, partner_id, qty)`. A refund calls `unbook_held()` scoped to the LINE, newest booking first: the note is cancelled and, when only part came back, a smaller one is issued in its place, so the trail reads sold five → gave two back → owe for three. Only the rest of the refund reaches our shelf; putting a maker's unit there would invent stock we never owned. `delete_sale()` sweeps with `unbook_all()` for a receipt voided before any of this existed.
  **Screen:** the tile splits the figure ("4 + 6 held") and the cart line says how many of it are a maker's; `heldPart()` mirrors the own-first rule so the note agrees with what the server will book. A till away from the workshop now **refuses** beyond own + held and says which is which.
  **Gates:** `heldtill.php` 72 checks on real SQLite SQL (the FIFO split across two makers, own-first, the blended cost, two lines of one product, a stall's catalogue, the part refund and its re-issue, the void, the delete) and 15 more in `primaryui.js`. The sweep is 26 faults, all caught — including the note that commits the sale early.
- **Non-stock purchase lines (1.143.0, DB 0.47.0)** — owner: *"we can purchase a machine with the same order we get some materials, I need to have a tab for that and the cost of the machine should go to the Expenses."* **Owner's decisions:** the cost appears **as soon as the purchase is saved**, dated the delivery and kept in step with it; a machine is a **one-off**, never depreciated; the **category is picked per line** (blank allowed) and whether it counts towards the per-product overhead **follows that category's existing `in_product_cost` switch**; ship an **Equipment** category with that switch **off**.
  **One table, not two.** A non-stock line lives in `purchase_lines` with `kind = 'expense'`, `item_id = 0`, its own `label`/`expense_category_id`/`amount`, and every quantity at zero. The reason is the discount: the delivery's discount is apportioned over ONE ordered list (`line_shares()`), and a second table would mean a second apportionment and two answers to what the wax cost. Repaired by `Install::ensure_purchase_expense_columns()` behind `vgc_sm_purchase_expense_v1`, which also seeds Equipment.
  **The two edits that carry the risk.** (1) `reconcile()`'s all-zero cleanup DELETE now says `AND kind = 'stock'` — every quantity on an expense line is zero by definition, so without it the machine is deleted at the end of the save that created it and its cost is orphaned. (2) `save_lines()` refuses to let a line **change kind**: sending a material's id back as a machine would leave `applied_in` — "ten kilos are on the shelf" — on a row `reconcile()` now skips, and the stock would never come off. Both directions insert a new line and let the old row go through the ordinary release path.
  **The cost side.** `VGC_SM_Purchases::sync_expenses()` runs after `reconcile()` and is the delta contract for money: one `expenses` row per line, written by `VGC_SM_Expenses::save_owned()` with the line's **discounted** net (`line_shares()[id]['line_net']`), dated `date_received`, noted with the purchase number, `due_day` forced NULL (a non-null one would make it a recurring TEMPLATE and hide the money from every spend query), and `delete_owned_purchase( $id, $keep )` sweeping anything whose line is gone — idempotent, so a second save changes nothing and an orphan heals itself. `Expenses::save()`/`delete()` refuse a row carrying `purchase_line_id`; the screen shows "from PUR-…" and links to the document instead of Edit/Delete.
  **`totals()`** gains `expense_net`, `expense_gross`, `materials_net`; an expense line is in `net`/`vat`/`gross`/`balance` and out of `retail_net`/`profit`/`units`/`still_held` — money owed, never goods. On consignment the machine is owed for in full while the goods are not. An accrual bill (`is_accrual`, written by `VGC_SM_Payables`) drops expense lines on save: it is rewritten from its own source and anything typed on it would vanish anyway. `Costing` skips `item_id = 0` in `recalculate_for_purchase()` and `backfill_all()`.
  **Screen:** tabs inside the lines card (`purchase-linetabs`, `data-ltab`) — **Materials (n) / Other purchases (n)**, counts always visible so a cost on the closed tab is never out of sight and out of mind; rows are mapped over the WHOLE line list and the other tab's dropped, so `data-i` stays the real index and `syncForm()` cannot write the wax's quantity onto the machine. The cost row is its own narrow grid (`.vgc-sm-xline`), with the same discount strip underneath. New tile **Other purchases**, shown only when there is one.
  **Gates:** `purchaseexpense.php` 68 checks on real SQLite SQL (the cleanup DELETE, the conversion refusal, a hostile row that carries quantities, the orphan sweep, the read-only guard, consignment, accrual) and `purchasetabs.js` 43 in jsdom. The sweep is 27 faults, all caught.
- **Vendor discounts (1.142.0, DB 0.46.0)** — owner: *"I need to be able to add discounts on each line or the entire order (both should be an option) — we have vendors that gives us different discounts."* **Owner's decisions:** both a **percentage and an amount off**, at **line** and at **document** level (the till's `pct`/`val` vocabulary); the document discount is **spread over the lines by value**, so every material's unit cost falls by its own share; and the **suggested sell price keeps using the FULL cost** — a negotiated discount is extra profit, not a cheaper shelf (the owner overruled the recommendation here, deliberately).
  **Schema:** `purchases.discount_type`/`discount_value`, `purchase_lines.line_discount_type`/`line_discount_value`, repaired by `Install::ensure_purchase_discount_columns()` behind `vgc_sm_purchase_discount_v1`. **Nothing stores an AMOUNT**: a purchase recomputes its money on every read, so a stored figure would be a second source of truth going stale (the lot-price precedent — store what was typed, derive the rest).
  **One authority: `VGC_SM_Purchases::line_shares( $purchase )`**, keyed by line id → `{priced, line_off, order_off, priced_net, line_net, unit_net}`. `totals()`, `shape_purchase()` and `Costing::landed_unit_cost()` all read it, so the document, the screen and the material cost cannot disagree. `line_priced()` (lifted out of `line_net()`) is the as-priced figure before VAT and before any discount; `line_full()` is the whole line as invoiced; `canonical_lines()` sorts by `(sort_order, id)` because the **rounding remainder goes to the last line that is worth something** and three readers have to walk the lines identically — costing's sibling query is deliberately unordered so this stays the only ordering. The maths: an amount off a line is clamped to the whole line and then pro-rated by what is billable (10.00 off a lot of ten of which four arrived, or six of which went back, is 4.00); the document discount comes off `priced − line_off`, spread by value; **then** VAT is stripped, so net and VAT fall together the way the invoice reads. Both clamps (≤100%, ≤ the base) mean a line can never go below zero.
  **Screen:** the line discount is a **sibling strip under** `.vgc-sm-pline`, never a fourteenth cell (the row is a fixed 13-cell grid with a hairline pinned to the 6th — `lotui.js` asserts it); the document discount sits in `purchase-details` as a `[data-s]` field, **not** in the summary card, whose innerHTML is rewritten on every keystroke. `discountControl()` moved from `pos.tpl.js` into `core.js` so the till and the purchase screen cannot drift. A **Discounts** tile appears only when there is one; the strip says when a figure was capped. `purchases.js` mirrors `line_shares()` exactly (`shares()`), which is what keeps the browser's running total equal to the server's.
  **Gates:** `purchasediscount.php` 48 checks (the real `save_lines()` by reflection, `line_shares()`, `totals()`, `landed_unit_cost()` and `Costing::receipts()` building its own copy of the document), `purchasediscui.js` 33 (the real screen in jsdom). The sweep is 24 faults, all caught — including the remainder dropped, the remainder handed to a worthless line, the lines read in the opposite order, the discount taken after VAT, and the spread done evenly instead of by value.

- **Material audit (1.141.0, no schema change)** — owner: *"make the same for materials, on different page — call the page material audit"*. The count screen is now TWO pages over one implementation: `viewCounts(query, mode)` in `counts.js`, registered as `viewCounts` (mode products, route `#/counts`, nav "Stock count") and `viewMaterialAudit` (mode materials, route **`#/material-audit`** — NOT `#/audit`, which is the Activity log; nav "Material audit", icon beaker). **Products** are counted at the workshop or a sale location (place picker); the products/materials/all switch is GONE. **Materials** are counted at the workshop only: no place picker, `/locations` is not even fetched, the sheet is `scope=materials&location_id=0`. Each page asks `GET /counts?role=products|materials` (`open_list()` filters on the shaped `for_sale`); the Overview card and the item page still see every count. A location's sheet searched from the products page finds products only (`sheet()` passes `for_sale` by scope). The reconcile screen's back link and the nav highlight follow the count's item: `V.setCountSide('product'|'material')` in core (like `setItemSide`), and `activeKey('/count/N')` reads it. Strings: Material audit → **Ревизия на материалите**. Gates: `countsui.js` 99, `counts.php` 116; the sweep is now 57 faults, all caught.

- **Stock count → gap → reconcile (1.140.0, DB 0.45.0)** — owner: *"I have made 30 candles and now I counted 10 remaining, I need the system to show me that so I can go and produce product until the difference is cleared or I can go to the till and log the sales."* **Owner's decisions:** clearing is **"I confirm them"** (nothing clears by itself); places are **the workshop and sale locations** (events keep their closing count); entry is **both** a Count button on every item page and a Stock count screen. **Why confirmation, not automatic:** till sales, production runs and location transfers are always stamped *now* and nothing computes stock-as-at-a-date, so the system cannot tell a forgotten sale rung up after the count from a new one — the owner does.
  **Model (`includes/class-counts.php`, `VGC_SM_Counts`):** a count is ONE item at ONE place (`location_id` 0 = workshop → `movements`; otherwise `location_ledger`). Tables `vgc_sm_counts` (item_id, location_id, counted_qty, book_qty [snapshot, for the record], **since_id** [watermark], note, user_id, counted_at — **no status column**) and `vgc_sm_count_claims` (count_id, row_id, qty; UNIQUE(count_id,row_id)). Everything else is DERIVED on every read: **book** = SUM of the place's ledger rows with `id <= since_id` (LIVE — editing a pre-count entry moves it; the header says so); **gap** = counted − book (negative = missing); **entries** = rows with `id > since_id` for that item at that place (`entries()` is the ONE definition): reversed rows (`reversed_at`) dropped, and a reversal whose target is also after the watermark dropped (the pair cancels); a reversal of a PRE-count row stays, flagged `cancels`; location `sale` rows are grouped per receipt (`ref_id`), net of refunds, and a fully refunded receipt vanishes; workshop `production_run` rows are grouped per run; the count's own write-off is excluded. **claims** are worth `sign × min(|claimed|, |entry now|)` and 0 if the entry is gone or turned the other way — so voiding a run, refunding/deleting a sale, or undoing an entry REOPENS the gap by itself (`dropped` counts them). **written** = ledger rows with `ref_type='count', ref_id=<count>` and `reversed_at IS NULL`. **remaining** = gap − claimed − written. **state** = open / matched / superseded (a later count of the same item+place exists; only the latest can be claimed, written off or recounted from).
  **Watermark by ROW ID, never by date:** backfilled hand entries are stamped `Y-m-d 23:59:59` (`trait-items.php entry_datetime`), so a date comparison would misfile entries on the day of the count. A backfill typed after the count but dated before it has `id > since_id`, appears, is flagged `dated_before` and is suggested FIRST. The client sends the watermark from when the sheet/page LOADED (`since_id`, clamped to the current max), so anything rung up while counting shows on the list to confirm. `summaries()` batches book (one JOIN per ledger), write-offs and claims; only counts with claims read `entries()`.
  **Claims (`claim()`, PUT replaces the set):** each row must be a current entry, same sign as it, `|qty| ≤ |entry|`; entries AGAINST the gap are taken in full first (they widen it), then same-sign entries oldest first, each **capped so the remaining never crosses zero** (reply lists `capped`). `suggest()` (pure): unclaimed entries in the gap's direction, `dated_before` first, then by id, the last one partial. **Write-off (`settle()`):** workshop missing → `scrap` movement (shows under Reports → Scrapped), extra → `correct`; location missing → `Locations::write_off(…'shrink')`, extra → new **`found`** ledger type via `Locations::record_found()`; note "Count #N — not found/found". A workshop write-off undone in Fix mode reopens the count; location ledger rows cannot be undone — count again. `discard()` refused while a write-off stands. `Repository::delete_item()` calls `Counts::delete_for_item()`. Refused: services, negative/blank/non-numeric counts, the same item twice on a sheet, events, inactive/unknown places — one bad line refuses the whole sheet.
  **REST (`rest/trait-counts.php`, all operator-level like Scrap/Set qty):** `GET /counts?state=open|all&item_id&location_id` (+ `places` with books per place and `since` watermarks when item_id given), `GET /counts/sheet?location_id&scope=products|materials|all&category_id&search&offset` (workshop: `list_items` with new arg **`no_services`**, 100 per page; location: `ABS(qty)>0` rows incl. negatives + search adds unknown items at 0), `POST /counts`, `GET /counts/{id}` (detail: count, entries with claimed/suggested/label/href, history, since, item kind, `till_location_id` = the location or the primary), `PUT /counts/{id}/claims`, `POST /counts/{id}/settle`, `DELETE /counts/{id}`. `/dashboard` gains **`stock_counts: {open, rows}`** for operators — NOT `counts`, which already carries `low_total`.
  **Screens (`app/js/counts.js` + `tpl/counts.tpl.js`, V.tpl.counts):** `#/counts` (nav Stock → Stock count): place picker (workshop + active non-event locations), products/materials filter, the sheet typed IN PLACE (never redrawn; typed numbers survive filters and paging; empty box = not counted, 0 = a count; on a phone the Difference column is replaced by a line under the name), sticky save bar above the tab bar, summary after saving, open gaps list. `#/count/{id}`: tiles, the since-list as stacked rows with ticks + "how many", live preview ("after this: …", warns when the server will cut), **These explain it**, shortcuts by direction and kind (missing product → **Log sales at the till** [`?vgc_sm_loc=`, new tab]; missing material at the workshop → Log use `#/item/N?act=use`; extra made → `#/produce?item=N`; extra bought → `?act=receive`; extra at a location, managers → the location), Write off the rest / Add the rest to the books (armConfirm), Count again, Discard. Item page: **Count** button (between Scrap and Set qty; not for services; operators only — a viewer never requests `/counts`), `item-count` form via `V.screens.openCountForm`, `item-counts` card, `viewItem` honours **`?act=count|receive|use`**. Overview: `home-counts` card. **All count writes go through `rawFetch`, never the offline queue** — a count is true at the moment it is taken. The sheet's unsaved numbers are guarded through `V.onItemLink` (first tap on a product link warns, a second within 8 s goes).
  **i18n:** ~115 BG strings. 'Count' → **Брой** on purpose: `translateTree` translates any matching text node, and Help uses "Count" as the unit dimension; Брой is both the noun and the imperative. Audit-log sentences with placeholders stay English, as elsewhere.
  **Known and accepted:** a sale voided at the primary counter puts its units on the counter's books, not the workshop's (unchanged behaviour), so a claimed workshop draw stays claimed; operators can write off at a location (matches Scrap/Set qty; could be made manager-only). Set qty stays as the blunt tool.
  **Gates:** `counts.php` (111, SQLite, the REAL class-counts/repository/locations/corrections; the harness adds the `ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` translation locally), `countsui.js` (83, jsdom, the REAL counts.js/tpl + catalogue.js/tpl + core atoms and link guard), **47 reinstated faults, all caught** (three first survived and each became a test: own write-off listed, sign-flipped entry, a box typed then emptied). Staging probe `stage_counts.php`: real checkout at a temporary primary counter, real Production::run + void_run, real refunds, all routes, a crash-safe `register_shutdown_function` tidy — **write probes that way**: an early probe died mid-way and left a temporary primary counter on staging until a cleanup script removed it. Also: a `WP_REST_Request` route must not carry a `?query` — pass it with `set_query_params()`, or the request 404s. Whole-app run from a staging dump at 1280/390: 41 checks, clean.

- **Order lines link to their products (1.139.0, no schema change)** — owner, in Dev mode: *"In `order-lines`: the product names are not links to the products"*. They had been left plain in 1.137.0 because an order is ALWAYS an editing form and nothing kept unsaved edits. They are now `itemLink(l.name, l.item_id)` in `orders.tpl.js` `lineRow()` (normal lines and display pieces alike), made safe by the second `V.onItemLink` hook, in `orders.js` `bind()`. **The order's "unsaved" test:** `headOut()` (reads the form via `syncForm()`, returns the save header) and `lineOut(l)` (one save line) are now the ONLY definition of what a save writes. `save()` builds its body from them, and `unsaved()` compares `JSON.stringify(headOut())` and `JSON.stringify(lines.map(lineOut))` against `saved = { head, lines }`. **Do not build a save body any other way, or the check and the save will disagree.** `saved` is taken (a) right after the FIRST `render()`, so whatever the form turns a value into (`30` → `"30"`) is not mistaken for an edit; (b) in `save()`, with the header read BEFORE the request (text typed while it is out stays unsaved) and the lines read AFTER adopting `res.lines` (new lines get ids); (c) `saved.lines` only, after a sale report is saved or deleted, because those hand back new lines without saving the header. **The hook:** nothing unsaved → the link navigates; unsaved → `preventDefault`, `save()`, then toast and `go(href)`; failed → a warning toast and you stay. For a NEW order, after the save it calls `history.replaceState(null, '', '#/order/' + id)` before leaving, so Back lands on the saved order rather than a blank `#/order/new` that invites a duplicate. This matches what Add payment and Record sale already did: save the order first, then act. The save moves stock exactly as the Save button would; that is the accepted meaning of "leaving saves".
  **Core guard change:** a click with **Ctrl, Cmd or Shift** is NOT handed to `V.onItemLink` (and is still kept off the row): the product opens in a new tab or window, this page is untouched, so there is nothing to save. Middle-click never reaches a `click` listener at all. This applies to the closing count too. Also fixed: 1.137.0 had inserted the guard in the middle of the sign-out listener's comment, separating that comment from its code.
  **Still unlinked:** purchase lines, stock-note lines, recipe component/substitute rows, custom-order materials, `settleAddRows`, the push list, and sale-cost/refund inputs. Each would need its own unsaved-changes test before it could be linked; copy the order's `headOut`/`lineOut`/`saved` shape.
  Gate: `orderlinks.js` (40, new) runs the REAL `orders.js`, `orders.tpl.js`, core guard and `screen()` in jsdom with realistic `shape_order()` data. It covers: untouched; an edited line, note, transport cost or date; a failed save followed by a retry; Ctrl/Cmd/Shift clicks; a new order (POST, `replaceState`, history length unchanged); a deleted sale report, with and without an unsaved header; the hook dropped by the next screen; and saved lines keeping their ids. `itemlinks.js` (177) now treats order lines as linked and checks the modifier pass-through. **55 reinstated faults, all caught.** Two earlier drafts of the unsaved check (number normalisation, excluding line ids) could not be made to fail against real server shapes, so they were removed rather than kept. The whole-app run on a temporary staging order passed 41 checks at 1280 and 390 px.

- **Product-link look, and the closing count (1.138.0, no schema change)** — owner, after 1.137.0 shipped: *"I still don't see the links"*, on **a sale location's stock list** and **the closing count**; then, asked how a link should look: *"I dont want visual distinction, just active link - might change color on hover, but remove the underline"*. **THE OWNER'S DECISION, binding on future work: a product name looks exactly like the text around it at rest. No underline, no colour, no weight change.** Under the pointer (and on keyboard focus) it turns `--vgc-brand` with a hand cursor; printed, it is ink. Do not reintroduce an at-rest affordance on the grounds that phones have no hover — that argument was made in 1.137.0 and the owner overruled it.
  **What "I still don't see the links" actually was**, found by running the WHOLE app, not a template: the stock-list names WERE anchors (faint underline, easy to miss); the closing count was deliberately unlinked (1.137.0 carve-out); and the **services card's name was an anchor styled out of shape** — the older `.vgc-sm-list li a` / `.vgc-sm-list--plain a` rules (block, full width, padding, brand colour) out-rank a bare class, so the name became an orange block. Fixed with `.vgc-sm-list li a.vgc-sm-ilink` / `.vgc-sm-list--plain a.vgc-sm-ilink` overrides back to inline, inherited colour; the services row also gained the `vgc-sm-lrow` / `vgc-sm-grow` / `vgc-sm-ltitle` structure every other list row has.
  **The closing count is now linked, behind a save-before-leaving hook** — the first unsaved-changes protection in the app, and deliberately narrow. **`V.onItemLink(e, a)`**: the capture-phase guard in `core.js`, after `stopPropagation()`, hands every product-link click to this hook if a screen has set one. **`screen()` clears it** (`V.onItemLink = null;` first thing), so a hook never outlives the screen that set it. The event screen in `locations.js` sets it: **not dirty → returns, the link navigates as normal; dirty → `preventDefault()`, POSTs `settleCounts()` to `/locations/{id}/event/draft` (exactly what Save progress sends), and only on success sets the state to Saved, toasts, and `go(href)`; on failure it toasts and STAYS**, with the typed numbers still in the boxes. This is not the silent autosave the draft code rules out — it happens because the person chose to leave, and it says so. **Still unlinked (at 1.138.0; order lines followed in 1.139.0):** a product just added to the count (`settleAddRows`), the push list, and every other line being built — the hook covers the count's existing rows only. A general unsaved-changes guard remains out of scope; if one is built, `V.onItemLink` is the seam for links, but hash changes from the nav are not covered by it.
  **How the whole app is run locally (the lesson of this release):** a probe on staging reflects `VGC_SM_Frontend::render_app` as user 1 and dumps the page HTML plus the REST answers the screens ask for (for an event: create a temporary one, push stock, dump `/locations/{id}/inventory`, `/event`, `/event/settlement`, `/event/costs`, `/event/pnl`, then delete everything it made). A puppeteer runner serves that HTML from the API's own origin (so fetches are same-origin), rewrites WP-CLI's `http://` asset URLs to `https://` (or every asset is mixed-content blocked), serves `app/` from the local tree, and answers REST from the dump. It then hovers and clicks with a real mouse and reads computed style. Every template harness was green while the owner could not see a link; this is the check that found why. In Git Bash, `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` breaks a POSIX script path, so give node the Windows-form path. **A number input cannot be selected with a triple click** — clear it with Ctrl+A, Backspace, or the typed digit is appended.
  Gate: `itemlinks.js` (164) — the look (pointer, no underline, brand hover, list override, print ink), the guard handing clicks to `V.onItemLink` only for product links, and the event hook EXECUTED from `locations.js` with stubs (clean → goes; dirty → waits for the POST of the on-screen counts, then goes; failed → stays and warns). 32 reinstated faults, all red. The whole-app run: 39 checks at 1280 and 390 px, clean.

- **Product names as links, everywhere (1.137.0, no schema change)** — owner: *"On all locations I would like the product names to be links to the product page. Currently they are not."* **Half of it was worse than a missing feature:** ten names already WERE links to `#/item/N`, and looked exactly like plain text, because `app.css` styles every anchor in the app with `a { color: inherit; text-decoration: none; }`. That rule stays — the nav, tabs and back links depend on it.
  **THE CONVENTION, for every future screen:** print a product's name with **`itemLink(name, id)`**, never `esc(name)`. It returns `<a class="vgc-sm-ilink" href="#/item/N">`, and **plain escaped text when the id is not a positive integer** (`Number()`, never truthiness — `'0'` is truthy in JS). Inside an element that is ALREADY a link to the item — a whole-row `<a>` — use **`itemLabel(name)`**, a `<span class="vgc-sm-ilink">`: a second anchor would be nested, which the HTML parser silently splits apart. Both are atoms on `V` in `core.js`, and each template takes them in its header (`var itemLink = V.itemLink;`) — **a template that forgets throws at render**, and so does any test stub that lacks them.
  **[SUPERSEDED by 1.138.0 — the owner rejected the underline; see the entry above]** `.vgc-sm-ilink` carried a permanent quiet underline (`--vgc-ink-3`, brand on hover/focus), not hover-only: a phone has no hover and "you cannot tell it is tappable" was the complaint. `a:hover .vgc-sm-ilink` lights the name when a whole-row link is hovered. Printed, it is plain text.
  **One capture-phase document listener** stops a click on `a.vgc-sm-ilink` propagating, so a link inside a `tr[data-open]` row does not ALSO fire the row. Capture, not bubble, is load-bearing: rows bind per element, so a bubbling document listener runs after them. `stopPropagation` does not cancel the link's own navigation. Desktop tables therefore gained a real anchor, which also opens in a new tab and is reachable by keyboard — the `data-open` rows never were.
  **Carve-outs, by decision — do not "finish the job" here:** the till (`pos.tpl.js` — every tile is a `<button>` that SELLS; an anchor inside a button is invalid and a navigating tap is a mis-ring); every picker (`.vgc-sm-result` — a tap selects, and it is a `<button>` too); **every line still being edited** (order, purchase and stock-note lines, recipe component and substitute rows, custom-order materials, the closing count *(linked since 1.138.0 — see above)*, the push list, sale-cost and refund inputs) — **the app has NO unsaved-changes guard anywhere**, so leaving loses the typing; print sheets and labels; an item's own page title. `itemlinks.js` asserts these stay unlinked by name, so a future sweep goes red rather than breaking a sale.
  Sites with no id in scope stay plain (the deletion-blocked list, the pull-from-shop list, some print sheets). No server change was needed: every read-only site already carried `item_id` or `id`.
  Gate: `itemlinks.js` (144) uses the REAL atoms lifted from `core.js`, EXECUTES the capture guard against a real row, and reads `app.css` to fail if the underline goes. 22 reinstated faults, all red. Nine older harness stubs gained the two atoms.

- **Finding the standard-cost picker (1.136.0, no schema change)** — owner-reported: *"other costs does not allow me to pick standard cost already created."* Everything worked: the endpoint returned the types, the deployed JS had the picker, and `costtypesui.js` asserted the row offered them. The picker was simply unreachable — it lived ON a cost line, so the only route to it was to add a line and then notice a dropdown reading "One-off". **A control nobody can find does not exist, and every test was green because each checked the control existed, never that anything led to it.**
  The Other costs card now carries two buttons, `#r-addstd` (primary) and `#r-addextra`: the choice is offered where the choice is made. "Add a standard cost" pushes a row already pointing at `costTypes[0]` with `quantity: 1`, so it lands priced and explains itself; the row picker then changes which. `recipePage` takes `hasTypes` — with no catalogue the standard button is not drawn at all (a picker over nothing is a dead end) and the card points at `#/costtypes` instead. Card copy also stopped saying the rate is set "in Settings", which was the wrong screen.
  The lesson for this codebase, alongside "execute, don't grep": **a UI guard should assert the PATH to a control, not just its presence.** Gate: `costtypesui.js` section 1b, 8 reinstated faults, all red.

- **Business overheads (1.135.0)** — DB **0.44.0**, guard `vgc_sm_expenses_v1`. Owner: *"The business has costs such as power, water, rent, advertisement and other miscellaneous cost. I need to have a way to track them and see how much they add to the cost of the products."* New table `expenses`; `categories` gains `in_product_cost TINYINT`; `production_runs` finally gets a `created_at` index (it never had one and every period report filters on it).
  **Report only, by decision.** Nothing here writes to a recipe, an item cost, a margin or a sale. The owner chose this over auto-applying rates, and over per-labour-hour absorption: *"All the miscellaneous costs come to a single value per product — they should be independent of product runs, as all product runs use relatively the same amount of power or water."* So the figure is `overhead ÷ units made`, one number for every product.
  **Two row kinds in one table, told apart by a fact**, as everywhere else here: `due_day IS NOT NULL` is a recurring TEMPLATE — a description of a bill — and every spend query carries `due_day IS NULL`. `template_id` on a real row says which template it was recorded from, which is how "has September's rent been entered" has an answer. **No cron:** `due_in_month()` is a question, `record_due()` is the answer somebody presses, because generating money nobody has spent while nobody is looking is the one thing this must never do.
  **The whole design problem is double-counting**, not arithmetic. Three exclusions, each stated on screen: a category flagged `in_product_cost` (wages — labour is already in every recipe via the hourly rate); event costs (already in that event's own P&L, `class-events.php:1054-1065`) reported beside the pool and never in it; materials (Purchases). Get any of them wrong and the figure looks authoritative and is wrong.
  **`per_unit` is null, never 0, with nothing produced** — the precedent is `VGC_SM_Events::pct()`, and "0.00 a unit" would read as overheads costing nothing. The denominator is the labour report's exact rule, `good_qty ?? qty` over completed runs.
  **Two real date bugs were found and fixed here**, both the memory's "never mix a local parse with a UTC format": `gmdate('t', strtotime('2027-02-01'))` returned **31** in Europe/Berlin (it had landed in January), and `gmdate('Y-m-01', strtotime($first.' +1 month'))` returned the SAME month — which made the already-recorded window empty, so every recurring bill would have nagged forever. Month arithmetic now runs on integers: `month_after()` and `days_in_month()` in `class-expenses.php`. A harness variable named `$now` had also silently clobbered the test clock (at file scope `$now` IS `$GLOBALS['now']`), and two assertions had been passing because a broken clock cancelled a broken window; `expenses.php` now reads the clock through `clock()`, which fails loudly if anything overwrites it.
  Reuse rather than rebuild: `categories` gained the scope `expense` (gated in exactly two places — `clean_scope()` and `refreshCategories()`), and the VAT two-way binding moved from `locations.tpl.js` into core as `grossFromNet`/`netFromGross`/`bindVatPair`, shared by event costs and expenses. `primaryui.js` still EXECUTES it, now sliced out of core.
  Screens: `#/expenses` (manager) and a **Overheads** tab in Reports beside Labour, manager for the same reason. REST: `GET|POST /expenses`, `POST|DELETE /expenses/{id}`, `POST /expenses/record-due` (registered BEFORE `/expenses/{id}` or the literal never matches), `GET /reports/overheads`.
  Housekeeping done in the same pass: `uninstall.php` and `class-reset.php` were missing `event_costs`, `cost_types`, `sale_backlog`, `location_services`, `bom_item_alts` and the recipe-template tables. Reset now also takes an optional `where` per spec — `expenses` is the first table holding both a transaction and a definition, and a recurring bill must survive "clear test data" the way a category does.
  Gates: `expenses.php` (51, SQLite), `overheads.php` (35, SQLite + the real handler lifted from its trait), `overheadsui.js` (94, jsdom + the executed payload builder), `scratchpad/stage_overheads.php` (48, the real MySQL 8 box, including a temporary REAL operator — the `vgc_sm_operator` ROLE does not exist on staging, so granting it grants nothing; access is a capability plus a meta level). 54 reinstated faults, all red.
  **Not done, by decision:** payroll, a company P&L, paying an expense (Purchases owns that), receipt photographs, and anything that writes an overhead into a recipe or a product cost.

- **Standard cost types (1.134.0)** — DB **0.43.0**, guard `vgc_sm_cost_types_v1`. Owner: *"for the recipes I need to standardise the non-material costs — like amortisation and such. Currently I need to type them in, but I can't track their use."* New table `cost_types` (name, `unit` = what one of it is, `rate`, `note`, `active`); `bom_extras` and `recipe_template_extras` each gain `cost_type_id BIGINT NULL` + `quantity DECIMAL(12,4) NULL`, indexed on the first.
  **The fact IS the mode**, as everywhere else here: `cost_type_id IS NOT NULL` means the line is priced from the catalogue (`amount = quantity × rate`, name copied from the type); NULL means it is the free-text one-off it always was. Nothing is migrated — adoption is per line, with the recipe open.
  **It is labour's mechanism, deliberately.** `hourly_labour_lines()`/`preview_labour_rate()`/`apply_labour_rate()` have exact siblings in `typed_extra_lines()`/`preview_cost_type_rate()`/`apply_cost_type_rate()`, same row shape and same per-finished-item division (`'item'` basis ÷ 1, `'run'` basis ÷ yield), so the two answers never look different. Apply is one transaction: rate on the type, then every line priced from it, then `forget_maps()` + `Costing::flush()`. It does NOT touch `avg_cost` or a run snapshot. **Labour may never carry a type** — two ways of arriving at one figure would fight — and `save_bom()` drops it if asked.
  **Two rules that are not obvious.** (1) A typed line with an amount of 0 is KEPT (`if ( '' === $label || ( $amount <= 0 && ! $type_id ) )`): naming the cost is a fact about the recipe, so pricing the type later reaches it without anyone reopening a recipe. (2) A `quantity` of NULL means ONE of the thing, not none — reading it as 0 would silently zero every older line at the next rate change.
  **Deleting is refused while recipes use it** (the lines would keep their money and label but no rate change would ever find them again); `active = 0` retires it instead — gone from the picker, every existing recipe untouched, and still re-ratable. A **rename propagates to `bom_extras.label`**, because the label is the copy history reads.
  **The round trip is the whole risk, and it is 1.112.0's exactly.** `get_recipe()` must hand back `cost_type_id` and `quantity` or opening a recipe and pressing Save severs it from the catalogue; the same is true of all four recipe-template paths (`save()`, `extras()` shaper, `from_recipe()`, `preview()`'s scaling, and `apply_to()`'s `$keep_ex`). The template shaper was in fact caught dropping them by `tplcosttypes.php` before release. **Scaling a template to a bigger yield scales the QUANTITY**, not the money — the money then follows the rate.
  Client: `extraTypeSelect()`/`extraRow(e, idx, {types})` in `catalogue.tpl.js` (a retired type stays listed while a line uses it, or saving would drop it), `hasType()`/`extraAmount()` in `catalogue.js` (`Number(id) > 0`, never truthiness — `'0'` is truthy in JS and falsy in PHP, which has cost this project a release twice). Screen `#/costtypes` (`costTypesPage`, `costTypeRatePreview`) at operator; re-rating is manager, like the hourly rate. REST: `GET|POST /cost-types`, `POST|DELETE /cost-types/{id}`, `POST /cost-types/{id}/rate/preview`, `POST /cost-types/{id}/rate`. The rate is NOT writable through the plain update — it only moves through preview-then-apply.
  Gates: `costtypes.php` (68, SQLite), `tplcosttypes.php` (21, SQLite), `costtypesui.js` (81, jsdom + the executed payload builder), `scratchpad/stage_costtypes.php` (36, the real MySQL 8 box and the real REST round trip). 39 reinstated faults, all red. Phone layout: `[data-devid="recipe-othercosts"]` wraps its control row (five controls where there were three ran a whole select off the card) and the catalogue list gives the name its own line under 560px.
  **Not done, by decision:** per-run tracking of which standard cost was consumed (phase 2 — `extras_for_run()` already computes the breakdown and throws it away; an Overheads report beside Labour needs a `run_extras` table first), folding labour into the catalogue, and back-filling history.

- **The product page says WHICH cost it is showing (1.133.0, no schema change)** — owner-reported: the recipe screen said € 2.55 and the product page € 3.92 for the same candle, “they differ like a lot”.
  **Neither number was wrong.** The ladder is `manual > subcontractor > produced-average > recipe > typed/derived`, and its own comment says why: *“a recipe says what a thing SHOULD cost; once it has actually been made, what it really cost is the better answer”*. So the product page showed the 29 Aug run (€ 3.92) and the recipe screen today’s roll-up (€ 2.55). The owner’s own figures confirmed it: labour + overheads matched to the penny on both sides (1.10/unit) and the whole gap was materials — 2.825 against 1.45.
  **The defect was that nothing said which was which**, and in Bulgarian the two labels were the same words: `'Recipe unit cost' => 'Себестойност на единица'` beside `'Unit cost' => 'Себестойност'`. The English UI drew a distinction the translation dropped. **A translation can delete a distinction the interface depends on** — worth remembering for the other 500 untranslated strings.
  `shape_item()` computed `$info['source']` and shipped only a boolean `cost_computed`, so the page could say “calculated” but never “from the last run”. It now ships `cost_source`, and `GET /items/{id}` adds `cost_recipe` from the new `VGC_SM_Costing::recipe_unit_cost()` — the roll-up, reachable whatever the ladder decided. **On the single item only**: it walks the recipe tree, and a 200-row list would walk 200 of them for a line that page never draws. A probe asserts the list does NOT carry it.
  The headline stays what it really cost (the owner chose this): **no figure anywhere in the app moved**. `Recost` on a run remains the lever when a past run is out of date, and it already recomputes from what actually moved at today’s prices.
  **A stale mirror, six harnesses deep.** Chasing where to put the test found `cattpl.js` — a copy of `catalogue.tpl.js` under a name the mirror sync cannot match (the 1.130.0 lesson again, wider): **237 lines behind**, missing whole functions added since (`labourRow`, `templatePicker`, `templateApply`, `soldCustomHint`…), and loaded by six harness pages. Repointed at the real mirror — all six still pass, so they were verifying current behaviour against an old copy by luck — and the stale file is gone. **Any file in the test directory whose basename does not exist in the plugin tree is a copy nothing keeps current.**
  23 new checks; ten reinstated faults verified red; the reported shape reproduced end to end on staging — a product made once, its material then made cheaper, so the two figures genuinely diverge.
- **A leftover with no price is not a sale at nothing (1.132.0, no schema change)** — owner-reported: a closing count showing 3 left over and “€ 0.00”, still 0 after the price was set and the page reloaded.
  **The reported cause was not the cause.** The sheet derives `price_gross` from a live join on the items row, and a staging probe proved it: set the price through the item form’s own route and a fresh read goes from 0 to 14.40. The service worker explicitly skips `/wp-json/`, and `get()` has no cache. That row simply had no price stored. **The fix is therefore not “make it refresh” — it is to stop a missing price from looking like a real nought**, because the screen gave no way to tell.
  **`has_price` on the sheet row.** A gross of 0 cannot say whether nobody has priced the product or somebody priced it at nothing, and only one of those is a mistake waiting to be invoiced. `price_net` distinguishes them — NULL against 0 — so the row carries the fact.
  **What the bug would have cost:** settling that sheet wrote a real sale of 3 units at zero. Stock leaves, no takings are recorded, and the P&L reads as though the weekend gave them away. `settle()` now refuses while anything LEFT OVER has no price and none was typed, naming the product, and refuses the whole sheet before writing — the same shape as the overcount refusal, for the same reason. Bringing an unpriced product all the way home is untouched: only what is sold needs a price.
  **A price can be typed on the row.** `settle()` has always accepted `price` per row; the screen never offered one. The box appears only where there is no price to fall back on, so the sheet is unchanged for every other row — which matters on a table that already squeezed eight columns at 900px in 1.127.0. It is sent as TEXT: parseFloat would make a deliberate nought indistinguishable from an empty box, which is the distinction the whole change is about.
  **Back-compatible by construction:** `settleExtra(extra, r)` with a row that has never heard of `has_price` behaves exactly as before, so nothing else on the sheet moved.
  **Two mutation traps, both mine, both the same one:** `'0'` is a truthy string in JavaScript, so `price ? … : null` and `c.value ? …` both leave a typed nought working and score the guard GREEN. The mutation that actually bites is `parseFloat(price) || null`. **Second time this exact trap has produced a toothless mutant** (see 1.129.0).
  **And one self-inflicted lesson worth more than the feature.** The test patch that added this section used `console.log('
' + pass + …)` as its anchor and replaced it — deleting the summary line. `run-all.js` scores a harness with no parseable summary as PASSING WITH ZERO CHECKS, so the suite went from 2483 checks to 2140, still “64/64 passed”. I misread the missing line as Node truncating stdout on `process.exit()` and changed thirteen unrelated harnesses before spotting the real cause; those were reverted. **A harness that reports nothing must not read as a pass** — `run-all.js` now fails one whose summary it cannot find, which is how this would have been caught in the first minute.
  33 new checks; sixteen reinstated faults verified red; the whole path proved on staging, including the refusal, the typed price reaching the sale line, and the takings that follow from it.
- **A sold custom piece leaves the product list and the tills (1.131.0, DB 0.42.0)** — owner-reported: “once custom products are sold, they will not be made again, so they should not appear in the product list and the till. Make a new page that will hold them.”
  **A flag on the item, not the Custom category.** `items.is_custom` (added by `Install::ensure_custom_column()`, guard `vgc_sm_custom_flag_v1`, backfilled once from the Custom category). “Made once, to order” is a fact about how the item came to exist; a category is a filing choice anybody can change, and hiding stock from a till on the strength of one would be a trap nobody could see. `sanitize_item()` accepts it so `custom_build()` can set it; the REST item-form whitelist leaves it out on purpose, and a check insists on that.
  **“Sold” is derived, never stored:** `stock_qty + COALESCE( SUM(location_stock.qty), 0 ) <= 0.0001` — company inventory, the same total the rest of the system uses. A piece on a stall’s table is NOT sold, and hiding it there would be a till that cannot sell what is in front of it. Because it is worked out on every read, a return or a correction puts a piece straight back with nothing to reset.
  **One fragment, three callers** — `VGC_SM_Repository::sold_custom_sql( $q )`, used by `list_items()` and by BOTH till catalogues (the primary counter’s own query and `shelf_plus_catalogue()`’s off-shelf tail, which a stall’s till can reach by search). Two details that had to be right: the subquery is aliased `cs` because the primary till already calls location stock `ls`, and every column is **qualified**, since an unqualified `id` inside a correlated subquery is one schema change away from binding to the wrong table.
  **In SQL, not after it.** The product list pages server-side, so filtering the rows after they arrive would hand back short pages. A check asks for a page of two and insists on getting two.
  **Scope is deliberate:** only the product list and the tills ask. Produce, orders and recipe pickers still show a sold piece, so “make it again” is never a dead end — and `hide_sold_custom` is opt-in, so nothing else changed behaviour by accident.
  **Hidden is not gone.** A search on the product list that would also have matched sold pieces says how many and links to them, carrying the search term. Silently dropping search results is how “it is not there” gets read as “it was deleted”.
  **The page** (`GET /custom-products`, `Production::custom_products()`): three queries whatever the number of pieces — the pieces, their completed runs (an undone run is `void`), their location stock. Status uses the SAME threshold as the SQL, so the page and the tills cannot disagree. Rows go through `shape_item()`, so a viewer gets no cost — and with no cost the row draws no margin rather than one from a nought.
  **A harness that runs the real SQL.** The suite’s fakes answer queries by pattern, which is the wrong tool for a rule that lives in a WHERE clause: a fake that applies the rule itself passes whatever the query says. `customhide.php` hands the real `list_items()` and the real fragment to **SQLite** (pdo_sqlite is present locally) and lets a database decide. Its searches use SKUs on purpose — SQLite’s `LIKE` ignores case for ASCII only, so a Cyrillic name would test SQLite’s quirk rather than the code. `primary.php`’s fake was taught the rule too, but only to prove the clause is PRESENT in both till queries; what it MEANS is proven against the real engine, and on staging against MySQL.
  Twenty-one reinstated faults, all red, each by name. **Four wiring faults have no local harness that reaches them** — the REST handler passing `hide_sold_custom` through, the hidden-count, the page’s status field and the backfill — and the staging probe is their guard. Named here rather than left to look covered.
  62 new checks. Rendered in Chrome at three widths: no overflow, and the money column never overlaps the text.
- **Finished products in a custom order (1.130.0, no schema change, no server change)** — owner-reported: “when creating custom orders I need to be able to also use final products, not just materials”.
  **The restriction was the screen, never the server.** `custom_build()` has always taken any existing item as a component (a service since 1.99.0). `custom.js` fetched `/items?for_sale=0`, with a comment reasoning that the whole catalogue would bury what was wanted. The fix is the list, not the model.
  **A product is consumed one level down, and that is the point.** `run()` works from `direct_requirements()` — the recipe’s OWN lines — so two finished candles in a gift box come off the candle shelf. They are never exploded back into wax and wicks, which left the shelf when the candles were made; consuming those again would take the same wax twice. The commission reaches the candle’s cost through `unit_cost_net()` (the recipe roll-up), and the screen’s running total reads `cost_net` from `/items`, which `shape_item()` fills from the same `VGC_SM_Costing::unit_cost_info()` ladder — so the preview, the recipe and a sale’s frozen cost cannot disagree. Verified on staging against the real database, with a candle that had a real recipe and a real production run behind it.
  **One list, two headings, materials first.** A switch would hide products from anyone who did not know it was there; one undivided list would bury the materials a commission is mostly built from. `matResults()` splits on `for_sale` into *Materials* then *Finished products*, search narrows both, and a product wears the existing `.vgc-sm-tag--product` on its result and on its row. A heading never prints over an empty group.
  **Doubling the pool made the 500 cap reachable**, so `renderPicker()` now does what the recipe screen already did: a search over a pool that hit `POOL_MAX` goes to the server. Two consequences, each guarded: a slow answer to an earlier keystroke must not paint over a newer one (the box’s value is compared when the answer lands), and a pick is looked up in what was **shown**, not in the pool — a server result was never in the pool, and looking it up there made it unpickable. Services are filtered out of the server answer exactly as out of the pool.
  **The UI harness loaded mirrors the sync could not see.** `customui.html` loaded `customjs.js` and `customtpl.js` — copies named unlike their sources, so the mirror rule (a file here whose basename also exists in the plugin tree) never matched them. They matched 1.129.0 only because they had been copied by hand; nothing kept them current, and this release was the first change they missed — while the sync cheerfully reported refreshing `custom.tpl.js`, a *different* file. Replaced with `custom.js` and `custom.tpl.js`, which the rule now keeps current (mirrors 25 → 26); the stale pair is removed. **Lesson: a mirror under the wrong name is a test of whatever it last held.**
  **A mutant that passed by crashing.** “Finished products left out” scored red with no FAIL line: a `querySelector(…).textContent` threw, the timer chain died and the run printed nothing. Caught, but unreadable. The checks now read through null-safe `txt()` / `clickIf()`, and it fails with fifteen named checks. The loader also now waits for the page to report that it has finished rather than sleeping a fixed three seconds: the chain is longer now, and a fixed sleep is either slow on every run or a false “(no output)” on a busy one.
  **Found by rendering, and older than the release:** on a 390px phone each row’s *spoiled* input ran 55px off the right edge at 1.129.0 — `.vgc-sm-crow__ctl` is a flex row that never wraps. Fixed with a wrap **scoped to `[data-devid="custom-materials"]`**, because the produce screen shares the class and has a one-line layout harness of its own; `runproducerow` still passes. 0px at every width now.
  33 new checks; thirteen reinstated faults verified red, each by name. The server side is checked twice: in the harness, whose fake now derives `short` from the shelf the way the real `direct_requirements()` does (it always said 0, so no test could see a particular component run short), and on the real database.
- **Putting a product straight onto the closing count (1.129.0, no schema change)** — owner-reported: “I have an offline list of product I had at my last event and I don’t want to go through the motions of producing them one by one and then pushing them”.
  **A row on the sheet cannot exist without a transfer behind it.** `settlement()` is DERIVED from the location ledger, so a row conjured onto it with no `transfer_in` would be settled by pulling stock home that never left — inventing inventory out of nothing. So “add to the count” is a push, and the only question was where the form lives. **The feature is the form, not a new kind of record.**
  `Locations::push_many()` is a LOOP over `push()`, deliberately not a second implementation: every rule about what may be pushed, when a workshop may go negative and which two rows a transfer writes stays in one function. Proof it holds: the mutation “let a bulk load overdraw a market stall” **cannot be written against `push_many` at all** — `push()` re-gates `allow_short` on `is_event`, so the only mutation that breaks the claim is in `push()` itself, which the 1.122.0 guard already kills.
  **All or nothing, in one transaction.** A list half entered is worse than one refused: nothing on screen says where it stopped, and the next attempt double-counts whatever went through. On any refusal the whole batch rolls back and the error carries `item_id`, `item_name` and `line` — “only 3 available” with no hint of which of twenty products is a message nobody can act on.
  **`Events::add_to_count()`** composes `push_many( allow_short: true )` with `save_draft()`. `allow_short` is not a choice here: the route exists to record an event that is already over, and refusing because the workshop cannot account for goods that were demonstrably sold would make it useless exactly when it is needed. A `counted` figure on a line is **merged** into whatever is already parked, never replacing it — there may be an hour of counting on that sheet already.
  **Two boxes per row — *Were there* and *Came home*** — because that is what the paper list says, and one pass down it is the whole point. The came-home box starts **empty**, meaning “all of it” (the sheet’s own default); a nought typed in is a different statement and is sent. Prefilling nought would claim a total loss on every row.
  **A collision only this screen has:** the *Send stock here* card is on the same page and already listens for `data-push`, so the adder’s results carry `data-tookpick` and a check insists they carry no `data-push`. Two handlers racing over one click misbehaves only where both cards render.
  **The fake database was made transactional.** It answered `START TRANSACTION` / `ROLLBACK` with 1 and changed nothing, so **every “rolled back on failure” claim in the suite was untestable** — `settle()`’s three steps included, whose existing guard passed only because the failure it injects happens at step 1, before anything is written. A snapshot on START and a restore on ROLLBACK makes them real; the boundary is the database, since a real rollback cannot reach a stubbed service either.
  **Three of my own mutations were toothless and taught something each.** A stale `$have` that gave the same answer on that fixture (the fixture, 2 then 1 out of 3, could not tell the two apart — replaced with 4 then 4 out of 5). An `allow_short = true` that `push()` re-gates. And `c.value ? … : undefined` to “drop a typed nought” — **the string `'0'` is truthy in JS**, so the mutant behaved identically. A mutation that changes nothing scores the guard GREEN and is a statement about the mutation, not the code.
  Layout: rendered in Chrome at four widths before shipping. No page overflow anywhere; wide tables scroll inside their own `.vgc-sm-tablewrap`. The render caught the adder’s first column shredding “12 pcs more than the workshop has” down five one-word lines on a phone — fixed with the same `min-width` floor the closing count already gives its own first column.
  64 new checks, thirty-three reinstated faults verified red, and a staging probe that reads both halves of the transfer out of the real tables and checks they are stamped with each other.
- **Saving progress on a closing count (1.128.0, no schema change)** — owner-reported: “the current settle button is too finite when I have a large amount of products”. Thirty rows is an hour of unpacking, interrupted.
  **The fix is NOT to let `settle()` write in pieces.** It is all-or-nothing on purpose — a half-settled event has the stock home and the takings never recorded — so the resumable thing had to be something that is not a settlement at all. A draft touches **nothing**: no stock, no sale, no write-off, no backlog, no P&L, no audit entry. `Events::draft()` / `save_draft()` / `clear_draft()`, and the only thing that can ever act on one is a person pressing Settle.
  **One option per event, autoload OFF** (`vgc_sm_count_draft_{id}`), not a table. The sheet is read whole and written whole, so there is no per-item query for a table to serve, and the row only lives between the first box and the settlement. The autoload flag is the part worth guarding: a stall's half-finished count loaded on every request of every page for the rest of the summer is a bug that never shows up as a wrong number and never goes away. The fake option store **records the flag** so a test can insist on it, and the staging probe reads the `autoload` column of the real `wp_options`.
  **The stored shape IS the shape the boxes read** — `item_id => counted, missing, gifted` — so a draft travels from the option to the input values with no conversion anywhere. It lights up `settleRow`'s `o.counts` parameter, which existed for the harness and was unused in production. One shape is one thing to get wrong instead of three.
  **Each row also stores `here`**: what the books said when those numbers were typed, taken from the server's sheet and never from the browser's claim. Hours pass between parking a count and finishing it, and stock moves — a top-up from the van, a late sale on the till. The counted figures stay true; the leftover they imply does not. So a row whose stock moved is **flagged** (`is-moved`, "was 9 when you counted") and the arithmetic is redone against today. That hazard is the whole reason a resumable count is not free, and it costs one number per row.
  **A refused settlement must not also lose the count.** `clear_draft()` is called AFTER `COMMIT`, never before the validation — moving it to the top of `settle()` turns four checks red. And it is called with `$log = false` there: the count was acted on, not abandoned, and a second audit line saying work was thrown away would read as somebody's mistake.
  **Three things the screen must not do**, each guarded: saving must not redraw (the cursor would leave the box being typed in, which is the entire point); a sheet full of restored numbers must not look settled (its own banner tone — neither the green of done nor the red of wrong — and the words *“nothing is settled until you press Settle the count”*); and discarding must ask first, because it destroys an hour of counting.
  **Dropped, not refused:** a row no longer on the sheet is skipped. Refusing the whole save over one stale product would throw away the hour this feature exists to protect. `settle()` still refuses an unknown item, because that one writes. A row of noughts is **kept** — “none came home” is a statement somebody made, and it is not “not counted yet”.
  **Two guards that did not guard, found by the sweep and fixed.** One asserted `c[5] && c[6]` by key access, so a sheet that sent half of itself **threw** instead of failing — and a check that throws prints no message, which also meant the mutation runner (reading only stdout) scored the mutant GREEN. Now it compares the key set, and the runner reads stderr and the exit code. The other gap was structural: every UI check handed `settleCard` a draft directly, so the fetch could stop asking for one and all of them stayed green. Split into an executed half (`detailPage` carries it down to the boxes) and a read half (the one fetch line). **Lesson: when every test injects the data, nothing tests where the data comes from.**
  52 new checks, thirty-three reinstated faults verified red, and a staging probe that caught its own missing precondition — `close()` refuses while stock is still there, so “a settled event takes no more counting” was testing an open event against an open event's rules.
- **Given away at an event (1.127.0, no schema change)** — owner-reported: “we might have given away some items — they should be considered only at cost value but no revenue”. A third box on the closing count, beside *Came home* and *Missing*, so `sold here = here − counted − missing − gifted`. **Without it a giveaway fell into the leftover and was invoiced at full price**, inventing takings nobody ever received — the same class of error as the shrink column fixed in 1.123.0, in the opposite direction.
  **A new VALUE in `location_ledger.type`, not a new column** — `gift` joins `transfer_in|transfer_out|sale|sale_return|shrink`, so DB version stays 0.41.0. `Locations::write_off()` gained a whitelisted `$reason` (`shrink`|`gift`, anything else falls back to `shrink`): an unknown type would sit in the ledger as a quantity nothing knows how to read, and the on-hand sum would count it as neither.
  **A gift and a loss are kept apart on purpose.** Six candles handed to the organiser is a decision; six that vanished is a problem, and reading one as the other hides either the generosity or the theft behind the other. Both leave the location the same way and both come off the profit at cost — the difference is only ever in the telling, which is exactly why it must be told. One private reader, `Events::gone_at_cost()`, does `type IN ('shrink','gift') GROUP BY type, item_id` and values both, so there is one query and one valuation, not two that can drift.
  The P&L gains a **Given away** line at cost, captioned *“at cost, no takings”* — said out loud, because a line showing only a cost invites the question. `$net = $gross − $shrink_cost − $gift_cost − $spend`. The line is absent when nothing was given away.
  **A layout consequence worth remembering:** the eighth column broke the sheet at 900px. Fixed by shortening the heading (“Sold, not rung up” → “Sold here”), narrowing the inputs 82→72px and stacking the derived figure over its money — not by shrinking the font. Adding a column to a table that already fits is a layout change, not just a markup one.
  38 new checks and fifteen reinstated faults verified red — including gifts falling back into the leftover, a gift written as a loss, and one that cost nothing so that giving stock away looked free.
- **Typing either side of the VAT rate on an event cost (1.126.0, no schema change)** — owner-reported: “allow adding of VAT included price so I don’t need to calculate it every time”. A **With VAT** box beside the net one; filling either fills the other.
  **Net stays the stored truth**, exactly as the consignment note lines have done since 1.9.0 — two places in this app now let you type either side of a VAT rate and they behave identically. Only `amount_net` is sent; the server is untouched. The arithmetic lives in `grossFromNet()` / `netFromGross()` in the TEMPLATE module as pure functions, so the screen and the harness share one implementation rather than the harness describing what the screen does.
  Four decimals, matching the column: 100 with VAT works back to 83.3333 and forward to 100.00 again. Two decimals would not survive the round trip, and a mutation to `toFixed(2)` proves it. The box being typed in is never written back to — the cursor would jump and a half-typed “14” would become “14.0000” under the hand — and changing the RATE re-derives from whichever side was typed last, so it never rewrites the number just copied off a receipt.
  **The guard for the two-way fill is EXECUTED, not read.** The first version was a regex for `grossEl.value = T.grossFromNet(...)`, which appears in the input handler AND again in the rate handler — so deleting the input binding left it green. The block is now sliced out of `locations.js` and run against a real form in jsdom, with real `input` events. **Lesson: a source guard for a line that legitimately appears twice is a guard for neither occurrence.**
  19 new checks, eleven reinstated faults verified red.
- **VAT on an event cost (1.125.0, no schema change)** — owner-reported: “I am claiming VAT on the extra costs”. 1.124.0 stored `vat_rate` and `amount_gross` and **never asked for either**: the form had label, kind, date and net, so every cost went in at 0% and the “incl. VAT” line in the table could not appear through the UI. The column existed; the question was missing. **A field the schema carries but no screen asks for is a feature nobody has.**
  The form gains a rate box defaulting to 20, the same default a purchase line starts on, and it is **absent entirely** for a company that is not VAT registered — the server already forced 0 through `Sync::vat_rate()`, so a box sitting at nought was a question with one possible answer. The card header now shows what there is to reclaim, in green, **beside** the spend and never added to it: VAT you get back is not what the weekend cost you, and a mutation that adds it turns two checks red. The P&L is untouched and still entirely net, which is the correct basis when the VAT is reclaimed.
  10 new checks, seven reinstated faults verified red. One of those started green and was the MUTATION's fault, not the guard's: hiding the input with `hidden` still satisfies `querySelector`, so the mutation was sharpened to remove the control outright — which is the fault that could really happen.
- **Events, phase 3: what it cost, and whether it paid (1.124.0, DB 0.41.0)** — the last of the four asks. New table `vgc_sm_event_costs` and `Events::pnl()`.
  **Event costs are PERIOD costs and the tests exist to keep them that way.** Hotel, diesel, pitch fee, market tax — stored net with the VAT beside it, belonging to one event. **They must never reach `avg_cost`, `unit_cost`, an average or a recipe.** Spreading a hotel bill over the candles is a tempting idea that would silently change the margin on every product sold anywhere, for ever; it is the single most dangerous thing this feature could do, and a guard both locally and on staging asserts that adding a cost leaves every item's cost untouched.
  **The P&L is assembly, not arithmetic of its own.** Revenue and COGS come from `Sales::recent()` scoped to this location, and revenue is taken as **profit + cost** rather than re-derived from `net` minus refunds — the report's totals carry refunds GROSS while the net side lives on the rows, so subtracting the wrong one would overstate every refunded weekend. Both figures are already net of refunds, so their sum is exactly what the sales screen shows for the same location and the two cannot disagree. A staging check asserts that equality directly.
  **Scoped by LOCATION, never by the event's dates.** The settlement sale is written when the van is unpacked, which is AFTER `ends_on`; a date window would leave a whole weekend's unrecorded takings out of its own P&L. An event location exists for one event, so every sale on it belongs to it whenever it was recorded. Both a mutation and a staging case (a sale rung up after the event ended) pin this.
  Shrinkage is read off the ledger's own `shrink` entries — the same fact the closing count wrote, not a second tally — and valued at the item's CURRENT cost, because nothing snapshots a cost at write-off time. An estimate, and the card says so. Margins are **null, not 0**, when nothing sold: “0%” reads as breaking even. Manager throughout, matching the existing `$sees_profit` gate.
  **A near-miss worth the guard it earned:** a new `costCard()` silently replaced the sale-cost editor's function of the same name. Every template file is one IIFE, so a duplicate declaration does not error — the second wins and the only symptom is a crash three screens away. `usecheck.js` now scans every module for two top-level functions sharing a name, and it was verified by renaming one to collide. **Lesson: in a single-IIFE module, a duplicate name is a silent overwrite, and nothing else in the toolchain will say so.**
  77 new checks, twenty-four reinstated faults verified red, 38 more on staging against the real sales report.
- **Events, phase 2: the closing count (1.123.0, no schema change)** — the other half of “all sales saved in a backlog that can be resolved after the event has passed”. Unpack the van, say what came home, and the app works out the rest.
  **Two numbers typed, one derived.** Per item: `counted` and `missing`; then `sold here = here − counted − missing`. Ten went, four were rung up, two came home, one is gone → **three were sold and nobody wrote it down**. Compared against `here` (what the books say is LEFT), never against `took` — a mutation swapping those turns the whole sheet into nonsense and is one of the twenty reds. This is what makes a festival where nobody touched the till still produce its takings, which the P&L in phase 3 depends on entirely.
  **The leftover becomes an ORDINARY sale** through `Sales::checkout()`, so it reaches revenue, VAT and the per-line cost snapshot like any other. A special kind of takings would be a second thing every report had to know about, and the one that got forgotten.
  **Three consequences, one transaction, checked first.** The whole sheet is validated and REFUSED WHOLESALE before a single row is written — settling up to the bad row would leave an event half-closed with no way back. Executed, not asserted from source: a refused sale must leave the stock exactly where it was, and the harness makes `checkout()` fail to prove it.
  **`write_off()` and the `shrink` ledger type** are new: units leave the LOCATION and go nowhere. No workshop movement, because they are not back in the workshop — a dropped candle is genuinely gone and company inventory really is smaller. `push`/`pull` move stock, `sale` sells it, and this is the fourth thing that can happen to it. A mutation replacing it with `pull()` (quietly carrying the lost units home) turns five checks red.
  **`sold_here()`** reads `type IN ('sale','sale_return')` off the location ledger rather than the sales tables, because the question is what left THESE shelves and a voided sale put its units back on them. The harness fake had to learn that filter: its on-hand branch was answering the narrower query with the wider sum, counting the transfer IN as a sale and reporting **minus seven sold**. A fake that answers a narrower question with a wider one turns a broken feature green.
  Boxes default to `here` and `0`, so an untouched sheet sells nothing and loses nothing — defaulting the count to zero would open every settlement by claiming the whole van was lost. Manager-only, both reading and settling, because it writes revenue.
  66 new checks, twenty reinstated faults verified red, 32 more on staging where the sale, its VAT and its frozen cost were read back off the real tables.
- **Events, phase 1 (1.122.0, DB 0.40.0)** — asked for as “a short-lived sales location, like an event or a festival”, where stock can be pushed “regardless if they are on stock or not”. `sale_locations` gains `starts_on`, `ends_on`, `closed_at`. Costs and the per-event P&L are phases 2 and 3.
  **`kind = 'event'` is the rule, read in exactly one place.** `Locations::is_event()`. `kind` used to be decoration — a label and an accent colour — and one of its values now carries a permission, which is a real cost worth knowing.
