The alternative was a second `is_event` column that has to agree with `kind` and can stop agreeing. **The DATES deliberately do not decide it**: a permanent stall with opening times must not silently acquire the power to overdraw the workshop, and a test pins that. A source guard asserts nothing else in the file compares `kind` to `'event'` — one rule must not quietly become two.
  **Status is derived, never stored.** `event_state()` → upcoming / running / to_settle / settled, from the dates and `closed_at`. The day it starts and the day it ends both count as *running*; a stored state column would need re-deciding at every midnight. Compared in the SITE’s timezone, per the 1.116.0 lesson.
  **The over-push, and why a negative workshop is the right answer.** `push()` takes `allow_short`, honoured only when the destination is an event AND the caller asks — `$allow_short = $allow_short && self::is_event( $loc )`, one line, and the mutation that removes it is the single most important red in this release. Ten pushed against seven writes the FULL ten out of the workshop and the FULL ten onto the event: workshop −3, event +10, **company still 7**. Moving only seven would invent the rest of the van; inventing three would print stock. The invariant is asserted directly, both in the harness and on staging.
  **The debt is derived too.** `Events::workshop_debt()` is “how far below zero is the workshop on something this event took”, capped at what it took so a negative caused elsewhere is not laid at its door. Nothing is stored, so making the candles for another reason clears the question by itself. **`settle_debt()` produces into the workshop and delivers NOTHING** — the opposite of the backlog, whose whole job is to deliver, because here the units are already in the van. A mutation that adds the delivery turns eleven checks red.
  **Closing freezes the figures** and refuses while anything is outstanding, saying which: units still there, units unmade, entries on the make-and-send list. A closed event turns away sales (`Sales::checkout` calls `Locations::assert_open`) and pushes.
  **What staging found, again, and no local harness could:** the form sent the dates and `create_location` threw them away — its parameter whitelist decided which fields existed, and the two new ones were not on it. The service was right; the route between the form and it was not. Now guarded by reading that whitelist, and mutation-verified three ways. **Lesson: a new field is not wired until something has driven it through the ROUTE, not the service.**
  Two smaller traps caught while writing the guards: a redundant “is the workshop square” check that no mutation could make fail (deleted — `min()` already carries a square workshop through to a negative), and a needle containing `$req->get_param` inside a **double-quoted** PHP string, which PHP interpolates before `strpos` ever sees it, so the guard passed while asserting something else entirely.
  104 new checks, twenty-nine reinstated faults verified red, 40 more on staging through the real routes.
- **Plan mode: the same list, read forwards (1.121.0, no schema change)** — asked for as “I want to be able to add items as backlog and in the sale locations — this will serve as sort of a plan mode”. A location’s card becomes **To make and send** and grows an **Add**: pick a product, say how many, and it joins the list beside anything sold ahead of the books. One button settles either.
  **`sale_id IS NULL` is the mode**, deliberately, and for the same reason `lot_cost` not-null is the mode for lot pricing (1.114.0): a second column saying “this is a plan” could fall out of step with the fact it describes, and here the fact is definitional — a sale-caused entry ALWAYS carries the sale that caused it, so its absence is not an inference. `plan()` therefore goes through the ordinary `record()` with `$sale_id = 0`, and `shape()` derives `planned` from it. No schema change, no migration, nothing to keep in step.
  **A plan writes nothing to any ledger.** Nothing has moved and nothing has been sold, so the location’s count is untouched until it is settled. The staging probe stocks the workshop ON PURPOSE before checking this — against an empty workshop a stray transfer would have been refused anyway, and the test would pass with `plan()` pushing stock about. (A mutation caught exactly that: the guard was green because the fixture guaranteed the result. **A test whose fixture makes the answer inevitable is not a test.**)
  **Counted apart, and that is load-bearing.** `count_owed()` (sale-caused) and `count_planned()` sit beside `count_open()`, and `Locations::shape()` feeds `backlog_lines` from **count_owed** — because that field does two jobs: it says how much paperwork is owed AND it suppresses the “Count is off” flag on a negative it explains. A plan explains no negative. Letting one into that number would hide a real discrepancy behind an unrelated intention.
  **Only a plan may be taken back.** `unplan()` filters `sale_id IS NULL`. Erasing a sale-caused entry would leave its negative with nothing to account for it — turning a job somebody can do into a mystery nobody can. The screen only draws the × on rows with planned units, and the server refuses regardless.
  **Settling a mixed row does both halves in one trip**, which is the whole reason they share a list: sell 5 short and plan 3 more, and one production run of 8 squares the negative and leaves 3 on the shelf. The note written into the permanent record follows what the entries actually were — *Made for …* rather than *Logged after selling at …* — because a batch made to order for a stall was never sold ahead of anything.
  **Tone follows content:** the card only wears `--warn` when something really was sold ahead of the books. A list of things you intend to make is work, not a fault, and colouring it red teaches people that red here means nothing.
  89 new checks, twenty-one reinstated faults verified red, and 32 more on staging through the real routes. Two of those mutations found guards that did not guard: the fixture above, and a source check reading the PUSH search instead of the PLAN search — both regexes over a file, both fixed by scoping to the function. **Lesson, again: a source guard must name the function it means, not a string that happens to appear twice.**
- **The backlog at every location, declared per line (1.120.0, no schema change)** — asked for as “do the backlog ordering for all locations, but the items should be pushed manually; when adding item I should be able to check a box to say it will be sold as backlog”. The 1.118.0 primary counter bundled two behaviours: the whole catalogue on the till with no push, and selling ahead of the books. This release separates them and gives every location only the second.
  **The rule is one named function.** `Sales::may_go_short( $primary, $declared, $item_id )` — true at the primary counter always, true elsewhere only for an item the operator ticked. `$declared` is built from every line BEFORE any is priced, keyed by **item id**, because the same product can appear twice with different prices and it would be incoherent to allow the shortfall on one of those lines and refuse it on the other. The refusal now carries `can_backlog`, so the till can offer the tick instead of only saying no.
  **Nothing is walked across.** `cover_from_workshop()` stays primary-only — the bench is behind that counter, and a market stall is not. Away from it the shortfall is just `Locations::uncovered_here()`, read LIVE so a second line for the same item sees what the first one took, and clamped at zero on both sides so a shelf already negative does not make the next line owe less than it sells. Settling is unchanged and was already location-agnostic: log the run (or the delivery) and push those units to **that** location, which is bookkeeping catching up with a move that physically happened — the goods were at the stall, which is why they sold.
  **`sellable_catalogue()` gained a second shape.** `shelf_plus_catalogue()` returns an ordinary location’s shelf exactly as `inventory()` always did, then appends the rest of the for-sale catalogue with `qty` 0 and **`off_shelf` true**. The till hides those until somebody searches by name, then shows them under a divider. The wall must go on meaning “what is on the table”; a stall listing 240 products it has not got would be answering a different question. `NOT IN` the shelf ids, so nothing is listed twice.
  **The tick lives on the cart line**, appearing the moment the line passes what the shelf holds and clearing itself when the quantity comes back down — a tick left on a line that is no longer short would go to the server as a lie. `totals().blocked` shuts the charge button and makes it say what would open it; on a phone the dock says a line needs an answer, because the tick is inside the sale sheet. `onHand()` is unchanged away from the primary: the cap is gone from `addToCart`, not from the figure the wall shows.
  **What staging found, and no local harness could.** The first build asked the ITEM whether a shortfall was allowed and asked the LINE whether to record it. Two lines of one product, ticked once, both sold — and only the first line’s shortfall was written down. The stall went to **−5 owing 3**, and the two missing units read as an unexplained count problem: precisely the fault this feature exists to prevent, shipped inside the feature. Fixed by making both sites call `may_go_short()` and deleting the per-line flag entirely. **The harness could not see it because it does not run `checkout()`** (see the note at the top of `primary.php` — a fake deep enough to host sales, lines, payables and receipt numbering would be testing the fake); `stage_backlog.php` drives the real one against the real database. A source guard now pins the invariant — the refusal and the recording must ask the same question of the same thing, and no per-line flag may reappear — verified red three ways. **Lesson: one decision, two call sites, is a bug waiting for its second call site.**
  73 new checks in `primary.php` and `primaryui.js`, twenty reinstated faults confirmed red, and 29 more on staging.
- **Recipe templates (1.119.0, DB 0.39.0)** — asked for as “I have a lot of similar recipes and it is tedious to add everything manually, and I forget items”. Three tables (`vgc_sm_recipe_templates` + `_items` + `_extras`) and one service, `class-recipe-templates.php`. A template holds lines exactly as a recipe does — `component_item_id`, `quantity`, `unit`, `basis`, plus extras with their `hours` — and one thing a recipe cannot hold: a **placeholder**, a line with a `placeholder` label and no item. `plan()` refuses to apply while any placeholder is unanswered, which is what lets ONE template cover twelve scents instead of twelve near-identical templates.
  **The owner settled the design in two answers, and both are load-bearing.** *“Add to what is there”* — applying merges, skipping components the recipe already has and leaving hand-added lines alone; `replace` exists but must be chosen. *“A template is just a template — when a recipe is saved it is saved for the product as usual and not linked to the template”* — so there is **no template_id on a BOM anywhere**, by design. It is a rubber stamp: copy the lines, forget where they came from. That is why `delete()` needs no dependency check, and why editing a template can never reach back into a recipe already made. Do not add a link later without asking — it would turn a stamp into an inheritance, and a template edit into a silent edit of every product made from it. The cost is real and worth stating: fixing a template fixes what you make AFTER the fix.
  **Scaling.** `$scale = $want / $tpl_yield`, applied to `basis: 'run'` lines and NOT to `basis: 'item'` — a template for ten candles applied to twenty doubles the wax and still uses one wick each. Extras scale the same way. A typed quantity overrides the scale, and changing the yield **clears** the typed ones, because they were answers to a different batch size. In `add` mode the product keeps ITS yield: the template is contributing lines, not redefining what a batch is.
  **One implementation of the preview.** The client never computes what applying would do — it posts `dry_run: true` to the same endpoint and draws the answer. A preview computed separately from the write is a promise the app eventually breaks; a REST probe on staging asserts row-for-row that what the panel promised is what got written.
  **`from_recipe()` is how the first templates get made.** Capturing an existing recipe, not typing a new one — the recipes already exist, and retyping one to seed the rest is the chore being removed. It captures from what is SAVED, not from the boxes on screen. `save_bom()`’s signature is `(item, YIELD, components, extras)` — easy to get wrong, and the wrong order is silently plausible.
  **61 checks in `templates.php`, twelve reinstated faults verified red**, plus two staging probes against the real database: the class path (capture → placeholder → apply at a different yield → apply again → gut the template and prove the recipe is untouched) and the REST path (routes, JSON bodies, dry-run/apply agreement, a subscriber refused). Staging also re-taught an old lesson: labour **money** is derived from `hours × the site’s labour rate` inside `save_bom()`, so a test that asserts an amount is really asserting the rate — assert the hours, and derive the rest.
- **The till wall squashed every card to fit (1.118.1)** — owner-reported with a screenshot: a full catalogue on the primary counter rendered as stripes. `.vgc-sm-pos__items` left `grid-auto-rows` at its default `auto`, and Chrome sized every row identically to share the container height out — measured at **7px per card with 240 products, and scrollHeight == clientHeight**, so it compressed rather than overflowed. The card's own `overflow: hidden` is what permits that: it drops the grid item's automatic minimum size to zero, so nothing pushes back. Fixed with `grid-auto-rows: max-content` (tracks that are not flexible), plus `align-self: stretch` on the card and a two-line `min-height` on the name so cards match each other, and `white-space: nowrap` on the price with an ellipsis on the stock line — a wrapped foot made the row 17px taller and left the wall ragged. **Only reachable since 1.118.0**: before that a till listed the handful of items pushed to it, so no test had ever rendered a wall of 240. New `measuretill.js` (puppeteer, not in the roster) renders 8/40/120/240 products at four widths and asserts the card is the SAME SIZE as it is with a small catalogue, uniform across cards, still a usable touch target, and that the wall scrolls; `cssbalance.js` carries a cheap source guard for the between-times. Both verified red. **Lesson: a list whose length changed is a layout that was never tested.**
- **The primary counter, and the backlog it keeps (1.118.0, DB 0.38.0)** — one location may be marked `sale_locations.is_primary` (at most one; `Locations::set_primary()` clears the others in the same transaction). Its till lists the WHOLE for-sale catalogue whether or not anything was pushed to it, and stock is taken from the workshop **as it sells**.
  **Why not just let the till sell freely.** A sale only ever deducts from a LOCATION ledger; stock leaves the workshop when it is pushed. Selling at the primary without a transfer would leave the workshop believing it still holds units that walked out of the door, and the invariant **company inventory = workshop + locations** would drift apart silently — the L3 fault again, in the direction where stock is LOST unnoticed. So `Sales::cover_from_workshop()` calls the ordinary `push()` first: `to_location -n` out of the workshop, `transfer_in +n` onto the counter, then the ordinary `sale -n`. A primary sale is not a special sale — it is a normal one preceded by the transfer nobody had time to do. Stock already pushed is consumed FIRST, so pushing keeps its meaning.
  **Selling ahead of the books** (nothing at the counter, nothing at the workshop) is allowed HERE ONLY. The owner's framing settled the long-open P5 question: at the workshop counter "we have not got it" means "it is made and nobody wrote it down". The sale goes through, the location goes honestly negative, and **`vgc_sm_sale_backlog`** records why — one row per sale LINE (traceable), grouped by item in the UI (one trip to the bench settles them all). `Backlog::resolve_item()` runs the production for something we make or records the receipt for something we buy, pushes it to the counter, and closes the entries — all inside one transaction, so a half-settled backlog cannot exist. Every OTHER location still refuses: a market stall with an empty table is a real shortage.
  **`kind_for()`** picks the resolution from the item: "log the production run" in front of something you have never made would be nonsense. **Refund/void calls `cancel_for_sale()`** — the units are back on the counter, and being told to make something sitting in front of you is how a list like this trains people to stop reading it. Only OPEN entries are cancelled; a settled one describes a run that really happened.
  **Two flags, one fact.** A backlog CAUSES a negative, which 1.115.0 already flags as "Count is off". Showing both would train the reader to ignore both, so `shortBadge()` suppresses itself when a backlog explains the negative: **To log** is a job, **Count is off** is a mystery.
  **Two callers, two questions.** `/locations/{id}/inventory` takes `?till=1`: the till wants what it may SELL, the location screen wants what is physically HERE (that is the list you pull stock back from). `itemTiles()` takes `{primary}` — a 0-qty tile is `disabled`, which at the primary would grey out the whole catalogue; it now reads "not logged yet" instead. `pos.js onHand()` returns Infinity at the primary, because capping the cart just moves the argument with the customer one layer earlier.
  **The one asymmetry, pinned by a test:** voiding a primary sale returns the goods to the COUNTER, not to the workshop — they came back over the counter, so that is where they are. The company total is unchanged.
  **Gates:** `primary.php` (60) drives the real `push()`, `resolve_item()` and the lifted `cover_from_workshop()`; `primaryui.js` (43) EXECUTES `onHand`/`isPrimary`/`itemTiles`. **23 mutations verified red.** Two were green at first and both were the same old trap: the FAKE was enforcing a rule the code was supposed to enforce (it filtered `status = 'open'` itself, so deleting that from the SQL changed nothing), and a lifted function cannot see whether the app wires it up. checkout() itself is exercised end to end **on staging only** — it reaches sales, sale lines, payables and receipt numbering, and a fake deep enough to host that would be testing the fake.
- **A lot price stopped losing money to rounding (1.117.0, DB 0.37.0)** — owner-reported with a screenshot, and their diagnosis was exactly right: 82.42 for 1000 g showed a cost of 82.40. `totals()` did `$b * net_cost($l)` and `net_cost()` read the STORED `unit_cost`, which is rounded — 0.08242 saved as 0.0824, and times 1000 that is 82.40. **The error scales with the quantity**, so the bigger the delivery the further the document drifts from the invoice it exists to mirror; at 100,000 units it would lose two leva.
  **Fix, in two layers so neither alone is load-bearing:** `priced_unit()` derives the per-unit figure from the LOT at full float precision instead of reading the rounded column, and new **`line_net( $line, $incl, $mode )`** returns `lot * ( $b / $div )` — which for a fully-received line is `lot * 1.0`, the invoice figure untouched, with no division and no multiplication to round away. `totals()` and `shape_purchase`'s `line_cost` both route through it. `unit_from_lot()` now rounds to **6**, and `purchase_lines.unit_cost` was widened to `DECIMAL(14,6)` (`Install::widen_unit_cost()`, guard `vgc_sm_unit_cost_6dp`) so a TYPED unit price survives being written down.
  **UI, requested in the same message:** the per-unit/per-lot toggle is gone. Both boxes are always on screen and either is fillable; the derived one is recessive (`.is-derived`, dashed) but still editable, because typing in it is how you switch which one counts. `lot_cost` null/not-null still records which was typed. The row went 12 → 13 columns and the card breakpoint moved 1100 → **1240**, because it genuinely stopped fitting — a real sideways scroll, found by MEASURING in Chrome rather than guessing. `measurelot.js` now checks both layout regimes at seven widths.
  **Testing lesson, again.** The first mutation sweep showed four guards GREEN. Two were redundancy — fixing either layer fixes the total, so each has to be mutated alone AND together, and the combined mutation is the one that reproduces the report. The other two were grep assertions a real regression walked straight past: `/l\.lot_cost = null;/` matched the *other* branch of the same function. The decision is now `costEdit( line, which, raw )`, module-level and pure, and the harness RUNS it. **A guard that cannot be made to fail is not a guard — run the mutation sweep before believing any of them.**
- **Expiry reached the two places stock actually arrives (1.116.0, DB 0.36.0)** — reported as "when I make a new purchase I have no way of adding expiration date, and on the products page this is also not available". Both true. `movements.expires_on` had existed since 1.108.0 but was settable from exactly ONE place — the Receive stock form on an item page — so anything arriving on a purchase document, which is how most stock arrives, could not be dated at all; and `expiryCard()` returned `''` until some lot already had a date, so there was no sign on the product that expiry existed.
  **New:** `purchase_lines.expires_on DATE NULL`, and `items.shelf_life INT NULL` + `items.shelf_life_unit VARCHAR(8) NULL` (`Install::ensure_expiry_columns()`, guard `vgc_sm_expiry_v2`). A number AND a unit, never a count of days — see the timezone/calendar note below. `Repository::expiry_from_shelf_life( $item, $from )` is the one resolver; `shape_item` exposes `shelf_life`, `shelf_life_unit` and a derived `expiry_default` so every screen offering a best-before box fills it the same way instead of doing its own date maths.
  **The flow:** the line stores the date → `reconcile()` puts it in the `record_movement` meta on the receive leg (and only that leg — a return carries no best-before) → the ledger is what the Goes off list reads. Correcting the date AFTER the goods arrived is the case that needed thought: `reconcile()` writes only the DIFFERENCE, so a fully-received line raises no new movement and the correction would go nowhere. It is restated in **`save_lines()`**, not `reconcile()`, because that is where the PREVIOUS row is in hand — so it writes only when the date actually moved, rather than once per line per save.
  **The bug this nearly shipped with, and the lesson:** the first resolver used `strtotime()` (which parses in the SERVER'S timezone) with `gmdate()` (which formats in UTC). Every computed best-before landed **a day early on every site east of Greenwich**, which is every site this runs on. It was caught only because the machine running the tests happens to be Europe/Berlin — on a UTC box it would have shipped, and staging IS UTC, so staging would not have caught it either. Now `DateTimeImmutable` + `DateTimeZone('UTC')` throughout, and `expiry.php` **pins four timezones** and asserts the same calendar day in each. **A date is a calendar day, not an instant: never mix a local-timezone parse with a UTC format, and never let a date test inherit the machine's timezone.**
  **Two implementations, cross-checked.** The browser needs the same arithmetic to pre-fill a line, so `shelfLifeDate()` in `purchases.js` mirrors the PHP. `expiryui.js` does not restate an expected table — it SHELLS OUT to the real PHP resolver and asserts both agree over eight cases (leap day, 31 January + 1 month, year boundaries). Two implementations of one calendar drift the moment either is touched, and a best-before that disagrees between the form and the ledger is worse than no date.
  **UI:** the purchase line grid went 11 → 12 columns, with Best before placed AFTER the money columns so the hairline and caption row pinned to `nth-child(6)` are untouched (`lotui.js`/`expiryui.js` both assert the cell count and that Cost is still 6th; `measurelot.js` re-measured at four widths, no sideways scroll). `expiryCard()` now draws as soon as a shelf life exists. Gates: `expiry.php` (35), `expiryui.js` (41), **9 mutations verified red**.
- **Two things staging found in 1.115.0 that no local harness could — one of them already shipped in 1.114.0** — **(1) dbDelta does not parse SQL.** It splits a CREATE TABLE body by LINES and reads each one as a field definition, so the `--` comments documenting `lot_cost` and `client_ref` were emitted as `ALTER TABLE x ADD COLUMN -- enough.` — four broken statements failing on EVERY request after a version bump, one of them a truncated `CHANGE COLUMN unit_cost unit_cost DECIMAL(12,4` that did no harm only because it was a syntax error. A shorter comment would have made it valid, and it would have redefined a live column. **Never put a comment inside a dbDelta statement** — explanation goes in a PHP comment above it. `installcheck.php` now refuses any `--` in a CREATE TABLE, verified red.
  **(2) The WP 6.7 textdomain notice was never fully fixed, and it was the visible half of a DATA bug.** 1.113.1 deferred the migration *logging*; it never touched `VGC_SM_Access::install()` (`add_role()` with a `__()` label) or three one-time migrations that write TRANSLATED strings into the database — a location named "Store", a contact labelled "Main", a payment noted "Brought forward". At `plugins_loaded` the textdomain is not loaded, so a Bulgarian site stored the ENGLISH word permanently. All four now run at `init` through **`vgc_sm_migrate_at_init( $option, $callback )`**, which sets the done-flag INSIDE the callback: scheduling the work and flagging it done in the same breath would let a request that never reaches `init` leave a one-time migration undone and recorded as finished, and there is no second chance. `installcheck.php` now follows the closure into every `VGC_SM_X::method()` it calls and fails if that method translates — the old check read only the closure's own text, which is exactly why it passed for two releases. It is also now bounded to the closure by brace-matching; the first version swept to end-of-file and flagged the activation hook, which runs long after `init` and is fine.
  **Both were found by reading `debug.log` on the box after forcing a full schema pass (`delete_option('vgc_sm_db_version')`) and clearing every migration flag. Neither is visible any other way** — do this on every release that touches `create_tables()` or the `plugins_loaded` closure.
- **The deferred findings, closed (1.115.0, DB 0.35.0)** — L3, Q5, L1, P4 from the v2 audit. Only **P5** is left, and it is a product decision rather than a defect (see below).
  **P4 — offline idempotency.** `client_ref VARCHAR(40) NULL` + **`UNIQUE KEY`** on `movements` and `production_runs` (`Install::ensure_client_ref_columns()`, guard `vgc_sm_client_ref_v1`). The column is inert; **the index is the mechanism** — existing rows are all NULL and MySQL never collides NULLs, so one column protects everything and adds nothing to history. The id is minted in `write()` **before the first attempt**, not when queueing: a request that landed and lost its reply is indistinguishable from being offline, and that is the exact case this exists for. `create_movement()` / `production_run()` look the ref up first and answer a replay with the ORIGINAL result (`replayed: true`), and both re-read on insert failure so two racing replays resolve to the winner rather than an error about something that did happen. **The other half was worse:** `flushQueue`'s catch deleted any item the server refused — an afternoon at a stall gone, silently. IndexedDB is now **v2** with a `failed` store; `failedRecord()` is hoisted and pure **so the harness runs it**, because the bug was never inside a function — it was a `return idbDel(...)` on the line after a perfectly correct one, which no regex over source would ever flag. `V.tpl.sync.failedCard()` shows them on the Overview with the server's own reason, Try again (same body, so the write id survives and a retry still cannot double-execute) and Discard (via `armConfirm`, never `confirm()`).
  **L3 — negatives were floored away.** `sync_stock_cache()` wrapped the ledger sum in `max(0, …)`, so a stall that had sold more than it was recorded receiving read as an ordinary empty shelf; `rebuild_stock_cache()` — the thing meant to be RESTORING truth — then dropped the row entirely. Both now keep the real figure (`location_stock.qty` was already signed `DECIMAL(18,4)`; no schema change). New `Locations::negatives($location_id = null)`, a `short_lines` count on `shape()`, a **Count is off** flag on the location tile, `shortCard()` naming the items on the location page, and `item_breakdown()` switched to `ABS(qty) > 0.0001` so the item page — the one screen an operator checks to explain a missing count — stops being the one hiding it. Deliberately NOT folded into `units`/`retail_value`: those answer "what is on this shelf", and a negative is not stock.
  **Q5 — the reversal named the wrong row.** `undo_location_transfer()` paired against the *newest* unreversed transfer for that (location, item), so undoing the first of three pushes was logged against the third. Quantities always netted out, which is what made it invisible. `push()`/`pull()` now stamp their ledger row with `ref_type='movement', ref_id=$movement_id` (`write_ledger()` already took both), and the undo matches on that. The old heuristic survives **only** as a fallback for pre-1.115.0 rows, which carry no ref (`ref_id IS NULL` added so it cannot re-capture a stamped row).
  **L1 — settling was a one-way door.** A settled note offered neither Mark paid nor Cancel, so a mis-tap wrote off a real debt permanently. `Notes::unsettle()` + `DELETE /notes/{id}/paid` at **manager** level (settling stays operator: doing it is daily, undoing it is reopening a debt somebody wrote off). Cancel is deliberately not reachable straight from settled — cancelling reverses STOCK, and doing that to a paid-for note should take two decisions.
  **Gates:** `deferred.php` (26) drives the real `sync_stock_cache`/`rebuild_stock_cache`/`negatives`/`undo_location_transfer`/`unsettle`/`movement_by_client_ref`/`run_by_client_ref`; its fake wpdb **enforces the UNIQUE index** and **records any query it does not recognise**, failing the run — a fake that answers silence to an unknown query is how a harness stays green over a broken feature. `deferredui.js` (52) EXECUTES `needsWriteId`/`replayOutcome`/`failedRecord`/`writeId`, lifting `IDEMPOTENT` **out of core.js** rather than restating it (the first draft restated it and passed with production runs unprotected — the labourcost.php trap, again). **8 of 9 mutations verified red**; the ninth — that `push()` stamps the ledger — is unreachable from a harness that seeds those rows itself, and is verified on staging instead.
- **A price for the whole lot (1.114.0)** — vendors quote both ways, and the operator was dividing by hand. One nullable column on each of `movements` and `purchase_lines`, and **the stored-truth pattern from labour hours applies again: store what was typed, derive the rest.** The alternative — a boolean plus a unit price — reopens 100.00 as 99.9999 and cannot be fixed by rounding harder. Purchase lines carry the switch UNDER the cost input inside the SAME grid cell: the row is a fixed grid whose money hairline and caption row are pinned to `nth-child(6)`, so an extra cell would slide both (`lotui.js` asserts the row still has exactly 11 children). On the item page the three modes had to COMPOSE — a lot price on a delivery received by the box divides by the base units, not by the boxes and then `pack_size` again — so `enteredUnitCost()` is module-level and pure **specifically so the harness runs it**, as is `linePayload()` in purchases.js, per the 1.112.3 rule. `lotprice.php` drives the REAL `save_lines()` by reflection (private, and a *copy* is what let `labourcost.php` stay green through a broken feature), the REAL `shape_purchase()` for the read leg, and the REAL `edit_movement()`; its fake table refuses a write naming a column it lacks, so a missing migration reads as "nothing was written". All eight guards verified by reinstating the bug.
- **One stray `}` deleted a rule, and nothing anywhere warned (1.113.4)** — `app.css` line 1877 had an orphan closing brace between two rules. CSS has no error reporting: a stray `}` at top level puts the parser into ERROR RECOVERY and it **silently discards the entire next rule**. That rule was `.vgc-sm-prow { display:flex; flex-wrap:wrap }`, so the produce rows were never flex at all — every part took its own line — and the 1.113.0 fix appeared to do nothing because the rule it added was never applied. Before 1.113.0 the victim was `.vgc-sm-waste`, which is why the Spoiled box had always sat on its own line. The file also ran to EOF with the `@media (pointer: coarse)` block unclosed (browsers auto-close, but the next appended rule would have landed inside it). **Diagnosis method that worked, after three wrong guesses from reading the CSS:** drive Chrome via puppeteer-core and ask it — `getComputedStyle(row).display` returned `block`, then walking `document.styleSheets` showed the base rule ABSENT while its siblings were present, which is the exact signature of error recovery. `measurerow.js` now measures the row at six widths in a real browser (same-line = vertical ranges OVERLAP; comparing `top` is wrong under `align-items:center`), and `cssbalance.js` guards brace balance, comment termination and the survival of the named rules. **jsdom cannot lay out and the preview pane renders static snapshots — for any question of the form "is this on one line", use puppeteer.**
- **A schema fix needs a DB_VERSION bump to reach anyone (1.113.2)** — 1.113.1 added the three missing `dbDelta()` calls and changed nothing on any site, because `create_tables()` only runs when `get_option('vgc_sm_db_version') !== VGC_STOCK_MANAGER_DB_VERSION`. Staging still had no purchases tables after updating. **Rule: any change to `create_tables()` MUST bump `VGC_STOCK_MANAGER_DB_VERSION`** (here 0.32.0 → 0.33.0), or it is a no-op wearing the clothes of a fix. dbDelta is idempotent, so a bump costs an existing install one harmless pass. Caught only because the fix was verified on a real box instead of assumed.
- **What a fresh install found in ten seconds (1.113.1)** — first SSH session, first activation on a virgin SiteGround box (PHP 8.2.33, MySQL 8.4.6), `WP_DEBUG_LOG` on. Three real defects in the log immediately. **(1) The purchases tables were never created.** `$sql_purchases`, `$sql_purch_lines`, `$sql_purch_pay` are built in full in `create_tables()` and no `dbDelta()` was ever called on them. Invisible on every existing install because they have the tables from an older version; a FRESH install had no purchases at all — which is exactly what Company 2 would have been. **(2) `SELECT COUNT(*) AS lines`** in `Locations::shape()` — `lines` is RESERVED in MySQL 8, so the whole aggregate is a syntax error, `$agg` comes back null and every sale location silently reports 0 items / 0 units / 0 value. Quote the alias (`` AS `lines` ``); the API key stays `lines` because the app reads it. **(3)** the two migration audit messages added in 1.112.x called `__()` at `plugins_loaded`, before the textdomain loads — WP 6.7 notice on every request, and the string would have been English anyway; now deferred via `vgc_sm_log_when_ready()` to `init` priority 20. New guard `installcheck.php`: every `CREATE TABLE` variable must appear in a `dbDelta()` call (and vice versa, catching a typo'd name), no alias on a *SQL* line may be a reserved word (prose like "reads as of that date" is excluded — the first version flagged it and a checker that cries wolf gets turned off), and nothing inside the `plugins_loaded` closure may call `__()`. Both halves verified by reinstating each bug. **Standing note: the local harnesses run PHP 7.4 (XAMPP) while the server is 8.2 — test anything version-sensitive on staging.**
- **Produce row extracted and its layout fixed (1.113.0)** — the consume row was `display:flex` with **no `flex-wrap`**, while its last child (the substitutes strip) carried `flex-basis:100%`. That child took the full width from its siblings on the same line, collapsing `.name` (which had `flex:1;min-width:0`, so it could shrink to nothing) and printing the quantity box over it. Wrapping alone is NOT the whole fix: the qty block and the spoiled label also needed `flex:none` so the name is the only part that gives way, and `.vgc-sm-prow__name` needs `flex: 1 1 150px` — a basis, not just a grow — so it has a floor. Phone (`max-width:560px`) gives the name its own line. The row moved to **`app/js/tpl/production.tpl.js`** (`consumeRow`, `runNoteCard`) — produce was the last screen building markup inline, which is precisely why a bug this visible shipped: its only test was `runprodswap.js` grepping its own source. Added to `app_scripts()` (**that list is also what the service worker precaches**). `runproducerow.js` renders the row and asserts the LAYOUT CONTRACT against the real stylesheet (jsdom does not lay out, so: does the row wrap, is the name the only flexible part, do the boxes hold their size) — verified it fails when `flex-wrap` is removed. `runprodswap.js` now reads the template for "is it rendered" and the screen for "is it bound", since the pair spans two files. **Run notes:** `production_runs.note` and the `note` param have existed since run costing; the client simply never sent one. Now a field on the produce screen, shown in the Production report and its CSV. **Test-dir wrinkle learned here:** `run-all.js` sync REFRESHES mirrors, it does not create them — a newly mirrored plugin file must be copied in once by hand (it fails ENOENT, which is the right way round).
- **The client never sent `kind` (1.112.3) — the actual cause** — `save` built `{label, amount, basis, kind, hours}` and then chained a SECOND normalising `.map()` (pre-existing, which the 1.112.0 patch inserted before instead of replacing) that rebuilt each row as `{label, amount, basis}`. Every save therefore posted no kind and the server defaulted it to `'other'`. 1.112.1 (read path) and 1.112.2 (one-tap move) were both real fixes and neither could work while nothing was being written. **The testing lesson, which cost three round trips:** `runlabourui.js` asserted `/kind: isLabour\(e\)/.test(source)` and passed — the line WAS there, and undone on the next one. A regex over source proves a line exists, never that it survives. The payload builder is now `extrasPayload()`, pure and module-level **specifically so a test can run it**, and the harness executes it and inspects the object. Verified: reinstating the stripping map fails 6 assertions, one of which prints the exact payload the owner reported. Rule for this codebase: **anything the client sends must be tested by executing the builder, never by reading it.**
- **Moving a cost line between sections (1.112.2)** — `data-tolabour` / `data-toother` under each recipe cost row, flipping `kind` and re-rendering (demoting also clears `hours`, since only labour has them). Promised in design brief 8 and not built in 1.112.0, which mattered more than it looked: the 1.112.1 auto-repair only matches the exact words `labour`/`labor`/`труд`/`работа`, so a line the operator named themselves was recoverable *only* by deleting and re-adding it. New guard `labourroundtrip.php` drives the REAL `save_recipe()` → `get_recipe()` → `save_recipe()` with a fake table that **refuses an insert naming a column it does not have, exactly as wpdb does** — so a missing migration shows up as "nothing was written", not as a silently mis-filed row. Verified it reproduces the 1.112.0 symptoms (6 failures) and passes on the fix.
- **The read path is part of the round trip (1.112.1)** — `get_recipe()` shaped extras down to `label/amount/basis` and dropped `kind`/`hours`. Labour drew under "Other costs" on reopen, and because the editor posts back the rows it was handed, **the next save rewrote every labour line as `other`** and it vanished from the report too. Saving was right; rendering was right; the endpoint between them was not. `labourcost.php` now drives the REAL `get_recipe()` shaping over what the REAL save wrote, three saves deep — testing the halves separately could never catch this, and that is the general lesson: **any field the editor round-trips needs a save→read→save guard, not a save guard and a render guard.** Repair migration `vgc_sm_labour_v2` re-runs the exact-label classification; a line named something bespoke cannot be recovered and must be re-ticked by hand. Also: the Labour tab now lists `recipes` (what each recipe charges per unit, from `recipe_labour_table()`) beside the run-booked figures, because the tab reads production runs and was therefore empty until the next run — honest, but indistinguishable from broken.
- **Labour as its own kind of cost line (1.112.0)** — DB **0.32.0**, guard `vgc_sm_labour_v1`. `bom_extras` gains `kind` (`labour`|`other`, default `other`, indexed) and `hours DECIMAL(12,4) NULL`; `production_runs` gains `labour_cost` and `labour_hours`. Deliberately NOT a `labour_amount` column on the `boms` header: that forks the cost model, and `recipe_roll()` already sums extras into both net and gross, so a `kind` column costs one column and NO new cost logic. Never match on the LABEL — bilingual site, free text, and a typo drops a line out of the report silently.
  **`amount` is always the money; `hours` is an optional way of arriving at it.** When hours are set, `save_bom()` computes `amount = hours × VGC_SM_Sync::labour_rate()` and ignores whatever amount was posted; with no rate set, hours are discarded and the typed amount stands (hours × 0 would silently cost work at nothing). Only `kind='labour'` honours hours. **A labour line with no label is kept** and defaulted to "Labour" — every other extra is still dropped for want of one, and applying that rule to labour would throw away the number the operator typed.
  **`VGC_SM_Production::extras_for_run( $item_id, $qty )`** is now the single place the extras loop lives; `preview()`, `run()` and `recost_run()` all call it (there were three copies, and the labour split had to mean the same in all three). Labour is **inside** `extras_cost`, not additional to it. A run with no labour records **0, not NULL** — NULL is reserved for runs made before this shipped, and the report counts those separately rather than folding them in as zero.
  **The rate:** `labour_rate` setting (defaults to 0 = unset), `VGC_SM_Costing::preview_labour_rate()` / `apply_labour_rate()` mirroring `preview_basis()`. Applying rewrites `amount` on every hours-priced line in one transaction, then `Repository::forget_maps()` + `Costing::flush()` (`forget_maps()` went public for this — the rewrite is raw SQL outside `save_bom()`). It does **not** touch `avg_cost` or any run snapshot: those are records of what was actually booked.
  **Report:** `GET /reports/labour?days=` at **manager** (wages, like sale profit — item cost stays at operator), plus `/labour-rate` and `/labour-rate/preview`. Reads each run's own snapshot, never today's recipe. Reports is now tabbed (`#/reports/:tab`, `.vgc-sm-tabs`); the Labour tab shows total, hours, share-of-production-cost, and a by-product table with the booked per-unit figure beside what the CURRENT recipe would charge — those disagreeing is the signal that the recipe moved after the work was done. **Also fixed in passing:** `report_production` priced runs at today's `unit_cost_net × qty`, so an old run's cost moved whenever a material price did — exactly the drift 1.106.0 run costing exists to prevent. It now reads `total_cost` and falls back only for runs that were never costed (`costed` flag says which).
  Migration `classify_labour_extras()` sets `kind='labour'` on exact label matches only (`labour`, `labor`, `труд`, `работа`), logs the count, and is reversible in the editor. Guards: `labourcost.php`, `runlabour.php`, `labourreport.php`, `ratechange.php`, `runlabourui.js`.
- **Blocked dialogs swept out (1.111.0)** — `window.confirm()`/`prompt()`/`alert()` are blocked in this deployment: they never appear, `confirm()` returns false and `prompt()` returns null, so `if (!confirm(...)) return;` is an unconditional early return and the button silently does nothing. SIXTEEN call sites had accumulated across 8 files despite the standing rule and despite three existing comments saying "never confirm()". The dead ones included **Issue note** (the stock-note screen's primary action), Delete draft, Cancel note, Delete item, Delete order, Remove sale-report, Delete purchase, publish/unpublish held units, **set a partner's price** (the subcontract rate — a core 1.99.0 flow), add-a-unit in two places, create-item-from-barcode, manual barcode entry, reset translations, and shop overwrite. Two shared helpers now live in `core.js` and are exported on `V`: **`armConfirm(btn, message, onConfirm)`** — first press restates the consequence IN the button and arms it, second press acts, any other click or 8s disarms; it saves/restores `innerHTML`, not `textContent`, because `btn()` builds an inline SVG icon before the label and text-only restore silently strips it. **`askInline(anchor, opts, onValue)`** — one field beside the control that raised it, Enter accepts, Escape cancels, the anchor is hidden while open so it cannot be pressed twice, and CANCEL CALLS NOTHING BACK (callers relied on `prompt()`'s null to tell "nothing" from ""). Armed colour follows the button's own variant, not the mechanism: `--danger` goes red, everything else goes brand orange — arming "Issue note" in red would claim it is destructive. `_tests/stock-manager/nodialogs.js` is now a permanent static sweep of `app/js` plus a jsdom drive of both helpers; a new dialog call fails the suite.
- **One cost resolver (1.110.0)** — Q1 from the v2 audit, done properly. `VGC_SM_Costing::unit_cost_info( $row_or_id, $mode )` is now THE ladder: manual > subcontract > produced-average > recipe > typed/derived, with `cost_basis` governing the LAST rung and nothing else. It returns a block — `{value, net, gross, source, vat_rate}` — not a float, because `shape_item` needs five answers about one cost and used to re-derive all five. `unit_cost()` returns null for "unknown" (1.104.0's rule: unknown is not free); `unit_cost_num()` reads that as 0.0 for arithmetic. `BOM::unit_cost_net`/`unit_cost_gross`, `Sales::unit_cost_of` and `shape_item` are one-line delegates; `BOM::cost_vat_rate` delegates to `Costing::cost_vat_rate`; `BOM::demand_per_item` went public for the roll-up. Four live divergences died with the copies: (1) `unit_cost_gross` dropped recipe EXTRAS, so any recipe with labour showed a gross BELOW its net (5.70 net / 3.24 gross on the probe); (2) `Sales::unit_cost_of` AND `shape_item` both ignored `cost_basis`, so switching it re-costed recipes only; (3) `cost_calc` echoed the manual override instead of the recipe, so the form's "calculated" preview lied; (4) `Production::waste_cost` and the waste row costed the same material two ways. **Memoisation is request-scoped and flushed, never persisted** — `flush()` drops ALL of it (a component's new price restates every parent) and is called from `Repository::create_item/update_item/archive_item/delete_item/save_bom`, `Costing::recalculate`, `Partners::set_price` and `Sync::save_settings`. `update_item` is the load-bearing one: the REST handler writes, re-reads and shapes in the same request. A truncated (cycle/depth) result is NEVER cached — `$trunc` is snapshotted around `resolve()`. `Repository::bom_items_by_bom()`/`bom_extras_by_bom()` join `boms_by_output()` as request-scoped maps; `forget_maps()` drops all three. DELIBERATELY NOT delegated, do not "fix" these: `Orders::save_lines` grosses at the ORDER LINE's sale rate (mode 'gross' would use the supplier's and the document would stop balancing); `Purchases::save_lines` keeps the vendor's own price; the `Locations` value aggregate stays `COALESCE(avg_cost, cost_net, 0)` SQL because a PHP resolver per row is the N+1 the aggregate exists to avoid. `last_cost` was KEPT as a final rung (the spec dropped it) — unreachable today, but dropping it is a behaviour change with no upside. Gate: `_tests/stock-manager/costladder.php`, 48/48.
- **Receipt ordering (1.110.0, P3)** — `receipts()` / `run_receipts()` / `all_receipts()` / `receipts_for_all()` now carry `sort_at`, `source` and `seq`, and all four sort through one comparator. A bare date is placed at **MIDDAY** — a delivery happened at some unknown point in the working day, so the middle is least wrong in both directions. Hand receipts keep the real `created_at` (the SELECT used to throw it away with `DATE()`). Ties break source-then-id because usort is unstable before PHP 8 and the project targets 7.4, so avg_cost could otherwise differ between two identical calls. `last_cost_at` is `substr(...,0,10)` — it is a DATE column and a run receipt carries a full timestamp.
- **Voiding a run (1.110.0, Q4)** — `void_run()` sets `reverses` on each cancelling entry, selects only entries with `reversed_at IS NULL AND reverses IS NULL`, and stamps the originals INSIDE its own transaction. `Corrections::undo_run()` no longer pre-selects or post-stamps: that ran after `void_run` had committed, so a failure between them left the stock reversed, the entries still reading as live, and no way to finish — `void_run` refuses a run that is no longer 'completed'. Side effect worth knowing: a void's cancelling entries are now correctly non-editable and non-undoable, and hide under "show cancelled" like every other reversal.
- **Settings are patched, not rewritten (1.110.0, L7)** — `save_settings()` = `settings_patch()` (only the keys the request actually sent) + `merge_settings()` (drop the option cache, re-read, merge, write, drop `alloptions` again). The old exhaustive `$new` literal meant any setting added to the defaults but forgotten there was silently deleted on the next save of anything, and two screens saving different fields overwrote each other. The second `wp_cache_delete('alloptions')` is load-bearing, not hygiene: without it `POST /settings` returns the pre-save snapshot and the screen looks like it did not save.
- **Physical store retired (1.110.0, Q2)** — `class-store.php` deleted; `/items/{id}/store-distribute` and `/store-sell` unregistered; `store_stocked`/`store_qty`/`store_workshop` no longer emitted by `shape_item`; the "Stocked at the physical store" tick box removed from the item form (it persisted a flag nothing branched on); the `store_ledger` CREATE removed from `class-install.php` **together with** its `class-reset.php::transaction_tables()` row — `counts()` does an unguarded `SELECT COUNT(*)`, so removing one without the other fatals the reset screen on a fresh install. The killer was `Store::clamp()`: `record_movement()` called it on EVERY negative delta, and it did an uncached `SELECT *` to compare a number that is always 0 after `migrate_store()`. **Columns `items.store_qty` and `items.store_stocked` STAY**, in both the CREATE TABLE and `ensure_item_columns()` — dropping a column is irreversible, dbDelta cannot do it, and keeping fresh-install and upgraded schemas identical is worth far more than nine bytes a row. Existing `store_ledger` tables stay as a frozen archive; `uninstall.php` still drops them.
- **Review-fix batch (1.109.0)** — from the v2 audit. **VAT-when-unregistered**: `Purchases::totals()` and `Orders::totals()` (and order `strip_vat()`) used the raw `vat_rate`; all now route through `VGC_SM_Sync::vat_rate()` like the till, so an unregistered company's gross==net. **Production `have`→`available`**: the non-recipe-waste shortage recompute in `run()` read a key `direct_requirements()` never sets, false-blocking any standing run with logged spoilage; the waste-only appended row now also carries `available` and a real `unit_cost`. **edit_movement** moved from `$write` to `$admin` (it rewrites the ledger + avg cost). **Checkout transaction**: `checkout()` now wraps header+lines+deduct+accrue in START/COMMIT with rollback, and `accrue_for_sale()`/`accrue_recipe()` propagate WP_Error so the sale rolls back on an accrual failure. **Duplicate-line oversell**: availability checked against a running per-item total. **push/pull atomicity**: bail before the ledger write if the workshop movement failed. **Order→service return**: `record_movement` accepts `meta['service_return']` for a reconcile reversal of a now-service item. **Costing precedence aligned across all sites** (did NOT collapse to one resolver — deferred): `unit_cost_net` now checks manual BEFORE supplier; `unit_cost_gross` short-circuits to net×(1+cost_vat) for manual/avg/supplier/leaf (no more gross<net); `shape_item` gained the supplier branch; net roll-up now rounded. **Updater** fails closed (mandatory https + sha256). **bridge_url** forced to https on save. Deferred with reasons: P4 client_ref idempotency (schema+client+server), P5 block-vs-warn (product decision), L1 note un-pay (needs reversal design), L3 negative-surface (M1 closed the path), Q2 dead store removal, Q4/Q5 audit-linkage, L7 settings race.
- **Drawer bindings are NOT APP-scoped (1.108.0)**: the quick-filter editor shipped unable to save. `openDrawer()` appends its overlay to `document.body`, not to `#vgc-sm-app`, so five `APP.querySelectorAll('[data-qf…]')` bindings found nothing; every row stayed blank, and `clean_quick_filters()` correctly discarded rows with no label. Only `#qf-add`/`#qf-save` worked because those used `getElementById`. **Third occurrence of "renders but nothing listens"** — now guarded by `rundrawer.js`, which walks every `*Drawer()` template for the hooks it renders and fails if any logic file binds one through APP. Verified by reintroducing the bug: 3 failures, then clean.
- **Lot expiry (1.108.0)**: `movements.expires_on DATE NULL` — on the LOT, not the item, because two deliveries of the same wax expire on different days and one date on `items` could only ever be wrong for one of them. `expiring_lots()` returns dated `receive` movements that are neither a reversal nor reversed, soonest first, with a three-state `state` (expired / soon ≤30d / ok) so the screen does not have to pick the threshold. **Quantities are what the lot ARRIVED as, not what remains** — there is no per-lot consumption, so a remaining balance would be an invention, and the panel says so. Called from `get_item()` only, never `shape_item()`, which also runs for every row of the catalogue list — one query per item there would be 500 to paint a page.
- **Pinned filter buttons (1.107.0)**: user-defined chips above the item lists, stored as the `quick_filters` setting — **and therefore touched in all three places** (default in `settings()`, forward-list in `save_settings()`, GET response in `trait-shop.php` including its key list), which is the 1.78.0 `cost_basis` lesson. Each is `{label, scope, category_id, low}`, sanitised by `clean_quick_filters()` — whitelisted scope, cast ids, stripped label, capped at 12, re-indexed so a dropped row leaves no hole — because whatever survives is rendered as a button on every list. **`/settings` is admin-only and holds the bridge token, so it was NOT widened for this**: a narrow `POST /quick-filters` at manager level writes that one key and nothing else. `save_settings()` falls back to current values for absent keys, so a partial payload is safe. Boot carries them so the row paints on first render rather than flashing empty.
- **Run costing (1.106.0)**: the first release in this series to move an existing number ON PURPOSE. Built on one sentence — **a production run is a receipt of the finished product** — so `VGC_SM_Costing` gains `run_receipts()` shaped identically to `receipts()` (`{qty, cost, at}` newest-first) and `all_receipts()` merges both by date for anything both bought and made; `average_for_on_hand()` is reused untouched. Precedence in `unit_cost_net()`: manual > **actual run average** > recipe roll-up, so a product never produced still costs its recipe and nothing is ever zero for want of history. `good_qty` is STORED, not divided out of total/unit — both are rounded to 4dp and the division lands on 7.9999 often enough to matter. Scrap does not reduce `total_cost`, only the divisor. **Deliberately NOT backfilled**: an old run's materials and their prices are gone, so any figure invented would be today's recipe cost wearing an old date — a flat line on the trend the feature exists to show; uncosted runs are excluded and the product falls back to its recipe. `recost_run()` is recoverable because the movements record what left the shelf and the accrual lines record subcontracted work that never did — but it values at TODAY's prices and says so. **Harness lesson: four checks failed on my own arithmetic, not the code — 15 on hand is 10@3.00 + 5@2.40 = 2.80, not 2.70. Compute the expected value twice when the test is the thing proving a money change.**
- **Substitutes at production (1.105.0)**: asked for as "more than one recipe per product"; it is not. The recipe's STRUCTURE never varies — a postcard always takes a sheet, ink and the cutting — only which item fills a slot does. Alternative whole recipes would duplicate the 90% that is identical to capture the 10% that is not, and every duplicate then has to be kept in step by hand. So: **nothing on `boms`/`bom_items` changed**, one new table `bom_item_alts (bom_item_id, item_id, quantity, unit)`, and costing is untouched by construction. Key facts: (1) `save_bom()` deletes and re-inserts every `bom_item`, so ids are NOT stable — substitutes are written in the same pass from the component that carries them, never stored independently; (2) a substitute carries its OWN quantity because one A4 is half an A3, scaled proportionally (`alt_qty / line_qty`) so the line's `basis` is irrelevant; (3) `apply_substitutions()` keys on the ORIGINAL component id, since a swapped row reports the new material and keying on the current id would make a second swap replace nothing; (4) **`merge_same_component()` folds rows that land on the same item — otherwise each is checked against full stock alone and both pass while together they exceed it**; (5) the client re-asks `preview` on every swap rather than recomputing shortages locally, so there is only ever one version of that arithmetic.
- **Sub-unit prices (1.104.1)**: reported as "material prices should allow values under 0.01", but nothing ever refused them — every money column is `DECIMAL(x,4)` (only `vat_rate`/`markup_pct` are 2dp, and those are percentages), `sanitize_item()` casts without rounding, and every price input is `step="any"`. `money()` hard-coded `maximumFractionDigits: 2`, so 0.0035 printed as **0.00** — not rounded, *wrong*, and wrong in the direction that makes a material look free. Now `< 1` gets up to 4 places, `>= 1` is untouched, so nothing a customer pays changes shape. **Worth remembering as a diagnosis pattern: "the system won't let me enter X" was really "the system won't show me X" — check storage, sanitiser, input step and formatter separately before assuming a validation rule exists.** No migration, no re-entry: existing values were always stored correctly.
- **Stock value (1.104.0)**: `stockValue()` in `catalogue.tpl.js` is the single definition — `stock_qty * cost_net`, ex-VAT to match the cost column beside it — used by the desk cell, the phone sub-line and `valueTotal()`, and mirrored in `ITEM_COLS.value` so sorting and display can never disagree. **Returns null, never 0, for a service (no stock) or an item with no cost (unknown, not free); a null is skipped by the total rather than added as zero**, which is the difference between a figure that is short and a figure that is wrong. The list now fetches `limit=500` (the server cap) so the total covers the catalogue; when the row count reaches that cap the template says "at least" and explains why, instead of understating what is held. Totals are over the FILTERED list and say so.
- **One supplier control (1.103.0)**: `items.supplier` (free text) and `supplier_partner_id` were two answers to one question, and the second only ever appeared as *Performed by*, hidden in Classification on a service. Unified into `supplierPicker()` in **Stock & sourcing**, same stored field, label switching Supplier/Performed by with the tick — the picker is NO LONGER cleared when the service tick comes off, because a material legitimately has a supplier and `supplier_of()` already refuses to accrue against anything that is not a service. Two accessors now, and the distinction matters: **`supplier_of()` = who gets PAID (service only, money-bearing); `linked_partner()` = who SELLS it to us (any item, drives `cost_vat_rate`)**. Reverse link via `supplies_of()` on the partner page, because the form's hint promises the link points both ways. Free-text box hides once a partner is linked. Three assertions from 1.100.1 asserted the design this replaced and were rewritten, not deleted.
- **Deleting a sale (1.102.0)**: admin-only, and deliberately built ON TOP of voiding — `delete_sale()` calls `refund( $id, array(), 'Deleted', true )` when the sale is live, so returning the goods and cancelling its accruals go through the path that is already tested, then removes `location_ledger` rows (`ref_type='sale'`, `ref_id`), the lines and the sale, and rebuilds the stock cache. The deduction and its void net to zero, so removing BOTH leaves stock where it stands and no rows pointing at a receipt that is gone. A cancelling line on a CLOSED supplier bill is deliberately kept: that money changed hands. `delete_sales()` reports per id rather than stopping at the first failure. **Route gotcha worth remembering: registering the same REST route twice makes WordPress `array_merge` the two flat arrays, so the second registration's keys silently overwrite the first — the existing GET /sales/{id} would have become a DELETE.** Both verbs go in ONE `register_rest_route` call as a list of endpoint arrays. UI confirmation is inline in both places (never `confirm()`, which is blocked here), and the bulk button states the count and disarms if the selection changes.
- **A service has no run (1.101.0)**: the 1.99.0 design credited subcontracted work "when it is used" — at the production run, or at a direct till sale. That covers a PRODUCT (its run consumes the recipe) and a service somebody else performs for us (sold directly). It missed the third shape entirely: **a service WE provide whose recipe contains subcontracted work**. A service is never produced, so no run ever walks its recipe, and the direct-sale accrual only looked at the sold item — which has no supplier precisely because we perform it. `accrue_for_sale()` now branches three ways: subcontracted item → one line for itself; our service → `accrue_recipe()` walks it (recursing through nested services we provide, cycle- and depth-guarded); **stocked product → nothing, because its run already credited it**. That last branch is the whole reason this is not "walk every recipe on every sale" — doing so would charge every subcontractor twice for anything manufactured. **Lesson: when a rule is stated as "at moment X or moment Y", enumerate the record shapes and check each one HAS an X or a Y.** Services have no production run, and nothing in the plan noticed.
- **Naming the performer (1.100.1)**: `supplier_partner_id` decides whether using a service owes anybody anything, and for two releases the ONLY screen that could set it was `create_partner_service()`'s drawer. Every service made from the item form, the quick-add drawer, or before 1.99.0 carried 0 — so selling it accrued nothing and the partner page stayed empty. Added a **Performed by** select to `formPage()` (hidden unless `is_service`, cleared when the tick is removed so a plain product can never owe anybody), posted from `catalogue.js`, and surfaced as `supplier_partner_name` on the item page. **This is the 1.98.1 lesson repeated on a different field — "a flag is not a feature until the screen someone actually uses can set it" was already written here, and I still shipped a second field only one screen could reach.** When adding a field that changes money, list every screen that creates or edits that kind of record BEFORE calling it done.
- **Editable sale cost (1.100.0)**: `sale_lines.unit_cost` is snapshotted at checkout so profit cannot drift — right, but a snapshot taken from a figure that was wrong at the time is wrong for ever without a way in. `VGC_SM_Sales::update_costs()` + `PUT /sales/{id}/costs` (manager), with the `Cost & profit` card on the sale page: frozen cost, `current_cost` suggestion, live profit as you type, `Use current costs` to fill from today. The write is scoped by `array( 'id' => $line_id, 'sale_id' => $sale_id )` AND checked against the sale's own line ids, so a line id from another sale cannot be written through this route. **Harness lesson: `$sees_cost` was read inside the `array_map` closure without `use ( $sees_cost )` — PHP closures capture nothing implicitly, so the cost was silently absent for everyone including managers, and the negative test ("an operator sees no cost") passed for entirely the wrong reason.** A test that passes because the feature is broken is worse than no test.
- **Cost VAT is the supplier's (1.99.3)**: `shape_item()` computed `cost_gross = cost_net * (1 + $vat/100)` using OUR SALE rate, so an unregistered subcontractor's final price had 20% added, and a recipe mixing a registered supplier with an unregistered one was grossed entirely at one rate that applied to neither. Added `VGC_SM_BOM::cost_vat_rate()` (supplier's registration when the item names one, ours otherwise) and `unit_cost_gross()`, which rolls up per component so each part carries its own rate — 4*1.2 + 6*1.2 + 15 = 27.00, where the old way gave 30.00. Exposed as `cost_vat_rate` so the label reads *final price, no VAT* and the gross box divides by the right number. **Keyed off a captured `$from_recipe`, NOT `$computed`** — `$computed` is widened to include history-derived costs a few lines above, and reusing it would work the gross out from a different figure than the net. Completes the rule stated in 1.99.2.
- **Two VAT rates, one field (1.99.2)**: `accrue()` set the bill line's rate with `VGC_SM_Sync::vat_rate()` — OUR registration — when the only thing that decides what a supplier charges is THEIR registration. An unregistered subcontractor was billed 20% they never charged, and a registered one billed 0 whenever we were unregistered (understating what we owe). Now read off the bill's `vendor_vat_registered` snapshot, mirroring `save_lines()`, so a partner registering later cannot restate a running bill. Separately, `create_partner_service()` never set `vat_rate`, so every new service took the column default of 20.00. **The rule, stated once: the SUPPLIER's status decides what they charge US (purchase-line rate); OUR status decides what we charge the CUSTOMER (item rate) and whether input VAT is reclaimable (`strip_vat`). Any code that answers one of those with the other is wrong.**
- **The vendor VAT lever (1.99.1)**: the SERVER was always right — `save_lines()` forces `vat_rate = 0` for an unregistered vendor and `strip_vat()` is then a no-op, proved by 11 checks. Every defect was on the client. (1) Toggling `p-vatreg` updated `st` but never re-rated the lines already on the document, so `totals()` kept summing the old per-line rates and the screen lied until save+reload — fixed with one `applyVendorVat()` that owns the rate on every line. (2) The vendor's status was adopted only in the `p-partner` CHANGE handler, so arriving at `#/purchase/new?partner=X` — the route from the partner page — never fired it and a registered vendor silently got rate 0. (3) `parseFloat(l.item_vat_rate) || 20` turned a genuine 0 back into 20, so a non-registered company still saw 20% on the retail column. **Lesson: `|| default` is wrong for any numeric where 0 is meaningful** — and a harness that stubs the very flag under test (runnovat stubs `V.boot`) cannot catch a lever that was never wired. The client helpers are now lifted out of the real source and executed by `runvatui.js` rather than restated.
- **Subcontracted services (1.99.0)**: a service carrying `supplier_partner_id` is work somebody else does for us; one without is work we do ourselves. That single field is the whole distinction, and it is why nothing about the existing tills changed — every service that predates this names no supplier. Consuming a bought service accrues onto ONE rolling bill per partner (`purchases.is_accrual = 1`), which is an ordinary purchase, so payments/balance/overdue/statement all work untouched. Credited **when the work is used** (the run, or a direct till sale), never when the finished piece sells — one moment only, so it cannot double-count. `VGC_SM_BOM::direct_requirements()` flags services, reports `available = null` and forces `short = 0`; `max_buildable()` skips them. **Lesson from the harness: `reverse()` skipped cancelling lines but not lines already cancelled, so a second void posted a second opposite line and the balance went negative** — the fix is `purchase_lines.reversed_at`, stamped on the line that WAS cancelled, mirroring the movement ledger's `reverses`/`reversed_at`. Idempotent undo is not optional when an offline queue can replay a void.
- **Known limit (1.99.0)**: `max_buildable()` returns 0 for a recipe made *only* of subcontracted work, because nothing stock-limited remains to divide. Display only — `run()` gates on shortages, which services never produce, so such a product still builds fine.
- **Reaching a bought service (1.98.1)**: the model was right in 1.98.0 but unreachable by the natural route — a partner has **New purchase**, the purchase has the quick-add drawer (`qaDrawer`), and the drawer had no service tick. Added `qa-service`, posted as `is_service`. The drawer's `qa-vat` was also still unconditional, missed when 1.97.0 swept the item form. **Lesson: a flag is not a feature until the screen someone actually starts from can set it** — services can be created from the item form OR this drawer, and both need every flag.
- **Bought services (1.98.0)**: a purchase line for a service called `record_movement( 'receive', +qty )`, which the 1.92.0 guard refuses — and `Purchases::reconcile()` only checks `if ( ! is_wp_error( $res ) )`, so the line never marked `applied_in` and was retried on every reconcile, **silently**. Fixed at the decision rather than the write: `$want_in` is 0 for a service, exactly as it already is for consignment goods, so no movement is attempted. The line still carries its cost, VAT and contribution to the supplier balance — those come from the LINES, never the ledger.
- **is_service no longer forces for_sale (1.98.0)**: it meant 'never stocked', and forcing the role was right only while services were sell-only. A courier is a service we BUY. `sanitize_item` and the item form both stopped forcing it; existing services keep their stored `for_sale`. Note the location service menu picker already filters `for_sale=1`, so a bought-only service cannot wander onto a till menu.
- **Supplier VAT registration was ALREADY handled (1.98.0 note)**: `partners.vat_registered` + `purchases.js addLine()` setting `vat_rate = vendor_vat_registered ? item_vat_rate : 0`, with the flag SNAPSHOTTED on the document so editing the partner cannot restate a settled purchase. A new partner defaults to NOT registered, which is the safe direction — it never invents VAT that was not charged. Nothing was built for this; check before assuming a gap.
- **Not-VAT-registered installs (1.97.0)**: setting `vat_registered` (default 1, so existing installs are untouched) — remember the three places that must agree: the default in `settings()`, the forward-list in `save_settings()`, and the GET response in trait-shop (the `cost_basis` bug of 1.78.0). **One lever: `VGC_SM_Sync::vat_rate( $raw )` returns 0 when unregistered**, and every calculation goes through it rather than reading `vat_rate` directly, so the company cannot end up with one screen still adding 20% while the rest stopped. Converted: `shape_item`, `Sales::checkout` (also STORES the charged rate on the line so a reprint still adds up), `Notes`, `Orders`, `Purchases::sell_gross`, `Locations::shape_stock_row`, and the location retail SQL via a `{$vat_expr}` that collapses to `0`.
- **VAT as a COST, not a charge (1.97.0)**: `Purchases::strip_vat()` existed to remove *reclaimable* input VAT — an unregistered company reclaims nothing, so it now returns the amount untouched. Without this every purchase cost was understated and every margin flattered by the VAT rate, which would have made the 1.94.0 profit figures wrong for exactly the company that needs them.
- **The UI side (1.97.0)**: `boot.vatReg` on both shells; the receipt drops BOTH the VAT and the Net rows (with no VAT, net equals total and the line is noise); the item form drops the VAT box and both gross boxes; the Sales VAT tile goes. **Stored `vat_rate` values are never cleared** — registering later restores every price, and a form that omits the field simply does not post it, which `sanitize_item` already skips. **Trap:** `catalogue.js` read `vatEl.value` and `pg.value` unguarded, so removing those fields would have thrown and broken the whole item form — all four call sites now guard.
- **Material waste in production (1.96.0)**: DISTINCT from the existing `scrap_qty`, which writes off finished units that came out defective — this is material that left the shelf without reaching the piece. `run()` takes `opts['waste']` ([id => qty] or [{component_item_id, qty}], normalised by `clean_waste()`, services refused) and writes a **separate `scrap` movement** per material alongside the `consume`, both `ref_type: production_run` — so `void_run()` reverses them with no change, since it reverses every movement on the run by sign.
- **`waste_in_recipe` — the flag that makes it correct (1.96.0)**: a one-off commission stores the recipe as what the job REALLY took (used + spoiled) so the waste reaches the piece's cost, and `run()` must then consume `need - waste` and scrap `waste`, or the material leaves the shelf twice. A standing recipe cannot carry one run's accident, so there the waste is EXTRA on top: consume `need`, scrap `waste`. custom_build passes `waste_in_recipe: true`; the produce screen does not. **The bug this caused, twice-caught:** the shortage check needs `need + waste`, and folding that into `need` broke consumption — `short` is now computed from `need + waste` while `need` itself stays the recipe figure. A material spoiled that the recipe does not use is appended with `need = 0` so it is checked and scrapped but never consumed.
- **Waste UI + the CSS trap (1.96.0)**: a `.vgc-sm-waste` box sits beside each material on Produce (`data-waste`, keyed by component_item_id) and on Custom order (`data-mwaste`, keyed by row index). Its input was appended to app.css AFTER the `@media (pointer: coarse)` block at 13px, which silently re-broke the iOS zoom fix — the touch block was moved back to the end, and **iosinputs.js now renders custom.tpl.js too**, so the sweep actually covers the new field. `run()` returns `waste` and `waste_cost` so a repeat run can report what the spoilage was worth even though its recipe cost is unchanged.
- **Custom orders (1.95.0)**: a commission is an ordinary manufactured product that happened to exist ONCE - its own recipe, one run, one sale, then archived. **No new stock, costing or profit machinery**: `run()` already consumes a component list, `unit_cost_of()` already costs a recipe, 1.94.0 already snapshots that onto the sale line. `VGC_SM_Production::custom_build()` composes create_item -> save_bom -> run behind `POST /custom-builds` (operator). **One product per commission, never a shared 'Custom' item**: a shared one pools builds costing 40, 90 and 15 into a single stock figure with no honest answer for which cost applies when one sells - which would make 1.94.0's profit fiction on the highest-value work.
- **custom_build internals (1.95.0)**: everything is validated BEFORE anything is written, because `save_bom()` silently `continue`s past a component it cannot use - a bad id would otherwise produce a piece made of fewer materials than recorded, costed wrong, with no error. Refuses: no name, qty <= 0, no components, a missing material, a zero quantity (named), and a **service as a material** (it has no stock to consume). No cycle check is needed - the output does not exist yet. **Rollback is compensating, not transactional**: `run()` opens its own transaction and MySQL has no real nesting, so wrapping it would silently commit the first; instead a failed BOM or run deletes the item, which is safe because a failed run leaves no movements and `delete_item()` clears the recipe too. Components are stored `basis: 'run'` against `yield_qty = qty`, so the maker enters JOB TOTALS and cost per piece falls out of the existing BOM maths.
- **The custom screen (1.95.0)**: `app/js/custom.js` + `app/js/tpl/custom.tpl.js`, route `#/custom`, nav beside Produce. **The page is painted ONCE and only `#cu-rows` is repainted afterwards** - the first version called a full `render()` on every add/remove, which wiped the name, quantity and price already typed (caught by the harness). Watch for a second trap found the same way: there were two `paint()` functions in one scope and hoisting made the confirmation one win, so the first render called it with no result - the confirmation painter is now `paintDone()`. Live figures: job cost, cost each (job / yield), margin vs the quoted price in red when negative, per-material 'x per piece', and shortage warnings BEFORE saving. The picker offers `for_sale=0` items minus services.
- **Profit + margin on Sales (1.94.0)**: `sale_lines.unit_cost` **snapshots what the goods cost at checkout** - deriving profit later from the item's current cost would silently restate every past sale each time a material price moved. NULL (not 0) where absent, so a pre-1.94.0 line is distinguishable from a genuinely free one and can fall back to the item's cost today. Column added by `VGC_SM_Sales::ensure_cost_column()` behind its own `vgc_sm_sale_cost_v1` guard, and in the dbDelta CREATE TABLE for fresh installs.
- **The profit maths (1.94.0)**: `unit_cost_of()` mirrors `shape_item()`'s precedence (manual typed > recipe BOM > typed > avg_cost > last_cost > 0) - **the two must be kept in step**; it is duplicated rather than extracted because shape_item is central and heavily relied on. Profit = net revenue - COGS, where revenue is **ex-VAT** (VAT was never ours) and **net of refunds** (returned goods were neither sold nor cost anything). `margin_pct()` returns **null** on zero revenue rather than dividing by zero. **`cogs_by_sale()` keys off the same WHERE the totals use, NOT the listed receipts** - that list is capped at 500 while the totals are not, so costing only what is listed would understate a busy month and overstate its profit.
