| **1.170.0** | **Fixing a run made with a wrong recipe.** New model `VGC_SM_Run_Fix` (`includes/class-run-fix.php`); routes `GET|POST /production/runs/{id}/fix` and `POST /production/runs/{id}/fix/preview` (manager). `plan($run, $lines=null)` compares the run's STANDING consume legs (per material; spoilage legs shown, never changed; output legs untouched) with `BOM::direct_requirements` now: status `removed` / `changed` / `added` / `same`, `substitute` (consumed item is a registered alt of an unconsumed recipe line, or its note names that line) kept as recorded, `replaced` (the line it stood in for) 0; a custom (`is_custom`) run proposes need − spoilage, the way it consumed. `apply()`: for each changed material the standing legs get a `correct` entry with `ref_type = 'production_fix'`, `reverses` = the leg, and the originals `reversed_at`; the right amount is written as a new `consume` leg with `ref_type = 'production_run'` (so void/recost see it); **all dated at the run's completed_at**. Cost keeps the day's prices: `materials_after = max(0, materials_before + Σ delta × unit_cost_net now)`, extras unchanged, unit = total / good_qty; uncosted runs move stock only. **Sales since** (owner's choice): the product's average is rebuilt per receipt period after the run (`Costing::all_receipts` sliced + `average_for_on_hand` on the stock at that moment, a run's own legs counted by ref, not clock), old vs new; a till line (not voided, `held_qty` 0, product not `cost_manual`) whose snapshot equals the period's old average within `MATCH` 0.0101 moves by the difference; an order line (status not transferred/requested) is de-grossed at its VAT rate when `prices_include_vat`, matched from its order date onward, and written back grossed. Anything else is left and named (`typed` / `held` / `other`). Warnings: a count (`ref_type='count'`) on a changed material since the run; shortages refuse (409) unless `allow_negative`. `offer_for($item)` (the latest completed run, if it differs) rides on `PUT /recipe/{id}` as `fix_offer` for managers → `#/item/{id}?fixrun=` shows `item-fixoffer`. `cost_history` rows carry `fixed`. **Also fixed:** `recost_run()` now filters `reversed_at IS NULL` (a cancelled leg was counted). Corrections guards `production_fix`. Client: `#/run/{id}/fix` = `viewRunFix` in production.js + `runFixPage`/`runFixEffect`/`fixRow` in production.tpl.js; boxes never redrawn while typing, preview per change (debounced, stale answers dropped), Fix tapped twice. Harnesses `runfix` (87, SQLite, real Repository/Costing/Production), `runfixui` (63), `runfixlook` (21, real Chrome); 21/21 mutants; staging probe on MySQL with ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY, self-cleaning. Numbering: error-report stage 2 is now 1.171.0, Statistics follow-ups 1.172.0 / 1.173.0. |
| **1.169.1** | **Till on a phone after a sale.** The phone grid is `1fr auto` (wall, dock); the finished sale (`#pos-cartcol.is-open`) was given `grid-row: 1/3` while the wall and dock were AUTO-placed, so the wall went into a third, implicit row that took the whole height, the sale's two rows collapsed to 1px and "Sale complete" / New sale / Print receipt drew UNDER the products. The dock is emptied while `state.receipt` is set, and `addToCart` kept rendering the receipt, so a product tapped then went into a cart nobody could see — the till looked frozen until reopened. **Rule now:** on a phone every grid child is PLACED — `.vgc-sm-pos__catalog` row 1, `.vgc-sm-pos__dock` (new class on `#pos-dock`) row 2, the open sale rows 1/3 with `z-index: 1`; the wall stays laid out beneath, so its scroll survives. **`clearReceipt()`** (pos.js) is the one door out of a finished sale: New sale, and `addToCart` — a product tapped (or scanned) while the receipt is up starts the next sale (desktop too, where the wall sits beside the receipt). Phone: the `Current sale` heading is hidden over "Sale complete"; `@media (max-height: 500px)` drops the tick and puts the two buttons side by side so New sale is on screen in landscape. **Print:** `@media print` hid `.vgc-sm-shell` but not the till's `.vgc-sm-pos`, so a till receipt printed a page of till screen, then the slip — now hidden too. Harness `tillphone` (75) is the suite's first REAL-Chrome harness (puppeteer-core, the real core/pos/app.css, stubbed fetch): iPhone, SE, landscape and desktop, asserting with `elementFromPoint` what a thumb would hit, the grid's track count, and a one-page PDF; `VGC_SM_APP` runs it on an unpacked release zip. jsdom has no layout and could never have caught this. 9/10 mutants (the survivor, spanning one row, is equivalent: the dock row is 0px while the receipt is up). |
| **1.169.0** | **Decimals on a comma-region phone.** An iPhone's decimal pad (`inputmode="decimal"`) carries the REGION's separator — a comma in Bulgaria — and no point, and Safari's `type="number"` discards a typed comma, so no decimal could be entered; in the fields that were already text (`cellIn`, the order/purchase line cells) `parseFloat("2,5")` silently saved **2**. **Rule now:** every non-negative decimal field is `type="text" inputmode="decimal"` (81 converted by codemod; `step`/`min` kept as documentation), and `core.js decimalInput` — one `input` listener in the CAPTURE phase at `document`, installed at load so the till has it — turns every `,` into `.` on INPUTs with `inputmode="decimal"`, caret preserved, before any field's own handler runs. **Kept `type="number"`:** whole numbers (step=1 / day counts) and the five fields that can be negative, because the decimal pad has no minus key — order and purchase payment amounts (a refund is a negative payment: the server only refuses `abs(amount) < 0.0001`), purchase-line `markup_pct`, `p-defmarkup`, and purchase-line `amount`. **New helper `V.numVal(n)`:** a field's VALUE as plain digits and a point — never `fmt()`, which is locale-formatted for reading ("1 500,5" / "1,500") and made the movement-edit quantity and the count-sheet "How many" box open EMPTY for ≥ 1000 or any decimal on a BG phone. CSS: the `.vgc-sm-oline` / `.vgc-sm-pline` input rule now also matches `[inputmode="decimal"]`. New code: a decimal field is `type="text" inputmode="decimal"`, prefilled with `numVal`; only a signed field stays `type="number"`. Harness `decimalinput` (38: a real form under a Bulgarian number format, 2,5 typed, 2.5 saved); 14/14 mutants. Numbering: error-report stage 2 is now 1.170.0, Statistics follow-ups 1.171.0 / 1.172.0. |
| **1.168.0** | **The item page's movements panel: tabs by type, pages of 15, a note search.** `GET /items/{id}/movements` (`trait-items.php item_movements()`) now pages on the server: `page` (1-based), `per_page` (15 default, capped at 100), `type` (sanitize_key'd; empty = all), `search` (a `note LIKE` built with `$wpdb->esc_like()`, so a typed `%` or `_` is literal — proved on staging's MySQL, whose LIKE escapes with a backslash by default; SQLite has no default escape, so `movespage.php` adds `ESCAPE '\'` to mimic MySQL), `cancelled=1` to include cancelled entries and their reversals (otherwise both are excluded, as the panel always folded them). Returns `{movements, page, pages, per_page, total, all, type, search, types:[{type,count}], cancelled}` — per-type counts under the same search, the open type kept at 0 when the search empties it, and how many cancelled entries the view hides. A page past the end is clamped to the last. **Legacy:** `limit` without `page` returns the newest `limit` rows with cancelled included, exactly as before, for a phone still holding the old app. Client: `tpl/catalogue.tpl.js` gains `movementsPanel(d)` (search box + `#mv-body`), `movementsBody(d)` (tabs, rows, fold, pager), `moveRow`, `movesPager`, `pageList` (all ≤ 7, else 1 … n-1 n n+1 … last), `movesFromList` (the same panel from a plain array, for older callers) and `MOVE_TYPES`/`moveLabel` (fixed tab order; rows now show the name, not the code — `consume` is **Used**, not "used in production", because a hand use (repair, sample) shares the type and must read the same). `catalogue.js` keeps `mvView` {item, type, page, search} at module scope so the full redraw after an edit or undo lands on the same place (reset when another item opens); `bindMoves()` redraws only `#mv-body`, so the search box keeps the cursor, debounces typing (300 ms), drops a late answer (request counter), and re-binds `bindUndo(item, root)` / `bindEditMovement(item, list, root)` inside the body. **CSS gotcha:** `.vgc-sm-tab` is also the phone bottom-nav class (flex column, 52px) — `.vgc-sm-tabs--in .vgc-sm-tab` undoes that. Phone: page numbers hide, "2 / 7" shows. Harnesses `movespage` (40, the real handler on SQLite) and `movespanel` (61, the real app driven); 29/29 mutants caught. Error-report stage 2 moves to 1.169.0, Statistics follow-ups to 1.170.0 / 1.171.0. |
| **1.167.0** | **Error reports, stage 1: the exact place and the exact error, one tap to copy.** An error kit in `core.js`, installed while core LOADS (not in `V.start()`) so the till — which loads core without booting the app — has it too. Its state `var ERR` is declared at the very top of core, before anything that could run (the 1.165.0 hoisting lesson). `reportError(e, ctx)` → `{code, text}`: parses the stack keeping only `/app/js/` frames (V8 and Firefox/Safari spellings), codes the bug `E-XXXX` by FNV-1a over **type + first frame** (never the message, which each browser words differently, so the same bug has the same code everywhere; an error built in rawFetch is keyed on request + status + server code instead), and writes a fixed-English report: version, company, role, shell, browser; route and last-clicked `data-devid`; the frames; the PHP location for a server fault; up to 20 breadcrumbs (route changes, clicks with their `data-*` attributes and panel, every `rawFetch`/`upload` with status and ms, warn toasts), newest kept first within a 4 KB budget; and an assets line comparing `boot.version` with the server's `X-VGC-SM-Version` header to flag a stale page. Query-string VALUES are stripped everywhere (`cleanPath`), paths are app-relative, the nonce never enters. **A bug** = TypeError / ReferenceError / RangeError / SyntaxError / URIError / EvalError, or a 5xx; a plain `Error` is NOT (the app rejects with `new Error('empty')` on purpose), a 4xx is a refusal, and a request that got no answer is marked `e.network` in rawFetch (`noAnswer`). `errorView(e)` now shows the error card (`data-devid="error-card"`: code, Copy report, Reload, Back, Details) for a bug, today's card for a refusal, and *Could not reach the server* for the network — all ~40 screens changed with no edits. `failToast(e)` replaced `toast(e.message, 'warn')` at **119** catch sites (codemod): a refusal keeps the old toast, a bug gets a red 8-second `'bug'` toast with its code that copies the report on tap. `.catch(quiet)` replaced the **9** silent catches: still silent for refusals and the network, a bug gets the red toast. Global `error` / `unhandledrejection` listeners report errors with one of our frames and paint the card if the screen is blank or still loading (`#vgc-sm-app`, or `#vgc-sm-pos` on the till). The report's buttons and the toast are answered by one delegated click listener. `copyReport()` tries the clipboard API, then `execCommand`, then opens the details with the text selected. **Server:** new `includes/class-errors.php` hooks `rest_dispatch_request` (after permissions, before the callback) and runs every route of our namespace in `try/catch(Throwable)`, returning a flat 500 `{code: vgc_sm_internal, ref, type, detail, where, trace[5], request, version}` with paths relative to the plugin (`wp:` for WordPress, basename otherwise) — and wraps a `null` return in `WP_REST_Response(null)`, because returning null from that filter makes WordPress call the handler a SECOND time. `rest_post_dispatch` stamps `X-VGC-SM-Version` on our responses. PHP's `code()` is the same FNV-1a as the browser's, cross-checked by running core's `errorCode` in node. Harnesses `errorsui` (76, scripts run under their real URLs via jsdom's VM context so stacks carry file names) and `errorsdata` (36); **the acceptance test** puts the 1.165.0 `INV_COLS` bug back and requires the copied report to name its three lines, the screen, the panel and the click. 34/34 mutants caught. Stage 2 (1.168.0): the server-side log table, the Settings → Errors tab, offline queueing, PHP fatals. |
| **1.166.1** | **Fix: a sale location with stock on its shelf would not open** — the error card instead of the screen. 1.165.0 put `var INV_COLS` near the BOTTOM of `viewLocation` (~line 940) while `renderInv()` is called near the top of it (~line 154). `var` hoists the declaration and not the value, so `sortRows( list, state, INV_COLS )` got `undefined` and died on `cols[state.col]` — the reported message, *Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'name')*, is literally `undefined['name']`, `'name'` being the shelf's default sort column. An EMPTY shelf never reached the sort (`renderInv` returns on `!list.length`), so the screen broke the moment the first thing was pushed to a location and stayed broken. `INV_COLS` now lives at module scope above `viewLocation`, where nothing can run before it. Its getters close over nothing, so there was never a reason for it to be inside. Harness `locationscreen` DRIVES the real screen — boots the app, answers its fetches and clicks *Send from stock* — because `listsort` rendered the same template happily: it supplied a COLS map of its own, which is exactly what was missing at runtime. It also carries a general guard: no `*_COLS` inside a function may be read by anything called before the line that assigns it. |
| **1.166.0** | **Statistics: one section for how it is going.** New model `includes/class-stats.php` (`VGC_SM_Stats`) and two routes, `/reports/overview` and `/reports/sales` (`$write`; the payload is cost-shaped by `sees_cost()` = `at_least('manager')`, so the gate is about quantities, not money). **Every figure is a GROUP BY over the whole range**, never a fold over a capped list — the shape `Sales::recent()` established: one WHERE reused by an uncapped aggregate, an uncapped per-day series, a per-product roll-up and a COGS grouped by `(item_id, unit_cost)` with a single batched lookup for the lines whose snapshot is NULL (`Sales::unit_cost_of()`). Profit is on NET revenue; margin uses the line's cost snapshot, so a re-costed product does not move last year's margin. `range()` resolves a preset (`this_month|last_month|last_90|this_year|last_year`) or a custom from/to into `{from,to,days,bucket,prev_from,prev_to}` — a month is compared with the MONTH before, and **`date()`, never `gmdate()`**, because `strtotime()` reads a bare date locally and reading it back as UTC loses a day east of Greenwich (it did: February's comparison ended on the 27th). `lines` is reserved in MySQL 8, so the stock-value count comes back as `line_count`; `sales_by_item()` groups by `sl.item_id` alone with `MAX()` on the text columns, which is what makes it safe under `ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY` (staging has the mode off, so the probe turns it on for a session and re-runs every query). **Client:** `salesChart()` and `buildSalesBuckets()` lifted out of the sales screen into core as `V.barChart(c, opts)` and `V.buildBuckets(series, range, opts)` (`opts.value` names the plotted field, `opts.sum` the ones carried along; a bucket is empty when NO DAY reached it, not when it earned nothing — a day that sold and refunded it all is a different fact); `V.statsTabs()` / `V.statsTabOk()` (one tab bar, reading `canManage()` rather than a caller's argument), `V.periodControl()` / `V.bindPeriod()` (the period lives in the hash, so it is bookmarkable and survives a tab change), `V.deltaOf()` / `V.deltaWord()` (null, never 0, when there is nothing to compare with). New `app/js/stats.js` + `app/js/tpl/stats.tpl.js`; `#/stats/<tab>` with seven tabs, and `#/reports`, `#/reports/<tab>` and `#/costs` now redirect in. Nav loses *Reports* and *Material prices* and gains *Statistics*. Harnesses `statsdata` (100 checks, real SQL on SQLite with DAYOFWEEK/HOUR registered) and `statsui` (90 checks, the real core and templates); 26/26 on the mutation sweep. |
| **1.165.0** | **Every list sorts by its columns.** core.js's kit (`sortState` / `sortRows` / `sortTh` / `bindSort`, remembered per table in `localStorage`) applied to the lists that lacked it: orders (`ORDER_COLS`, default `date desc`), purchases (`PURCHASE_COLS`, `date desc`), sales (`SALE_COLS`, `when desc`), a location's shelf (`INV_COLS`), open gaps (`OPEN_COLS`, `when desc`, repainted by `paintOpen()` without re-fetching), material prices (`COST_COLS`), the team (`TEAM_COLS`) and a partner's orders / purchases / outstanding / held (`PO_COLS`, `PP_COLS`, `goodsCols()`; the last two share one template so each carries its own state and is bound inside its own card). A date getter falls back (`date_sent || date_ordered || created_at`) so nothing sorts blank-last, and a figure the row prints as a dash sorts as `null`. Orders' and purchases' default fetch raised 200/100 → **500**: a list you can sort must be the whole list. The audit log and the shop-pull list are deliberately left alone — they are paginated server-side, and sorting one page of many would lie. Harness `listsort`. |
| **1.164.0** | **Two companies in two tabs, and screens that keep their place.** A scope holds ONE service worker, so a script URL per company (`sw_url()`'s `&c=`) made the two tabs replace each other's worker, delete each other's cache, claim each other's clients and reload for ever. One script for every company, cache `vgc-sm-<ver>`, the precache reduced to the shared assets, and every navigation kept under its own URL (`named` = the request carries `vgc_sm_co`, so no cross-company fallback). `switchCompany()` no longer purges (logout still does), and `V.start()` pins `vgc_sm_co` into the tab's address with `replaceState` when there is more than one company — the per-company URL, without rewrite rules. `screen( html, opts )` now scrolls to the top only on a route change: it latches `V.keepY` before the redraw and holds it across `loading()` (`opts.placeholder`, `V.holdY`), because the placeholder collapses the page and the browser clamps the scroll to 0; `opts.top` is for a render that swaps in something new to read (the custom build's result, an issued note). Harness `tabscroll`. |
| **1.163.2** | **Fix: the till showed VAT in a company that is not registered.** `Locations::shape_stock_row()` reported the product's stored `vat_rate`, and `pos.js totals()` split it out of every final price, so the cart panel showed a VAT the company never charges. The row now reports `VGC_SM_Sync::vat_rate()` (as `price_gross` beside it always did), `pos.js` reads it through `V.vatRate()` (so an offline-cached price list is right too), `pos.tpl.js` drops the Net and VAT rows when unregistered — the receipt's rule — and `locations.tpl.js` drops the sale detail's VAT % column. Recorded sales were always stored with the rate as charged; only the screen was wrong. |
| **1.163.1** | **Fix: the item form crashed in a company that is not VAT registered.** `bindPricing()` bound `input` listeners to `f-cost_gross` and `f-vat_rate` without checking they exist; the template leaves both out when `vatReg` is false, so the null threw and took the rest of the form's wiring with it. Both guarded. New harness `itemformui` lifts the shipped `bindPricing()` and runs it against the real rendered form in both companies — the first test that executes that wiring at all. |
| **1.163.0** | **The VAT boundary between the companies.** A company that cannot reclaim pays final prices: a trade crosses at the buyer's gross (`Orders::gross_unit()` + `unit_gross` in the payload; `write_mirror()` writes it gross-typed when `! vat_registered()` in the buyer), and a request from such a buyer is priced gross-typed so the seller invoices exactly what was asked. One-time repair `remirror_all()` (flag `intercompany_gross_v1`) restates trades booked short. On the client, `core.js vatRate()` is the gate `VGC_SM_Sync::vat_rate()` always was, and the purchase screen, the order screen and the printed order sheet read every rate through it. |
| **1.162.0** | **A trade started by either company.** A purchase whose vendor is a company row becomes an order **request** there (`Intercompany::on_purchase_saved()` on the new `vgc_sm_purchase_saved` hook): the buyer's SKUs, quantities and prices, pointing at the purchase from the start. Status `requested` counts for nothing (no stock, no turnover, nothing owed, no sending, completing, paying or reporting) and the buyer leads until `accept_request()` turns it into an ordinary order and makes that very purchase its mirror; `decline_request()` (or deleting the request) marks the purchase *Declined* for the buyer to change or delete, and the buyer deleting the purchase takes the request with it. Refusals: buying from yourself, a consignment from a company, goods received on a request; a missing SKU is named and the state is `failed` until it is shared. Columns `request_company_id/order_id/state/error`, routes `/orders/{id}/accept` and `/decline`, banners `order-request` and `purchase-request`. DB 0.60.0, flag `intercompany_request_v1`. |
| **1.161.0** | **Consignment sales on the purchase; the main counter.** `Notes::plan_held_sale()` splits each maker's share across their open consignment purchase lines for the item (`Purchases::consigned_lines()`: received at the selling place first, then the oldest; price = the line's `net_cost`), the rest stays a `sold_held` note. `Sales::book_held()` books purchase parts with `Purchases::book_sold()` (line `qty_sold` +, `applied_held` −, a consignment row type `till_sale` at the held place) and records `sale_held_notes.purchase_line_id` (note_id 0); `unbook_held()` reverses with `unbook_sold()`; the shop `reconcile_apply()` does the same. `Purchases::save()` refuses (409) a Sold below `till_booked()`, dropping a booked line, switching to buy; `delete()` refuses while booked. `purchases.location_id` = Received at (normalised by `Locations::held_place()`; reconcile writes held there; a change walks what is still held via `move_held`). `Locations::ensure_primary()` on create/update and a per-company flag: one active till (not online, not event) with no active primary becomes primary. Screen: Received at, "N at the till" with `min`, the Locations warning, the till notice and its default to the primary. DB 0.59.0, flags `consignment_link_v1`, `primary_till_v1`. |
| **1.160.0** | **A product in more than one company.** `VGC_SM_Item_Share` (`includes/class-item-share.php`) + the shared `item_shares (share_id, company_id, item_id)` table (DB 0.58.0). Products stay per company; a shared product is a TWIN under the same SKU in each company, remembered by `share_id`. `set_companies( $item, $ids )` (REST `companies` on POST/PUT `/items`, administrators only, after the save; a refusal comes back as `share_error`, the item stays saved) reads the source first, checks every destination (a SKU already in another group → `vgc_sm_share_taken` 409), then in one transaction CREATEs the twin (definition + category by name and scope + `kind='raw'` + supplier = the source company's row for a made product, else the source's supplier when visible there) or ADOPTs an existing row of that SKU (definition only, its kind/supplier/prices kept), and drops unticked links (a group of one dissolves). `after_update()` is called from `Repository::update_item()` (so imports and the shop pull keep the group in step): the DEFINITION set + sku + category flows to every twin, diffed first, under a re-entrancy flag; a clash is audited `item.share_failed` in the source. `after_delete()` from `delete_item()`. `companies_of/twins/companies_map`; GET `/items/{id}` carries `companies` + `companies_detail` (with `link_to`), the list carries `shared`. Screen: `itemform-companies` (Also in), `item-companies`, a Shared tag. |
| **1.159.0** | **The whole palette follows the accent.** `accent_vars()`/`accentVars()` now also derive every neutral token (`THEME_LIGHT`/`THEME_DARK` in `VGC_SM_Frontend`: bg, canvas, subtle, sunken, border, border-strong, divider, ink 1–4, material-tint, panel*, shadow/shadow-lg; the dark set adds card) by moving each reference token's hue to the accent's, keeping its lightness, and fading its saturation by `min(1, S_accent/0.5)` — a grey accent gives grey neutrals. HSL both ways with `floor(x+.5)` rounding, identical in PHP and JS (the node cross-check compares the whole structure). The stylesheet's own colour (`ACCENT_DEFAULT`, also a constant in core.js) paints nothing: the hand-drawn palette stands. Five stray hand-written neutrals in app.css became tokens. |
| **1.158.0** | **Moving an order to another company.** `VGC_SM_Order_Transfer` (`includes/class-order-transfer.php`): `move( $order_id, $to )` copies an UNTOUCHED order (nothing sent/paid/reported, open, not `already_sent`) into the destination through the real `Orders::save()` — its own number, items matched by SKU (a missing SKU refuses, naming it), the customer linked there if hidden, the destination's own cost snapshot — then marks the source row `status='transferred'` with `transferred_company_id/order_id`; the moved row carries `origin_company_id/order_id` (four columns, DB 0.57.0, `ensure_order_transfer_columns` + flag `order_transfer_v1`). Prices: across the VAT boundary the line arrives gross-typed (`round(P×(1+r), 4)` when the registered source was net-typed), rate from the destination item when it is registered; same status copies verbatim — the customer's total is identical. The stub is frozen by `Orders::moved_error()` at every writer (8 sites; `save()`/`delete()` gained `$opts['transfer']`), excluded from `count_for_partner`, `turnover_for_partner` and the statement, and shown as *Moved* with no money. Moving back to the origin revives the stub (same number, current lines); deleting a moved order removes its stub through `vgc_sm_order_deleting` (priority 20), a chain cascades. One `START TRANSACTION` around both companies, `ROLLBACK` + `order.transfer_failed` audit on any error or Throwable; `order.transfer` audited in both. REST `POST /orders/{id}/transfer {company_id}` (`$mgr`; the model checks the manager level in both), `shape_order` gains `moved*`, `origin*` (+ `origin_number` via `peek_number`, no `run_as` per row), `can_transfer`. Screen: `order-transfer` banner, frozen stub (a disabled fieldset, only Print), Move control, *Moved* tags in the lists, `statusBadge('transferred')`. |
| **1.157.0** | **An accent colour per company.** `settings.accent` ('#rrggbb' or '', validated in `settings_patch()` and again by `VGC_SM_Sync::accent()`, not copied to a new company). `VGC_SM_Frontend::accent_vars()` derives the light and dark token sets (`--vgc-brand`, `-dark`, `-tint`, `-glow`) by mixing; `accent_style()` injects them in `head()` after the stylesheet so the first paint — app, till, sign-in — is the company's colour; `core.js` `accentVars()`/`applyAccent()` hold the same derivation for the Look card's live preview, cross-checked in `pwa_php_test`. The stylesheet's three hard-coded brand colours became tokens. Boot carries `accent`; `/settings` GET carries `accent` + `accent_default`; `Company::shape()` carries `accent` and the Companies list shows the dot. `settings-look` (swatches, native colour input, Save, Back to the default; `V.onScreenLeave` restores the saved colour when a preview is abandoned). |
| **1.156.1** | **Hotfix: the Settings screen crashed** ("boot is not defined") with the pull on and gateways listed — `feeRules()` (1.151.0) read `boot.currency`, which no template has; it now reads `o.s.currency`. New harness `settingsui.js` boots the REAL core.js + every screen file under jsdom and opens Settings (pull on, gateways, a fee rule), Companies and Team; it also pins the rule that no template file reads `boot`. |
| **1.156.0 · Bridge 0.7.0** | **Refunds from the shop.** The bridge sends `qty_refunded` and `refunded` (tax included) per line and, on an incremental read, also the orders whose REFUNDS changed since the cursor (`refund_parents()`: a refund is its own object and making one does not reliably mark its order modified). The manager's `lines_for()` carries both; `refund_delta()` = per sale line, shop `qty_refunded` minus our `refunded_qty` (matched like `differs()`, never negative; postage/fee refunded whole = its one unit); `sync_refunds()` refunds the delta through `Sales::refund()` (money off, goods walk home for an online sale) and notes money BEYOND the goods on the sale — `sales.ext_refunded` holds the extra noted so far, pending units are projected before the extra is measured, drift ≤ 0.05 is ignored. `apply()`: agreeing lines + a delta → action `refunded` (preview says which units); `import()` applies the shop's refunds at once. Pull/resync count `refunded`. DB 0.56.0. |
| **1.155.0** | **Trade between the companies.** An order to a company row is mirrored into it as an owned, read-only purchase (`VGC_SM_Intercompany`): sent → received, price → cost, the seller's VAT status → the vendor's, items matched by SKU and created with the seller as supplier, payments and completion mirrored, deletion propagated, a partner change re-mirrored, a failure audited and retried on the next save. `IC<seller>-<number>`; the two back-link columns; five refusals on the buyer's side; no on_sale, no notes, no self-sale. DB 0.55.0. |
| **1.154.1** | **Companies.** (1.154.0 was built and staged but never published: the new company's first load exposed `migrate_category_scopes()` flattening the just-seeded expense categories to `product` — a defect every fresh install has had since 1.135.0 — fixed and released as 1.154.1.) Registry + create/rename/archive (Settings → Companies), page and REST context (`vgc_sm_co`, `X-VGC-Company`, 403), the sidebar switcher (purge, then reload), a level per company (Team per company), one shared partner list with `partner_companies` (Visible to) and the rows that ARE the companies, one shop per company, tills and the service worker per company, the offline queue per company. DB 0.54.0. |

## 9. Consignment: what is agreed but not yet built

All five phases have shipped: outbound (1.1.0), inbound/held bucket (1.2.0), multi-location/contact partners (1.3.0), shop publishing of held stock (1.4.0), and partner statements + printable notes (1.5.0). The consignment feature set is complete.

Standing defaults the user accepted: **held stock is consumed oldest-received-first** across makers when reconcile books shop sales (FIFO); **the shop never offers more held units than we hold** (`clamp_shop_held`); own-stock online sales remain out of scope (push-only design — the shop is authoritative for its own sales, we don't pull orders back). **You can only release what you own** (a maker's held goods cannot be released onward on an outbound note).

---

## 10. Known gaps / candidate next steps

- Historical costs are **not snapshotted** at run time — reports estimate using each item's *current* unit cost.
- Pulling many products with photos does one download per product; a huge catalogue can hit a PHP timeout (pull in batches, or move image copying to a background job).
- No margin view (computed cost vs shop selling price) — the Bridge already returns `price`, so this is cheap to add.
- No order pull-back: online sales reduce shop stock but not local stock. Deliberate (the user chose push-only), but a `/orders` style sync is the natural extension if local and shop stock must fully agree.
- Categories are a **flat list** (no nesting).
- Archiving a sellable item does **not** touch the shop (the WooCommerce product stays live).
- The wiki (`class-help.php`) is **English only** — its body HTML isn't routed through the i18n catalogue.
